# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are recorded here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and each release is a dated merge to `main`.

## [2026-11-24] - The Incident RCA

**The Incident RCA, Phase 1:** the first item of the Insights app's Wave 2 ([ADR 0031](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md)). The app explains an incident the way a DBA would, from what the demo's own capture recorded and never from what it injected: who blocked whom, head blocker first, with what it ran and who ran it; the errors and deadlocks; and why T4's read replica fell behind; with recommendations and next steps. Its live proof, a new deployment and an update with a full Run all session each, comes before its release ([Phase 1](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#phase-1)).

**What's new:**

- **The capture:** during every session, the workload runner's observer captures, read-only, what a DBA's own tools would show on T1 to T4: blocking chains with their head blocker, errors, deadlocks, lock escalations, and the replay of T4's read replica. The session's history keeps it.
- **Root cause in the Insights app:** the incidents the blind detector finds; each blocking chain as a tree, head blocker first (its module or statement, program, login, idle time, open transaction, and lock); the errors by class; the replica's lag and its cause, from the capture's own samples; T4's connections against the limit, by what held them; and recommendations, each with its Microsoft Learn page and who approves it, and next steps.
- **The chat investigates:** Ask's Copilot gets six tools to triage a window, find its incidents, and explain an incident, its blocking, its errors, and the replica's lag, citing the captured rows and their times. A card whose Ask had only the answer key to hand over hands the chat a window to investigate instead.
- **Honest detection:** the detector, the Root cause view, and the chat never read what the demo injected, or a scenario's name. Only the "Did we catch it?" scoreboard and the tests compare what was found with what was injected.
- **The Insights app in earth tones** (the owner's request, 2026-11-23): cream and ivory instead of white, espresso instead of near-black, softer text in light and dark, a terracotta brand, and earth-toned statuses and chart colors, with text at 4.5:1 or more and marks at 3:1 or more (WCAG 2.2 AA) where they sit, a hovered or pressed row, button, or menu item included. High contrast and Windows forced colors are as they were.

**How to update:** run `./scripts/deploy.ps1`. It updates the runner, the runbooks, the notebooks, the history model, and the Insights app, with no new permission or resource and nothing to set; a session saved before it says "no RCA capture recorded" ([upgrading](README.md#upgrading-an-existing-demo)). The notebooks come with it when `fabric.importNotebooks` is true, which `init.ps1` writes for a workspace it creates, and, with it false, when nobody edited them in Fabric (follow-ups decision 2a, which ships with this release). An activity notebook someone edited is kept as it is, so run `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1 -SkipHubChecks -Notebook NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity -RunLiveActivity` once afterwards, which replaces that notebook alone, then `./scripts/configure-powerbi.ps1`: until then neither Run all nor the scripts' history saves carry RCA evidence, and each says so with its own fix, which that command covers (`FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.RCA`, `FABRIC.RCA.EVIDENCE`, Run all's `RCA.EVIDENCE` **Warning**), while the rest of the history is saved as before.

**Cost:** no new resource or meter. The export makes each session's Automation jobs longer, by at most 36 minutes, so the estimate counts 116 job minutes for each of a light-use month's 5 Run all sessions: with the nightly safety stop's 120, 200 minutes past the 500 Azure Automation includes, about $0.40 a month (2026-11-03 list price), where the release's follow-ups counted 20, about $0.04. The history grows by about 1 to 2 MB a session in OneLake ([cost management](docs/cost-management.md#the-incident-rca-2026-11-20)).

### Added

- **The observer lane** in the workload runner's `profile run`: a 30-second sentinel on each target while its activity runs, and 5- or 10-second detail capture during an episode it sees (a blocked process, a lock waiter or a deadlock on T4, the replica's replay stalled, T4's connections near the limit, or a jump in errors), from documented DMVs, Extended Events, and PostgreSQL's statistics views, each query with its Learn page in [the capture catalog](rca/capture/README.md). It keeps what it captures on the VM, SYSTEM and Administrators only, at most 20 MB per target, and deletes it at its first export or capture there at least 7 days after the export that took all of it (30 days after it started, when no export took it); `rca export` and `rca part` print it as checksummed gzip parts.
- **The observer's one Extended Events session,** `ContosoCare_RCA`: in memory, filtered to ContosoCare, created when the observer starts and dropped at the session's end; server-scoped on T1 and T2, database-scoped on T3 ([ADR 0010's amendment](docs/adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md#amendment-the-incident-rca-observers-extended-events-session-2026-11-20)).
- **The transport:** the session control's runbooks export the capture through managed Run Commands that only read, at a session's stop, at each 30-minute re-check, and at a start that finds an earlier session's capture still on the VMs, and write it as earlier versions of the control vault's existing `dbhub-session-metrics` secret ([the runbooks](automation/README.md#the-incident-rcas-evidence)). Run all stages it in the lakehouse and hands the activity notebook its path, `RCA_EVIDENCE_FILE`; `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory`, `deploy.ps1`'s history snapshot, and `configure-fabric.ps1 -RunLiveActivity` export and stage it with your own roles, and list what a save didn't take for the next one ([the contract](rca/README.md#transport), [what each result says](docs/troubleshooting.md#the-incident-rcas-capture)).
- **Eight history tables** (`rca_sessions`, `rca_waits`, `rca_events`, `rca_queries`, `rca_resource`, `rca_replica`, `rca_coverage`, and `rca_answer_key`), which `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` writes and the deployment notebook's purge drops, with `Files/rca/inbox` ([the history](fabric/README.md#the-incident-rcas-evidence-in-the-history-2026-11-20)); and the history model's eight RCA tables with seven measures, added once the lakehouse has them (`FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.RCA`, [the model](fabric/powerbi/README.md#the-incident-rcas-tables-2026-11-20)).
- **The Root cause view** in the Insights app, with the RCA tables ([the app](fabric/app/README.md#whats-in-it)): the shared blind detector's episodes, the blocking chain as a tree, the timeline, errors by class, recommendations with their Learn pages and approvers, next steps, what wasn't captured, and **Explain this incident**, in every theme and in Windows forced colors. The Incidents page's selected incident and a fault picked on the scoreboard link into it with a target and its minutes only, never a fault's name, and Mission Control links to it. A read that Power BI throttles or that fails is tried again; one that still fails says the history couldn't be read, with **Try again**, never that no capture is recorded or that the minutes were quiet.
- **The Root cause list, organized** (the owner's feedback, 2026-11-23): facets for severity, category, and server and database, each value with its count; the minutes it reads (the newest 90 minutes, 6 or 24 hours, or the viewer's own); the list grouped by category or by server and sorted by severity or by time; and the filters in the app's address, so a view can be shared, which the Incidents page's and the scoreboard's **Root cause** links fill with a target and its minutes. Severity is High, Medium, or Low from each incident's own measurements, how far over its level it went and how long it stayed there, with why on hover and in its report ([ADR 0031, section 7](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#7-the-rca-view)). The chat's `find_incidents` and `triage_window` take the same categories, severities, and targets.
- **Six investigation tools in Ask,** with the RCA tables: `triage_window`, `find_incidents`, `explain_incident`, `explain_locking`, `explain_errors`, and `explain_replica_lag`, with the investigation playbook in the chat's instructions and four starters.
- **The postmortem's cause from the blind investigation,** with the RCA tables: for a session, **Write the postmortem** investigates the session's minutes, as the session control recorded them, the way the Root cause view does, and writes up to six incidents, the capture's first, each with its root cause, its confidence, and the rows it cites, or why it has none. A lone incident request's minutes are its fault's own, so its postmortem points to the Root cause view instead. What was injected follows in a section labeled as the answer key. Like the investigation, it reads every target, whatever the page's filter shows: the session's faults and T4's and T5's episodes on targets the filter hides follow under a heading of their own, and any other plan's faults in the investigated minutes, on any target, such as a lone incident request just after the session, are labeled as outside it. The investigation's queries join the postmortem's. Without the RCA tables, the postmortem is as it was, apart from the filter's wording below ([ADR 0031, section 9](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#9-the-postmortems-cause)).
- **[The evidence contract](rca/README.md)** (`rca/`): the schemas, the tables' spec, the capture catalog, the blind-detection lists, and the fixtures that the runner's, the notebook's, the model's, and the app's tests share (`tests/unit/Rca.Contract.Tests.ps1`).
- **`validate.ps1`'s `T1.QUERYSTORE` to `T4.QUERYSTORE`** say whether Query Store is on wherever the demo keeps it on ([ADR 0012](docs/adr/0012-validation-model.md#2026-11-23-query-store-on-t1-to-t4)): `READ_WRITE` in ContosoCare on T1 to T3, from the runner's status (which now reports it, before its `host` node, and which the VM's relay keeps from the whole output) and T3's status row; T4's capture mode and wait sampling through Azure Resource Manager, judged by its size. A `READ_ONLY` gets its measured reason's fix, not always a redeploy, and a state that wasn't read is a **Warning**. Only `validate.ps1` runs them, so Query Store never stops `deploy.ps1`.

### Changed

- **The Insights app's colors** ([ADR 0030](docs/adr/0030-insights-app-mission-control.md#the-look-earth-tones)): every gray of Fluent 2's light and dark themes becomes a warm one, so ivory cards sit on a sand-cream page in light and espresso replaces Fluent's near-black in dark; body text is a soft espresso or a cream, 11.6:1 and 11.7:1 on a card instead of 15.5:1 and 14.5:1. Terracotta replaces Microsoft blue on buttons, links, and the current page; brick, ochre, pine, and slate mark Fail, Warning, Pass, and Manual action required, with their icons and words, and Fluent's message bars and badges take the same families; slate, moss, ochre, clay, plum, and verdigris mark the targets and the charts' categories; and Mission Control's stage takes sage for its accent and brick for its alert. The statuses and the targets are now told apart under simulated protanopia, deuteranopia, and tritanopia (at least 0.09 and 0.07 apart in OKLab, from as little as 0.022 and 0.015). Text keeps 4.5:1 and marks and controls 3:1 (WCAG 2.2 AA) on cards, the navigation, the page, and the stage, and in a hovered or pressed row, button, or menu item, as do the chart's incident band's label over the band and every badge the app shows, with tests that hold each pair (`fabric/app/src/lib/app.test.ts`); in dark, a hovered button or menu item takes a hovered row's fill, a step above a card, so the statuses and the targets' marks keep theirs there. The Copilot mark's purple-to-blue gradient and the chart stylesheet's own palette, the last colors outside the theme, now come from it too, and the Fabric account's avatar takes the brand's color instead of Fluent's colorful one. The agent panel's two outline badges, drawn in a color meant for a dark fill and so invisible on a light card, now take Fluent's informative color, and the plain links in tables (the Readiness page's **Open**) and on the Links page (**Open in Fabric**) the theme's link color instead of the browser's blue. Redeploy the app with `./scripts/deploy.ps1`; nothing to set.
- **Ask is blind, with or without the RCA tables** ([decision 7](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#decisions-and-open-items)): `run_dax` refuses the demo's answer key (Scenario Runs, Incident Windows, Analysis Findings, RCA Answer Key, and every measure that reads them) and the functions that read the whole model (`COLUMNSTATISTICS`, the `INFO` functions, `CALENDARAUTO`, and `EXTERNALMEASURE`). It compares names as leniently as the engine might, takes only plain ASCII in a query's code, and sends exactly the text it checked. Every page's Ask hands the chat measured data only; one with nothing measured to hand over is gone, or, with the RCA tables, hands the chat a window to investigate. A lone incident request's postmortem has no **Draft with Copilot**, because its minutes are its fault's own; a session's still has one, and no Ask's queries read a lone request's minutes. The pages and the scoreboard still show the answer key, labeled.
- **The history model's descriptions name no scenario,** and `npm run guide:build` refuses a model change that would let a query reach the answer key without naming it.
- **The postmortem, when the page's filter hides all of a session's faults,** with or without the RCA tables: its Summary, Timeline, Impact, Response, Follow-ups, and Cause say that none of the session's faults is on the targets the filter shows. They said that nothing was injected, and that the window had no event or response step ([ADR 0031, section 9](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#9-the-postmortems-cause)).
- **The scoreboard's T4 and T5 rows marked Not supported** show the scenario's title as their signal, never the family a keyword in its name gives: a connection surge read "Buffer IO (PAGEIOLATCH) waits" before the release's follow-ups, which grade it by T4's connections (F34).
- **The estimate counts the session control's job minutes with the export:** 116 a session, the release's follow-ups' 80 and the export's limits, 6 at the stop, 8 at each of a session's about three re-checks, and 6 at a start that fetches an earlier session's evidence. A light-use month's 5 Run all sessions are 580, so 200 past the 500 Azure Automation includes with the nightly safety stop's 120, priced at its next tier: about $0.40 a month, $0.36 more than the follow-ups' estimate, and so is every month figure it gives ([the session control](docs/cost-management.md#the-session-control)).
- **`./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` during a Run all session hands the session's stop to the session control,** as Stop now does ([ADR 0026's amendment](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#2026-11-22-amendment-stop-demops1-hands-a-sessions-stop-to-the-control)). Its export of the session's evidence ran beside a re-check's on the T2 host, which takes one Run Command at a time, and nothing saved the session's metrics in the control vault. Now the control's `DbHub-Stop` job stops the session, and the session's status says `stop-demo.ps1` stopped it. `stop-demo.ps1` waits for that job, prints what it did (`STOP.CONTROL.SESSION`) with its `STOP.RCA.*` lines, and then does the rest as before, except that `-SaveHistory` saves the history without that session's Incident RCA evidence, which the control keeps in its vault for the next Run all that starts a session within 7 days, or for Run all's own save at the session's planned end, and after 7 days nothing saves it (`STOP.FABRIC.SAVEHISTORY` says so; Stop now in Run all saves it at once). Right before the job, it waits for the T2 host to delete the host password's Run Command (`STOP.CONTROL.HOSTPASSWORD` when that outlasts 12 minutes) and removes the session's auto-stop, and a second stop of a session, its auto-stop, Stop now, or another handoff, leaves the session's export, metrics, and status to the stop that began first. When the control can't take the stop, `stop-demo.ps1` stops the demo itself, with a **Warning** that names the risk. Starting the job needs only your Contributor ([permissions](docs/permissions.md)).
- **A Run all session that a `./scripts/start-demo.ps1` session took over** still stops nothing at its end (the release's follow-ups), and exports its Incident RCA evidence then, as a stop does, so its history has it, with the stop's `RCA.*` lines after "Taken over: ..." (the merge of the two). Before, the end saved the session's metrics without the evidence's index, so its history had none of it. **Stop now** again after that end, which stops the demo, says so in the session's status, so the notebook no longer waits 25 minutes for it before a **Warning**. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the runbooks and imports the notebook.
- **What the RCA doesn't explain yet says what the history lacks, never what the target lacks** ([ADR 0031](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#8-the-chat-as-the-first-line-investigator)). Live, the chat read the note on a CPU incident, "the CPU's cause (Query Store's plans, waits per query) isn't captured yet", as "T1 doesn't have Query Store", though T1 to T3 have it on. A CPU or wait-class incident now says that this history doesn't hold Query Store's per-query statistics yet, which is the capture's limit, not the target's, and its next step names where the target's own Query Store has them for the incident's minutes, while it keeps them (14 days on T1 to T3, 7 on T4; past that, the note says so): SQL Server Management Studio's reports or the catalog views on T1 to T3, `azure_sys` on T4. A read replica's queries are in neither, as Learn says. The investigation playbook adds that a not-captured note describes the history, never the target, and the model's RCA Queries and RCA Waits, which no capture writes yet, say that their emptiness says nothing about a target.
- **T4's Query Store and its wait sampling are on at every size but Burstable,** whatever `t4AutonomousTuning` says ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-23-user-directed-query-store-and-its-wait-sampling-at-every-size-but-burstable); the owner: "We would want query store enabled wherever possible"). The template sets `pg_qs.query_capture_mode` and `pgms_wait_sampling.query_capture_mode` to `all`, so Query Store also records the statements the workload's functions run, where tuning had set `top` and nothing set the wait sampling; on Burstable both are `none`, as Learn advises. The read replica gets neither, since Query Store doesn't record a replica's queries. Both parameters are dynamic, so `./scripts/deploy.ps1` sets them on an existing T4, and setting them restarts nothing. No extra charge: the data is in T4's `azure_sys` database, kept 7 days.
- **An update keeps the session control's status** (2026-11-23, [ADR 0031's risks](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#risks)): every `./scripts/deploy.ps1` wrote the control account's variable `DbHubSessionStatus` again, empty, so the next session's start after an update no longer had what the last stop listed of the Incident RCA's evidence still on the VMs, and that evidence never reached its session's history. Now the template creates the variable only when Azure has none, and an update keeps an existing one as it is, encrypted or not (`CONTROL.RESOURCES` says which). When it can't read the variable before the deployment, it reads it again after it and creates it then if it's missing; one it still can't read it leaves as it is, with a **Warning**. In a tenant whose policy encrypts Automation variables, an update therefore no longer makes the variable readable either, so `capture-t2-golden.ps1`'s fix for an encrypted status deletes it, once the sessions have nothing left to list, before `./scripts/deploy.ps1` creates it again. And the capture captures nothing while the variable is missing from the control's account, with `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, which creates it, as the fix.
- `./scripts/deploy.ps1` brings the demo's notebooks up to date when nobody edited them, with `fabric.importNotebooks` false too (follow-ups decision 2a). Before, it kept every notebook that was already in the workspace, so an update left out the live-activity notebook's fixes, such as F34's and F36's, until `fabric.importNotebooks` was true or `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1` ran. It now reads each notebook first, as ipynb ([Get Item Definition](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/fabric/core/items/get-item-definition)), and compares its code, without outputs, metadata, or the workspace, lakehouse, and GitHub values the import sets, with every version of it in this repository's history (`fabric/notebooks/published-versions.json`). An earlier version is updated, and this version is left as it is (both `FABRIC.IMPORT.<notebook>` **Pass**). One that matches none, which someone edited, or a newer copy of the scripts published, is kept (**Not selected**, with the command that replaces it), and so is one that couldn't be read (a **Warning**, or **Manual action required** while the capacity is paused). `fabric.importNotebooks` true, `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -ImportNotebooks`, and `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1` still update both, edited or not ([ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-fabric-resources-by-name.md#amendment-2026-11-23-an-update-brings-the-notebooks-that-nobody-edited-up-to-date-follow-ups-decision-2a)). A value the import doesn't set is compared too: a `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` changed by hand while `github.repository` is the example's placeholder is an edit, so the notebook is kept. A copy whose code is exactly an earlier version's counts as that version and is updated, even when an edit made it so. That Fabric gives a notebook back with the repository file's code needs a live proof: when it doesn't, every notebook is kept, as before.
- A new VM's notes that its bootstrap gives by design are a **Pass** everywhere (the owner's decision on the expected notes): a sign-in cleanup that cleared an event log after T2's Arc onboarding, the restart SQL Server setup asks for when it removes Analysis Services and Integration Services from a new T1, T1's sysadmin recovery, and Jumpstart's image checked by its pinned SHA-256. The bootstrap records them as information, in health.json's new `notes`, rather than as warnings, so health.json, `validate.ps1`'s `INFRA.HEALTH.<vm>`, `status.ps1`'s `STATUS.VM_<vm>`, and the status page all say **Pass**, and the messages still name each note and why it's expected ("Its bootstrap noted, as information: ..."). Before, each kept a new VM a **Warning** that said "That's expected" (F32). Any other warning still makes it a **Warning**. Only a new VM's own setup gives these notes: on a VM that was set up before, the same events stay a **Warning**, which isn't called expected, since nothing by design causes them there. A VM gets this with the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, which sends it the new bundle; that bundle's first pass clears the notes the earlier one gave as warnings, as it clears every one-off note.
- The fixes that bring `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` up to date name it alone, as the Incident RCA's do: `STOP.FABRIC.SAVEHISTORY`, `FABRIC.T4.HISTORY`, `FABRIC.T5.HISTORY`, the fix for a lakehouse that was just created, and `DEPLOY.FABRIC.HISTORY` for a missing notebook give `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1 -SkipHubChecks -Notebook NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity`, after `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, which updates the notebook unless someone edited it. Before, they named a `configure-fabric.ps1` run that replaced `NB_DatabaseHub_Deployment` too, edits and all. The fixes that only run the notebook again, `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURES`'s and the one for a partial capture of T5's metrics, now skip the import (`-SkipHubChecks -SkipNotebookImport -RunLiveActivity`).

### Security

- **No new grant, resource, or secret:** the capture uses the runner's existing sign-ins, the export the session control's existing Run Command and secret, and the scripts your existing roles ([ADR 0031](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#10-permissions-cost-and-volume)).
- **Redaction:** only the demo's own sessions keep statement text, login, and host; a statement is kept with its literals replaced, and never a password, connection string, or token ([ADR 0031](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#3-redaction-and-privacy)).
- **The bundles stay** as earlier versions of `dbhub-session-metrics` until `destroy.ps1` purges the vault. They hold what the history holds, never a secret, and only those who can already read the history can read them; disabling them would take a new grant ([transport](docs/adr/0031-incident-rca.md#4-transport-into-the-history)).
- **A T2 golden snapshot carries no capture:** `capture-t2-golden.ps1` stops T2's workload runner and deletes what its observer kept in the guest before the snapshot, and captures nothing while any is left. Otherwise a T2 restored from the snapshot would keep this environment's capture, and its first export would give it to the new environment's session (the docs review of 0189a4b). It also captures nothing while T2 documents a history save didn't take are listed, in this PC's pending list or in the session control's status (`GOLDEN.CAPTURE.RCA`), and starts the runner again when a later step fails (the review of 3f6cd14). A status that a tenant's policy has encrypted, which Azure Resource Manager returns with no value, counts as one it couldn't read, and the fix says how to save what it lists first. When the demo workspace's `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` is from before the Incident RCA, which no save takes them with, its fix updates that notebook first (the review of c059878). `GOLDEN.CAPTURE.PREPARE` says how much it deleted, and how much of it no export had taken.

## [2026-11-24] - Release follow-ups

**The release's follow-ups.** Small fixes found during the 2026-11-20 release tests. **How to update:** run `./scripts/deploy.ps1`; your configuration needs no edit.

### Added

- `./scripts/reset-demo.ps1 -RebuildGuest` makes T2's guest `arcsql01` again from Arc Jumpstart's image, for a guest broken beyond `deploy.ps1`'s own repairs, such as one with no network adapter: it asks `archost01` (`RESET.GUEST`), then runs `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts`, which deletes the guest with its disk, makes it again, deletes the old guest's Arc resources, and onboards the new one (`DEPLOY.T2.REBUILD`), in about an hour. T1, T3, T4, T5, and Fabric are untouched. It needs `archost01` running, refuses a guest that a golden capture holds, and in `ProductKey` or `Developer` mode, or the evaluation-only mode, it's **Not supported**, with the fix (`./scripts/destroy.ps1 -KeepFabric`, then `./scripts/deploy.ps1`). Before, it was **Not supported** in every mode.
- T2's host connects its guest's network adapter to the NAT switch again when something took it off (`GuestNetwork`, a new host step, in every mode), so `./scripts/deploy.ps1` repairs a disconnected guest; a golden capture's air gap is left alone. `T2.GUEST.STATE`'s fix for a disconnected guest names `./scripts/deploy.ps1` first, then `-RebuildGuest`, with the by-hand command last. A host gets the step with its next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`.
- Preflight checks that a new deployment's two Azure Automation accounts, the session control's and the nightly safety stop's, fit the subscription's quota in the region (`PREFLIGHT.P3.AUTOMATION`, F30): live, a new environment in a subscription with 10 accounts in westus3 got neither, and found out only at the end. It counts only the accounts the deployment adds, once each, even when Resource Graph still lists them minutes after `./scripts/destroy.ps1` deleted them, against the new optional `automationAccountQuota`, or else Learn's default for the subscription's offer (10 per region for Enterprise and CSP, 2 for pay-as-you-go and Visual Studio). A subscription's own quota can be higher than its offer's default, so the message calls that number the default, and the fix starts with `automationAccountQuota`. A new deployment that doesn't fit is a **Fail** before anything is created, with how to free an account or raise the quota; an environment that has deployed before gets a **Warning** and its update goes on, and one whose accounts are there is never stopped.

### Changed

- `init.ps1` sets a new configuration's hard monthly ceiling (`monthlyCeilingUsd`) from its region's worst case for a Contributor's new deployment, priced then as preflight prices it, with 4% headroom, rounded up to 5 USD, and never below 415, so preflight passes it in every region: before, 415 fit only West US 3 and East US 2. `INIT.CONFIG` says the ceiling and why. A value already in your configuration doesn't change.
- In public mode, a new T5 has no private endpoint unless something needs it, as T3, which saves its endpoint and private DNS zone, about $7.80 a month (2026-11-18 list prices). Its public network access is on, limited to T1's subnet (`snet-sqlvm`) by a virtual network rule and that subnet's Azure Cosmos DB service endpoint, with no IP rule, and Database Hub's Security page lists it, as it does T3's. A new T5 keeps the endpoint in private mode, under a policy that turns Azure Cosmos DB's public network access off or denies it (`PREFLIGHT.POLICY.COSMOSPUBLIC`), and when the policies couldn't all be read. So does one under such a policy assigned to an existing demo resource group, which the inventory doesn't read: preflight's what-if checks the T5 the run deploys, and when Azure Policy denies T5's public network access or turns it off, the run deploys T5 again with the endpoint, in the same run, rather than leaving it out or ending **NOT DONE**. A move to the endpoint keeps T1's subnet's Azure Cosmos DB service endpoint until the account reads public network access off, so a move that Azure refuses for another reason leaves T5 reachable, and `T5.NETWORK` checks that service endpoint. An existing T5 keeps what it has: an update changes nothing in its network, and deletes no endpoint. `DEPLOY.T5.NETWORK` says which and why, `T5.NETWORK` checks it, and the estimate counts the endpoint and its zone only when T5 has or gets them ([ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-t5-cosmos-db.md#2026-11-21-a-private-endpoint-only-when-something-needs-it)).
- The cost estimate counts the session control's Azure Automation job minutes from the light-use month's Run all sessions, the main path, at about 2 billed hours each: 5 in 10 running hours, 80 minutes each, 400 in all. With the nightly stop's 120, that's 20 minutes past the 500 Azure Automation includes, about $0.04 a month (2026-11-03 price); before, it counted 2 five-hour sessions, within them. The cost guide also says that those 500 minutes are shared by every Automation account in the subscription, so a second environment shares them, and that rate plans with a flat discount or a fixed monthly credit don't include them.

### Fixed

- On the first update that turns on T2's migration assessment, the Insights app's **Ready to migrate** linked the Azure portal until the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, because the app deployed a few minutes before the assessment's first upload (F18). The stop at the end of an update already waits for that upload; the app now deploys once more after it, so the card shows T2's readiness from that update on (`FABRIC.APP.ESTATE`'s `t2MigrationReadAt`). When that deploy doesn't pass, the app deployed earlier in the run stays, and `FABRIC.APP.MIGRATION` (a **Warning**) says how to add the readiness.
- `validate.ps1` checks the scenario bounds during a session too (F20): `WORKLOAD.<target>.BOUNDS` was a **Warning** whenever the runner's status listed two or more runs, because a Run Command keeps only the last 4,096 bytes of output. The VM's relay now reads the running runs from the runner's whole status, as it does the profile, and returns them in a compact form; a VM gets it with its next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`.
- `INFRA.HEALTH.<vm>` and `STATUS.VM_<vm>` name the step behind a Ready VM's **Warning**, and what to do (F24): before, they said only "Every step is in the desired state" and that the bootstrap was still working. Each warning now starts with its step, and the health summary carries the first two. A one-off note, such as a restart that has happened since, goes with the next bootstrap generation (the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` that sends the VM a new bundle); any other warning stays while its cause does. A note that a new VM's bootstrap gives by design, such as a new host's sign-in cleanup that cleared an event log, is called expected, with why, and when every warning is one, the fix says there's nothing to do (F32: live on dbhubra, that cleanup was the **Warning**'s only cause).
- A Run all session that a `./scripts/start-demo.ps1` session took over no longer stops the demo at its planned end: the local session's own auto-stop does. Its end still saves its history, and the control notebook says "Taken over: ...", with when that auto-stop comes; **Stop now** again stops the demo sooner. An explicit **Stop now**, the notebook's or the Insights app's, still stops everything. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the runbooks and imports the notebook.
- After a cold start, the control notebook's times for T2 came a few minutes early, because T2's Run Command was still waiting for its guest when the session started. The session's start now follows that Run Command and writes when it set T2's activity. The guide marks T2 until then, and the live view announces T2's beats, and the history saves them, from that time. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the runbooks and imports the notebook.
- A new T2 host's log no longer warns "Database check in the guest: The specified module ... was not loaded": the database step's first check ran before the step copies its tools into the guest. It now says the step copies them in, and any other failure of the check is still a warning.
- When the nightly stop waited for an update and the update's own stop at its end then stopped the demo, `DEPLOY.NIGHTLYSTOP` still said the demo stops later tonight and offered `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1`, while `DEPLOY.ENDSTOP` and **DONE** said it was stopped. It's now a **Pass** that says the run stopped the demo itself. `-KeepRunning`, and a run that leaves the demo running, keep the **Warning**.
- A Run all during the hour after a `deploy.ps1` that the nightly stop waited for no longer loses its VMs mid-session (F25). That run moves the VMs' auto-shutdown to its deferred stop's time, which the session control can't move back, so the session now ends before it, and its **Plan** step says why and how to get a full session (Run all once that time has passed, or `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` first). The moved time is now kept in the demo resource group's `DbHubAutoShutdownMoved` tag, so `start-demo.ps1`, `stop-demo.ps1`, and `status.ps1` still put it back or report it after a Run all session cleared the busy marker; before, it stayed moved until the next `deploy.ps1`. `start-demo.ps1`, taking over from a Run all session on such a night, now cancels the deferred stop and puts the auto-shutdown back too, and **DONE** says what Run all does until then ([ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md#amendment-a-run-all-session-and-a-moved-auto-shutdown-f25-2026-11-21)).
- After a Run all session in the grace of a `deploy.ps1` that the nightly stop waited for, `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` puts the VMs' auto-shutdown back and removes the deferred stop (F27): the session had replaced the busy marker with its own and cleared it at its end, so `stop-demo.ps1` said nothing, and both stayed until the next `deploy.ps1`. `validate.ps1`'s `COST.AUTOSHUTDOWN` now names such a time and these commands, rather than a redeployment.
- T3's Database Agent beat no longer starts without its regression (F26): a lookup that another session compiled around the switch could put the fast plan back within the same second, so T3's CPU stayed low and Database Agents had no issue to raise. The other sessions now stay out from 2 seconds before the switch, and the run checks the lookup's cached plan after it and switches again, at most twice, when it doesn't scan. A switch that doesn't hold is the run's `plan-switch-unverified` event, and Run all's live view says from T3's CPU whether the regression started (a **Warning** below 30%), as the session's summary does. Every way a session ends still puts `FORCE_LAST_GOOD_PLAN` back ([ADR 0013](docs/adr/0013-agent-experience.md#amendment-2026-11-21-the-database-agent-beats-switch-is-checked-f26)).
- The Insights app no longer rounds a number to 100 % when it isn't: **Ready** on the Readiness page and **Demo readiness** on the Overview read "more than 99 %" until every check passes, and so does the Performance page's largest wait family and busiest hour. T5's normalized RU peak, beside its **Pass** on the Estate page (the tile, the chart's summary, and its peak marker), on the service map, and in **Live now**, and T5's lowest availability, read 99.96 %, not "100.0 %", the throttling line. Nor does a value just under a status line read as the line beside the other status: a CPU peak of 79.996 % reads "79.996 %", not "80.0 %" beside a **Pass** (the CPU tiles of T1 to T4, their charts' summaries and peak markers, T4's CPU insight, and the service map), T4's read replica lag of 59.96 s reads "59.96 s" (its tile, its chart's peak marker, and its insight), and T5's lowest availability of 99.989 % reads "99.989 %", not "99.99 %" beside a **Warning**. An incident's ratio to its baseline just under the 2× **Warning** reads "1.96×", not "2.0×" without it (the incident insight, the Incidents page's tile, and the replay). `./scripts/deploy.ps1` deploys the app.
- **DONE** and the status page no longer say "Run all is ready" when Run all can't start a session (F28). Live, Azure refused the session control's Automation account because the subscription's quota for Automation accounts in the region was used up, yet `DEPLOY.CONTROL` was **Pass**, and Run all then ended at once, blaming the vault's permissions. `DEPLOY.CONTROL` now checks that the control's Automation account, its vault, its webhook, and the session status are there. When one isn't, it's a **Warning** that names it, the step that says why, and the fix, and **DONE** names `./scripts/start-demo.ps1` for each session. A quota refusal says so in plain words in `CONTROL.RESOURCES` and `SAFETYSTOP.RESOURCES`, with how to free an account (`./scripts/destroy.ps1` on an environment you no longer use) or raise the quota. When an update's setup of the control stopped early but what an earlier run set up still works, `DEPLOY.CONTROL` is a **Warning** that says so, not a **Pass** ([ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#2026-11-21-amendment-run-all-is-ready-only-when-run-all-can-start-a-session-f28)).
- A health read no longer stays Pending for up to 10 minutes, and `validate.ps1` no longer fails `INFRA.BOOTSTRAP.LOADER_ARCHOST01` with "archost01 has no bootstrap loader Run Command" on a running demo (F31). The VM's Run Command handler skipped a Run Command named `DbHubDemo-HealthRead` while its earlier delete hadn't reached it. Health reads, T1's SQL mode read, and T2's isolation probe now run under a fresh name each time, without the leftover wait, and a health read that hasn't started in 90 seconds runs once more under another. The fresh copies don't pile up: each is deleted after its read, a copy left more than an hour goes with the next read, and at 24 Run Commands on a VM a read uses its fixed name, as before. When the health read doesn't answer, the loader check is a **Warning** that says so, not a **Fail** ([ADR 0012](docs/adr/0012-validation-model.md#2026-11-22-a-health-read-that-doesnt-answer-isnt-a-missing-loader-f31)). The end of an update, which reads each host's health before it stops it, no longer waits those minutes either. `status.ps1` doesn't count its own health read, whose delete is still in flight seconds later, as a Run Command stuck on T2's host (`STATUS.RUNCOMMANDS`): one whose name ends in a time less than 15 minutes old is only in the result's evidence, but a chunk Run Command never is. Every Run Command that changes something runs under a fresh name too, since the handler skips a name it ran before whatever the script does. That covers the T2 host's one-time password, which a skipped set left unchanged at a stop, the parallel tracks' bootstrap loader, whose retry ended **NOT DONE**, Arc onboarding, and the occasional ones. Each still deletes what an earlier version left under its fixed name first, waits for its own delete, and is never sent twice, and the loader check reads the newest fresh copy when `DbHubDemo-Bootstrap` isn't there. When Azure keeps that fixed name in Deleting, the run reports it (`DEPLOY.RUNCOMMAND.STUCK_<VM>`), and the same step waits 30 seconds at most for it after that ([ADR 0012](docs/adr/0012-validation-model.md#2026-11-22-fresh-names-for-actions-too-the-review-of-f31)). Each send of the bootstrap bundle or the workload runner gives its chunk Run Commands a suffix of their own, so sending the same content to a VM again never reuses a chunk's name ([ADR 0003](docs/adr/0003-t2-host-size-security-type-bootstrap.md#2026-11-22-amendment-a-name-of-its-own-for-each-send-the-review-of-8ff25e95)). The fresh names for actions and chunks need a live proof before they ship.
- Run all, and the control notebook's **Stop now**, say that the session control isn't set up, with `deploy.ps1`'s fix, when that's why they can't read the control vault or its webhook (F29): live, Run all said "you need Key Vault Secrets User on it", which nobody could have fixed that way. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` puts what it found missing in the notebook's new `CONTROL_PROBLEM` parameter when it imports it, and clears it once the control is set up.
- Run all with a `DURATION_MINUTES` under a session's 30 minutes says so, "Nothing is started: a session runs at least 30 minutes, and you asked for 20", with the fix, "set DURATION_MINUTES to 30 or more" (F33): live, at 14:20, it said the VMs' nightly auto-shutdown at 19:00 was too soon. It names the auto-shutdown only when that's what leaves too little time. One over the showcase's 120 minutes is refused the same way, "at most 120", where the plan said it ran 120 of the minutes asked, cut short by the auto-shutdown, and then the session's request failed. Without an auto-shutdown, a duration under 30 no longer reads "This session runs 20 minutes" while nothing starts. One that isn't a whole number is refused the same way, before anything is sent, and quoted as it was given: "a whole number of minutes, from 30 to 120, and you asked for 45.5" (F33's review). Before, the plan read 45.5 as 45 minutes and then the session's request failed, None or "abc" stopped the plan with a Python error, and True read "you asked for 1". A whole number given as a float or as text, such as 90.0 or "90", as a pipeline or the Fabric job API can pass a parameter, now runs as 90, where its request failed. The plan and the request read the duration with one rule, and an activity's `intensity` and `concurrency` are read the same way. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` imports the notebook.
- `connection-surge` is graded and described by what Azure Monitor measures, T4's **Connections** at 35 or more, the slots clients can use (`max_connections` 50, less the 15 Azure reserves), and never by **Failed connections** (F34). Live, every wave's attempts past the limit were refused while Azure Monitor's `connections_failed` stayed 0 in every interval, and **Connections** peaked at 36 to 39 in each surge. The scenario promised **Failed connections** above 0; now its expected signals, its README, `T4.MAXCONNECTIONS`'s messages, and the docs name Connections, and say that a client refused at the limit isn't in Failed connections. The live-activity notebook adds a `postgresql.T4.connections` finding, a **Warning** at 35 or more, and its failed-connections finding says what it leaves out. The Insights app's Estate page shows T4's **Connections, peak** against the 35 slots, with the chart's line, where a **Failed connections** tile read **Pass** at 0. Mission Control's T4 card shows the same peak, and a session's surges are T4's measured episodes. The business lens says "connections at the server's limit", where it said "connection attempts refused". A recorded surge's **Signal seen?** is graded by those connections, where it was **Not supported** under "Buffer IO (PAGEIOLATCH) waits": "io" inside "connection" matched the IO family, in the app and in the notebook, and so did `idle-in-transaction`'s. R645 is closed. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` publishes the app, and imports the live-activity notebook where a workspace doesn't have it. An existing workspace keeps its notebook, with no `postgresql.T4.connections` finding, no caveat on failed connections, and a surge's window labeled "IO pressure", until `fabric.importNotebooks` is true, `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -ImportNotebooks` runs, or `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1` does (F34's review); the app's grades don't depend on it.
- The Insights app's T4 and T5 pages, Mission Control's T4 and T5 cards, the replay's and kiosk's T4 and T5 lanes, and T4's and T5's measured episodes read every save in the latest capture's window, each interval from its finest save (F35). Before, they read the latest save alone, so after an update, whose day-long save at its end is every 15 minutes, a session's own 1- and 5-minute points were gone: in a live test's history, the read replica's lag showed 178 s from 16:15 for 15 minutes, where the session had it at 16:20 for 5. Now a coarser save fills only the minutes the finer ones don't cover, a total as what's left of it and a peak only where it's higher, so nothing counts twice and nothing is lost: that save's 34 s of lag from 16:57 to 17:00, after the session's window ended, still shows. Totals show per minute where the window holds saves at different intervals, the replay's 429s too, and a run that the metrics break off from says so at its last interval, not one of the newest save's intervals before its end (F35's review). The rule is one function, `fabric/app/src/data/finest-per-interval.ts`, which the incident RCA uses too. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` publishes the app. The history model's telemetry measures still take each bin from its newest save, so the history report's telemetry and the app's T1 to T3 views count a session's minutes twice once a later save covers them; that fix is pending ([limitations](docs/limitations.md#the-history-models-overlapping-saves-f35)).
- T3's storage IO is its files' again (F36). Every telemetry sample of T3 holds two files under each of its database's FileID 1 and 2, with counters of their own, and the storage query's series by file stepped between them: each step up counted the gap between their totals as IO, and each step down was dropped as a reset. In a live test's session, T3's IO came to 735,042 MB where its files' counters rose by 10,358 MB, in the history report, the Insights app's IO for T3 (Estate, Performance, and the replay), the Real-Time Dashboard's IO tiles, and the live-activity notebook's IO table, chart, and IO signal for a scenario on T3. It was so since the first release. The query now adds the files of each ID within a sample before its deltas, and skips a step whose number of files changed; on that session, T1's and T2's IO is the same to the byte. The agent prompts for Copilot CLI and VS Code that ask for KQL on `SqlServerStorageIO`, and the Insights app's IO prompt, now ask the agent to add the rows of each database and file ID within each sample before taking deltas, and to report them as one file. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` updates the Real-Time Dashboard, and imports the live-activity notebook where a workspace doesn't have it. An existing workspace keeps its notebook, whose saves keep T3's IO too high, until `fabric.importNotebooks` is true, `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -ImportNotebooks` runs, or `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1` does; a save from before keeps its values until a save of the same window replaces it ([troubleshooting](docs/troubleshooting.md#database-hub-and-telemetry), [limitations](docs/limitations.md#preview-disclosures)).
- Azure refusing a size for T4, or for its read replica, over the subscription's vCore quota for Azure Database for PostgreSQL no longer ends the run at that size (F37): only `SkuNotAvailable` moved the size fallback on, so the refusal failed the template, or left the session without its replica. Azure's refusal, "exceeding approved ... Cores quota" as Learn gives it, now moves the fallback on as `SkuNotAvailable` does, in `deploy.ps1`, `start-demo.ps1`, and Run all's sessions, but never to the same size in another zone, since a quota is the subscription's in the region; the notice names the quota with Azure's figures, what was deployed, and the cost difference. When Azure refuses every size, `DEPLOY.T4.SKU`, `DEPLOY.T4.OFF`, `DEPLOY.T4.REPLICA`, Run all's status, and the status page name the ways out: free vCores in the region (a stopped server still counts), request more quota, or another region. Preflight now logs the region's vCore usage against T4 and its replica, and keeps it in `PREFLIGHT.T4.REPLICA`'s evidence, for diagnosis only: live, Azure created servers past those readings in West US 3, so they stop and warn on nothing ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-22-f37-a-refusal-over-the-subscriptions-vcore-quota-moves-the-fallback-on-preflight-reads-the-quota-for-diagnosis-only)). `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the runbooks.
- `./scripts/destroy.ps1 -WhatIf` runs to its end in a non-interactive shell, such as an assistant's: it failed with "PowerShell is in NonInteractive mode". Without `-Force`, destroy passes on `-WhatIf` with `-Confirm`, and before its one question, with the list of what it deletes and keeps, the subscription guard didn't record its context under `-WhatIf`, so the list's reads of Fabric and the security group couldn't run, and each Azure CLI call asked twice about its temporary file, when it wrote the CLI's errors to it and when it deleted it. Everything before that question only reads, so now it runs as without `-WhatIf` and asks nothing, and nothing asks again after it; in a terminal, `./scripts/destroy.ps1` without `-Force` asks that one question only, where the guard and each Azure CLI call asked too. An Azure CLI call, and a .NET SDK call, now asks nothing for any caller with `-Confirm`: in a terminal, `./scripts/check-sign-in-data.ps1 -Clean` without `-Force` asked about a temporary file after a Yes for a VM, and so would the module's `Publish-DemoWorkloadRunner -Confirm`. Tests run `destroy.ps1 -WhatIf`, both kinds of call for a caller with `-Confirm`, and a .NET SDK call for one with `-WhatIf`, in a non-interactive shell.
- Run all's note on a read replica that runs on another size than T4's no longer says it "has more vCores and costs more" when it has T4's vCores: the note said so whenever the sizes differed, also for `Standard_D4ads_v5` in place of `Standard_D4ds_v5`, the next size tried, and F37 makes that the likely fallback for a quota refusal. The note, which the notebook prints as the Replica step's message, now says it costs more only when the size has more vCores than T4's; otherwise it says the size has T4's vCores, and that `./scripts/estimate-cost.ps1` shows what it costs. `deploy.ps1`'s Warning when it tries `Standard_D4ads_v5` for T4, and `t4SkuName`'s description, no longer say that size costs the same per vCore, which the Azure Retail Prices API doesn't list in every region: its 4-vCore rows for the two sizes differed in Norway West on 2026-11-22. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the runbooks.
- An existing T4's move between `Standard_D4ds_v5` and `Standard_D4ads_v5` is priced first, as a move to a larger size is: `deploy.ps1` counted the two as one price, so such a move, to the default or to a size `t4SkuName` sets, met neither cost ceiling when the run skipped preflight (`./scripts/deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure`). They're separate meters, whose list prices can differ in a region. Over either ceiling, or without a price, T4 keeps its size, and `DEPLOY.T4.SIZE` names the ceiling to raise ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-04-user-directed-general-purpose-standard_d4ds_v5-by-default-with-autonomous-tuning)). A move to the size `t4SkuName` sets between the two now deletes T4's read replica first, as a move to a larger size does, and the run creates it again at T4's new size. The fallback's order still doesn't compare the two sizes' prices, which the limitations page now says ([limitations](docs/limitations.md#targets-and-licensing)).
- T4's estimate in Indonesia Central priced `Standard_D4ads_v5` at about a tenth of its list price, in `./scripts/estimate-cost.ps1`, preflight's cost checks, and every T4 and read replica cost notice there. The Azure Retail Prices API listed its series' per-vCore meter there twice, as a "vCore" row at about a tenth of its "1 vCore" row (2026-11-22), and the estimate read the cheaper row. It now reads a series' "1 vCore" row first, then its "vCore" row. That changes one other price: in UAE Central, `Standard_D4ads_v5` reads its series' newer "1 vCore" meter, listed since 2026-10-01, about 17 % above the older "vCore" one, because the newer meter is the likelier bill and an estimate too low could let a move past the cost ceilings. Every other region's T4 prices are the same, as a check of every region and T4 size on 2026-11-22 showed.
- `./scripts/setup-activity-control.ps1 -Remove`, and `destroy.ps1`'s `DESTROY.CONTROL.RESOURCES`, say which of the session control's resources were already gone, where they said "Deleted" for them: live, after `destroy.ps1` had deleted the control, `-Remove` listed all three as deleted. Each is now read first; one that's gone gets no delete, and the message lists it under "Already gone", and the control's group too when it's gone. So when Azure would refuse its delete, as it does for a role without the delete permission, one that's gone no longer fails the run or holds back the Automation account.

### Security

- The Ask relay passes on GitHub's device sign-in page only when it's exactly `https://github.com/login/device` (https, origin `https://github.com`), and that page otherwise, as defense in depth: the Insights app already links only that page. It takes effect with the next relay image, which `config/versions.json` (`askRelay.image`) pins once CI has published it.

## [2026-11-23] - The Ignite updates

**The Ignite updates.** Microsoft Fabric Database Hub is generally available since 2026-11-17, and the demo follows it.

**What's new:**

- **T5, Azure Cosmos DB for NoSQL,** in every deployment and every Run all session, with an RU storm that Database Hub shows as throttling. Its container, which holds the throughput, exists only during a session ([ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-t5-cosmos-db.md)).
- **The Insights app is the demo's operations product:** it opens on Mission Control, with the "Did we catch it?" scoreboard, a replay, and a postmortem of each session ([ADR 0030](docs/adr/0030-insights-app-mission-control.md)); it's a DBA console over the saved history, with T2's migration readiness; and **Manage** starts a session, runs an incident, stops, or resets, each bounded and confirmed ([ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-insights-app-dba-console.md)).
- **Ask, a GitHub Copilot chat in the Insights app,** on each viewer's own Copilot plan, through a small relay in your resource group that scales to zero ([ADR 0028](docs/adr/0028-ask-copilot-in-the-insights-app.md)).
- **The Database Agent loop on T3** in every Run all session, and **T2's migration assessment** for Database Hub's Optimization view.
- **The docs follow Database Hub's GA:** what's generally available, and what's still a preview.
- **Fixes:** an update that broke PowerShell Direct into T2's guest ([#65](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/65)), and a first deployment on a busy Fabric capacity ([#68](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/68)).

**How to update:** run `./scripts/deploy.ps1`. Nothing changes until you do, your configuration needs no edit, and there's nothing to build. Then:

- **If you aren't an Owner:** an Owner runs, once, the `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1` that **DONE** names first. Until then, Run all can't run a whole session, and T5's container stays between sessions and bills.
- **Once, for the Database Agent beat:** select T3 for Database Agents ([step by step](docs/database-agents.md)); everything else works without it. **DONE** lists it under **Please confirm** (`HUB.AGENT.T3`).
- **For Ask:** each viewer signs in to GitHub once per browser.
- **To demo right after it:** an update that found the demo stopped stops what it started at its end (`DEPLOY.ENDSTOP`); `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -KeepRunning` leaves it running.

**Cost:** T3 now scales up to 2 vCores. A new configuration's ceilings are $415 a month and $20 a session; an existing configuration keeps its monthly ceiling, and one whose session ceiling is 15 gets 20. Ask's relay bills only while someone asks. An update of a stopped demo stops it again at its end, so it no longer bills until 19:00, and a `start-demo.ps1` session stops by itself after 4 hours by default. `./scripts/estimate-cost.ps1` prices your configuration from Azure's list prices; the Fabric capacity, licenses, and each viewer's GitHub Copilot plan aren't in it ([what it costs](README.md#what-it-costs)).

[Upgrading an existing demo](README.md#upgrading-an-existing-demo) has the details; [Delete everything](README.md#delete-everything) says what `destroy.ps1` removes and what stays.

### Added

- **T5, Azure Cosmos DB for NoSQL** (`cosmos01-<id>`), in every deployment: Microsoft Entra only, a private endpoint, the subscription's free tier when it's free; its container exists only during a session; the `ru-storm` scenario; `"t5Enabled": false` leaves it out ([ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-t5-cosmos-db.md)).
- **Ask, the Copilot chat in the Insights app,** on each viewer's own GitHub Copilot plan, through a relay Container App (`ca-<prefix>-<environment>-ask`) that scales to zero and holds no credential; nothing to build; `"ask": { "enabled": false }` leaves it out ([ADR 0028](docs/adr/0028-ask-copilot-in-the-insights-app.md)).
- **Manage in the Insights app:** start a session, run an incident, stop now, or reset, each bounded and confirmed; operators send from the app, and others copy the request for Run all. A session it starts bills like any other ([ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-insights-app-dba-console.md)).
- **The Database Agent loop on T3** in every Run all session, with one selection per deployment, which the beat requires (`HUB.AGENT.T3`) ([walkthrough](docs/demo-walkthrough.md#the-database-agent-loop-on-t3)).
- **[Database Agents for T3](docs/database-agents.md), step by step:** required once for the Database Agent beat; the steps for a demo or lab tenant only you use (a reset first) and for a tenant others use too, the Agent policy, what was seen live on 2026-11-20, and troubleshooting rows for a save that fails or is "temporarily limited", look-alike `ContosoCare` rows, and no PostgreSQL or Cosmos DB in the agent's list.
- **T2's migration assessment,** turned on by `deploy.ps1`, at no charge ([walkthrough](docs/demo-walkthrough.md#optimization-t2-ready-to-migrate)).
- **The GA Copilot prompts,** links to Database Hub's PostgreSQL and Cosmos DB dashboards, and `presenterObjectIds` as the demo workspace's Viewers.

### Changed

- **The Insights app is a DBA console:** Fluent 2 in Microsoft's colors (Microsoft blue, with the Microsoft logo's colors as accents; the owner's choice), twelve pages over the history, opening on Mission Control ([ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-insights-app-dba-console.md), [ADR 0030](docs/adr/0030-insights-app-mission-control.md)). Its live views from Database Hub aren't available yet: Database Hub refuses the app's own sign-in (R924), so the pages show no live parts, **Live views** says so in one line, and Database Hub itself has the live view.
- **The Insights app's scoreboard says what it verifies:** "Did we catch it?" has the subtitle "Verified against the injected faults: each one's expected signal, in its own window", and it says **Signal seen** instead of "Caught", and **time to signal** instead of "time to detect", in the app and its postmortem.
- **T3 scales to 0.5 to 2 vCores** (Database Agents needs more than one); `deploy.ps1` scales an existing T3 online.
- **The ceilings:** `sessionCeilingUsd` 20 (a configuration with 15 gets 20); `monthlyCeilingUsd` 415 for a new configuration; preflight counts the nightly safety stop that an Owner's first run sets up ([ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md)).
- **The cost checks show their totals:** preflight's monthly check names the total it compares with the ceiling, `estimate-cost.ps1` prices a new environment as preflight will for you, and no Bastion hour is counted by default ([ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-19-the-monthly-check-says-its-total-and-no-bastion-hour-by-default)).
- **DONE** names Run all as each session's path, and, when you aren't an Owner, the one grant an Owner runs, first.
- **An update leaves the demo as it found it:** when the demo was stopped as `deploy.ps1` began, the run stops again what it started once **DONE**'s checks are done (`DEPLOY.ENDSTOP`); a new deployment keeps running, and **DONE** says what it bills an hour; `-KeepRunning` keeps an update running ([ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-19-an-update-leaves-the-demo-as-it-found-it-and-a-start-demops1-session-stops-by-itself)).
- **A `start-demo.ps1` session stops by itself** 4 hours after its start (`-Hours`), through the same auto-stop as Run all's ([ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md#amendment-2026-11-19-start-demops1-schedules-its-own-auto-stop)).
- **The docs follow Database Hub's GA;** the presenter's fallback agent uses the GA `databasehub-cli` skill ([agent guide](fabric/agent/README.md)).
- **The user docs are shorter and current:** the README's quick start and cost come first; [cost management](docs/cost-management.md#at-a-glance) starts with four tables from the module's own estimator, each figure with and without Defender for Cloud, and what's not included; each fact is said once; and the fixes the scripts print point only to what works: `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, an Owner's `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1`, or `./scripts/destroy.ps1 -KeepFabric`.

### Removed

- The Insights app's decorative visuals, web fonts, and motion, with its "History, not live" label ([ADR 0029](docs/adr/0029-insights-app-dba-console.md)).
- DONE's and the status page's suggestion to capture a T2 golden snapshot: a snapshot bills every month, also after `destroy.ps1`, and T2's build needs nobody ([deployment](docs/deployment.md)).
- Printed directions to set the session control up or grant its roles with `setup-activity-control.ps1`, to rebuild T2 with `reset-demo.ps1 -RebuildGuest`, to finish a T2 setup wizard, to repair by hand first, or to run GitHub Actions: `deploy.ps1` sets the control up, an Owner's `grant-safety-stop.ps1` grants its roles, and no user builds the relay image.

### Fixed

- The Insights app's **Sign in to GitHub** in its header opened an empty page inside Fabric (the owner's report): it now opens the Copilot panel, which gets GitHub's code and shows it with **Copy the code and open GitHub**, opens no tab of its own, and the header shows the sign-in's progress and why one didn't finish.
- The GitHub sign-in opens only GitHub's own device page, github.com/login/device, whatever page the chat relay's answer names; an answer without a code no longer breaks the app; and selecting the header's chip while a sign-in finishes no longer starts a second one that left it saying "signing in".
- An update that broke PowerShell Direct into T2's guest ([#65](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/65)).
- A first deployment on a busy Fabric capacity ended **NOT DONE** ([#68](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/68)).
- `destroy.ps1` kept the demo's workspace because of the Insights app's functions item; run it again if an earlier destroy left it.
- `validate.ps1` no longer spends minutes reading the Insights app's packages, and a stopped T4 is a **Warning**, not a **Fail**.
- The history keeps one correlation finding per session beat.
- A session's planned times came early on a VM that had just started; each target's now counts from when its runner set the profile ([ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#2026-11-19-amendment-each-targets-showcase-counts-from-when-its-runner-set-it)).
- T5's activity now runs to the session's end, not just to its last storm's recovery.
- A Contributor who deployed can Run all after an Owner's one `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1`, with nothing to edit (`CONTROL.ROLE.DEPLOYER`).
- A second environment (`init.ps1 -Prefix`) got the first one's Fabric workspace; it gets "Database Hub Demo `<prefix>`", and preflight stops before anything is created when a configuration names another environment's workspace (`DEPLOY.FABRIC.SHARED`).
- Every command `init.ps1` prints names the run's `-Prefix` and `-ConfigPath`, and `init.ps1 -Force` keeps the prefix of the file it replaces, so following one never writes another environment's configuration.
- A Contributor's update from `main`, whose configuration keeps 375 or 390, stopped at preflight because T5's container counted all month; when only that puts the month over the ceiling, it's a **Warning** now, with the fix (an Owner's `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1` once), and the update goes on.
- A session's history that a cancel cut off is completed with its beats when it's saved again, and analyzed once.
- A resumed `deploy.ps1` run's **DONE** dropped what the earlier run found, such as `HUB.AGENT.T3` under **Please confirm** and the Insights app's state; it lists them now, and says which run they came from (`DEPLOY.CARRIED`).
- A first deployment could end **NOT DONE** at T2's sign-in data check right after the Arc onboarding. The check now waits for the host's own cleanup and recovers T2's guest, and **NOT DONE**'s one next step is `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, which checks again by itself.
- `check-sign-in-data.ps1 -Clean` waited on its question where no one could answer; it now stops at once, changing nothing, and gives the same command with `-Force` (`SECURITY.SIGNIN.CONFIRM`).
- `destroy.ps1` said it kept a golden group that doesn't exist; it now says the group doesn't exist (`DESTROY.GOLDEN`).
- A new environment's Insights app copied every Manage request for Run all; its first `deploy.ps1` now gives Manage the control notebook (`FABRIC.APP.ACTIONS`).
- A `rayfin up` whose Azure CLI token expired during the command was taken for Fabric refusing the token, so the Insights app fell back to the PC's own Rayfin sign-in, which a new PC doesn't have. `configure-fabric-app.ps1` now takes a new token right before each `rayfin up`, and runs it once more with a new one when the token expired (F16).
- `destroy.ps1` called an environment that was still being deployed one "deployed by an earlier version of the demo"; it now says it hasn't recorded its workspaces yet.
- `validate.ps1` reported a **Fail** in `SECURITY.SECRETSCAN.OUTPUT` on every environment: a test of the Insights app held a fake bearer token as text, and the app's deploy stage, `out/fabric-app`, copies its tests too. The test builds it at run time now, and a new test runs the output scan over the staged app. The next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` replaces the stage's copy.
- After `destroy.ps1`, every **Save** in Database Hub's **Database agent settings** failed ("Database agent changes couldn't be saved"): a deleted T3 can stay selected there for a while, and no API removes it. `destroy.ps1` now says to uncheck only its T3 there and **Save**, before it asks and at its end (`DESTROY.HUB.AGENT.T3`). `HUB.AGENT.T3` and Run all's guide give the steps for a tenant where only you use Database Agents, which reset the selection first, and for one others use too, which leave their databases selected; `HUB.AGENT.T3` also says how to tell `ContosoCare` rows apart, and [Database Agents for T3](docs/database-agents.md) what to do when a **Save** fails.
- The Insights app's scoreboard said a fault's signal "stayed under" its level when a bin had gone over it (found live: "CPU stayed under 10.0 %" beside a bin at 65.95 %). A fault that isn't visible now says what its bins measured, with their peak, never written equal to the level it stayed under, and a level that isn't whole is written with its decimals ("at least 0.45", never "at least 0"), and so is every peak beside a level ("its peak was 23.33 %" beside "23.32 % or more", never "23.3 %"), in the business impact too ("up to 0.6 blocked processes", never "up to 1"); a throttled share is never rounded to none or to all, in the business words and in Mission Control's numbers ("less than 1 %", "more than 99 %"), nor is T5's on the Estate page ("less than 0.1 %"), the busiest target's share of the wait time (on the Overview and Performance), or the Readiness page's share of the checks that pass; and the T4 and T5 card writes its peaks as measured, so a peak under 100 % never reads "100 %"; a signal is "still visible" at the telemetry's end only when its last bin was, and one whose telemetry breaks off says so; and the postmortem says the same, saying a thing of every fault only when it's so of every one. Under Windows forced colors, the navigation's current page and the kiosk's current scene are marked with the system's selection colors, their names legible (black on cyan, 14.11:1, where Edge had painted them over its black readability backplate, 1:1).

### Security

- The session control's role gains T5's container read, write, and delete, and the nightly safety stop's role gains read and delete, at the demo resource group only (approved by the owner on 2026-11-19; an Owner's `deploy.ps1` or `grant-safety-stop.ps1` updates them).
- The one exception to "documented interfaces only": the Insights app's estate function reads 8 allowlisted, read-only Database Hub routes ([ADR 0013](docs/adr/0013-agent-experience.md#amendment-2026-11-18-the-ga-skill-and-one-narrow-exception)).
- Ask's relay runs a public image pinned by digest and holds no Azure or Fabric credential; Manage sends only for operators, known by their Fabric sign-in.

### Details of this release

The engineering detail behind the lines above, for maintainers; the [implementation report](docs/implementation-report.md) has the validation and the risks.

#### Added

- **The Database Agent loop on T3** (2026-11-18; the review of the Ignite announcements, item 3, with the owner's and the coordinator's decisions; [ADR 0013](docs/adr/0013-agent-experience.md), [ADR 0010](docs/adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md)). In showcase cycle 2, T3's `plan-regression` run is the Database Agent beat: it holds `FORCE_LAST_GOOD_PLAN` off (`demo.auto_tuning_hold`), regresses at 10% of its 30 minutes, and at 85% makes Database Agents' documented manual response itself, forcing the last good plan through Query Store and turning `FORCE_LAST_GOOD_PLAN` back on. `demo.scenario_run_events` logs `auto-tuning-held`, `agent-response`, and `auto-tuning-restored` with who restored it.
  - **Every way a session ends restores it:** the response, the run's end, reset, a cancel, the next run's start, `stop-demo.ps1` (through T3 itself, before it stops T1, so only a demo hold is put back; `STOP.T3.AUTOTUNING`), and `deploy.ps1`'s setup. `status.ps1` shows it (`STATUS.T3.AUTOTUNING`).
  - **Run all:** the guide gives the one-time **Manage agents** selection, the exact database, and the beat's times in the demo's clock; the live view prints "Now: T3 Respond" and compares T3's CPU before and after the response; the summary has the loop's line.
  - **The one manual step:** Database Agents has no API, so `deploy.ps1` lists the selection under **Please confirm**, with the link and the database (`HUB.AGENT.T3`), and `validate.ps1 -Area T3` checks what Azure can see (`T3.AGENT`: write access, a one-vCore serverless T3, an elastic pool, monitoring; `T3.AUTOTUNING`).
  - **The history:** `SESSION_PLAN`'s agent beat carries `agent`, `regressUtc`, and `respondUtc`; `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` writes the beat's `scenario_runs` message and a `database_agent` finding (T3's CPU from 8 to 1 minutes before the response and from 1 to 6 after; **Pass** when it fell by a quarter or 10 points), and the history model's Analysis table has the key.
- **T2's migration assessment** (2026-11-18, item 4): `configure-arc.ps1` and every `deploy.ps1` turn it on for T2's SQL Server instance (Sql Server Instances - Update, `properties.migration.assessment.enabled`) and start a run (`runMigrationAssessment`) until one has uploaded, so Database Hub's GA Optimization insights have T2's migration assessment ready. `T2.ARC.MIGRATIONASSESSMENT` reports each Azure SQL target's readiness. It's free.
- **The GA Copilot prompts** (2026-11-18): [overview.in-hub](fabric/agent/prompts/overview.in-hub.md) asks "What changed in my databases?", "Which resource should I investigate next?", and "Which security findings should I address first?"; Run all's guide has them too.
- **One dashboard for every engine** (2026-11-18, item 5, the coordinator's option a): `RTD_DatabaseHub_LiveView` links Database Hub's GA PostgreSQL and Cosmos DB real-time dashboards, and `configure-fabric.ps1` adds the configuration's `presenterObjectIds` to the demo workspace as Viewers (`FABRIC.PRESENTER.VIEWER`), with no Log Analytics workspace.
- **Demo it yourself** (2026-11-18, user-directed: "Make sure to account for users signing in and demoing this themselves and repeatable. Simple and one click as possible"): the README's steps for anyone who deploys the demo, from `az login` to the one-time agent selection, the DBA moments, and reset, stop, and repeat.
- **T5: Azure Cosmos DB for NoSQL, in every deployment** (user-directed, 2026-11-18: item 2 of the Ignite 2026 review, "Azure Cosmos DB as T5", and "Make sure to account for users signing in and demoing this themselves and repeatable. Simple and one click as possible"; [ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-t5-cosmos-db.md)). Database Hub, generally available since 2026-11-17, shows Azure Cosmos DB in Overview, Estate, Performance (normalized RU, throttled requests, availability), and Security, from Azure Monitor with no setup; the demo had no Cosmos DB.
  - **The account:** `cosmos01-<deploymentId>`, one region, Microsoft Entra only (`disableLocalAuth`), public network access off, with a private endpoint and the `privatelink.documents.azure.com` zone in both network modes; the database `contosocare` and the container `patient-events` (`/patientId`) at 400 RU/s of manual throughput. T1's managed identity gets Cosmos DB Built-in Data Contributor at the account, a data-plane role assignment that Contributor creates, so no key exists and no Azure role assignment is added. It uses the subscription's one free-tier account when no other account has it (`DEPLOY.T5.FREETIER`; Azure sets it only at creation), but not in an internal subscription, which Azure refuses it in ("Free tier is not supported for Internal subscriptions", live 2026-11-18; the run reads the subscription's offer first), and deploys again without it, once, with Azure's reason, when Azure refuses it anyway. `t5UseFreeTier` false never asks for it; `t5Enabled` false leaves T5 out (`DEPLOY.T5.OFF`).
  - **One click, new and upgraded alike:** the template creates T5 with T3 and T4. T1's track checks it (`T5.ACCOUNT`, `T5.ENTRAONLY`, `T5.NETWORK`, `T5.CONTAINER`, `T5.FREETIER`, `T5.ACCESS`) and warms the runner up against it through T1, retrying while the new role and private DNS record come into effect; **DONE** needs T5 in Database Hub's estate (`HUB.ESTATE.T5`) and its Azure Monitor metrics (`METRICS.T5`). An older demo gets T5 from a plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1`: a resumed run whose environment has no account starts from preflight (`DEPLOY.T5.MISSING`). Nothing to sign in to or set up: Database Hub reads T5 with your Reader access. The status page has a T5 group.
  - **The workload and the DBA moment:** the runner drives T5 through the documented REST API over HTTPS with T1's identity, only in `contosocare`/`patient-events`. `healthy-baseline` upserts and reads a fixed set of documents. The new `ru-storm` (T5 only; 10 minutes by default, 30 at most) writes waves to one hot partition until normalized RU reaches 100% and requests are throttled (429), with calm gaps in which both recover; it upserts a fixed set of 100 documents and deletes them at its end, however it ends, and `reset-demo.ps1 -Target T5` deletes any left over, so every session starts from the same data. `./scripts/demo-control.ps1 -Target T5 -Scenario incident:ru-storm -Duration 10` starts it; `business-day` runs it at :25 each hour; Run all's showcase runs it twice in each 30-minute cycle, and no storm is running when the session ends (since 2026-11-19 T5's baseline keeps going to the session's end: a storm that couldn't end 2 minutes before then doesn't start; R896). The walkthrough's [RU storm (T5)](docs/demo-walkthrough.md#ru-storm-t5) has the rehearsal steps.
  - **Stop, start, cost, and teardown:** an account can't be stopped, so `stop-demo.ps1` turns T5's activity off and says what bills on (`STOP.T5`, **Not supported**), and `start-demo.ps1` has nothing to start (`START.T5`). `estimate-cost.ps1`, preflight, and `validate.ps1 -Area Cost` price it all month from the Retail Prices API: the throughput (the Free Tier meters when it has or gets the free tier), about 1 GB of storage, the private endpoint, its DNS zone, and Defender for Azure Cosmos DB when that plan is on. Dated example (2026-11-18, West US 3, list): $31.41 a month, $7.80 on the free tier, plus about $3.50 with Defender; a light-use month goes from $315.82 to at most $347.23, within the $375 ceiling. `destroy.ps1` deletes it with the resource group.
  - **Status, validation, and history:** `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T5`, with the last 15 minutes' peak normalized RU and 429s), `validate.ps1 -Area T5`, the Workload area, and preflight (`Microsoft.DocumentDB` registered). The session's T5 metrics are read from the operator's side, as T4's are, and passed to the live-activity notebook as `COSMOS_METRICS` (analysis `cosmosdb`, target `T5`), so `SM_DatabaseHub_History` has T5's peak normalized RU, requests, 429s, request units, provisioned throughput, and availability.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.CosmosHistory.Tests.ps1` (new), the runner's Cosmos DB tests (new), and updated cost, track, one-command, lifecycle, validation, estate, runbook, control, notebook, and model tests. Docs: the README, `AGENTS.md`, ADR 0027, the architecture, deployment, cost management, limitations, troubleshooting, permissions, the security model, cleanup, the walkthrough, the visuals guide, and the automation support matrix.
