- **Ask: the GitHub Copilot chat in the Insights app** (2026-11-18, [ADR 0028](docs/adr/0028-ask-copilot-in-the-insights-app.md); user-directed: "The GitHub Copilot chat interface right in the App is a must and a gamechanger"): a chat docked in the app, on each viewer's own GitHub Copilot, for conversational analytics on the history model and the live estate. Copilot's tools run in the page and only read: `run_dax` through the app's delegated connector, `show_chart` with the app's own charts, and `estate_overview` through a new app function. Every answer shows its DAX or source, with **Ask Copilot** and **Ask why** on the app's visuals, starters from the prompt pack, follow-ups, **Export**, and **New chat**. With T5 on, it covers Azure Cosmos DB too: T5's live availability and normalized RU, and its history from the latest capture, with **Was Cosmos DB throttled?** once T5's metrics are in the history.
  - **The relay** (`ask-relay/`): a Node.js service on the GitHub Copilot SDK that passes messages between the page and Copilot over a WebSocket; it holds no Azure or Fabric credential, allows only the app's origin, and scales to zero. `deploy.ps1` deploys it with the app (`infra/ask/ask-relay.bicep`, `Invoke-DemoAskRelaySetup`) and checks it (`ASK.RELAY`, `ASK.RELAY.HEALTH`, `ASK.SIGNIN`, `ASK.CSP`), each a **Pass**, a **Warning**, or **Not selected**, never blocking **DONE**; the status page has its own row. `start-demo.ps1` wakes it. `destroy.ps1` deletes it with the resource group.
  - **GitHub sign-in:** the device flow through the shared OAuth App **Database Hub Insights Ask** (client ID in `config/versions.json`, `askRelay.githubClientId`; `ask.githubClientId` overrides it), once per browser, renewed for up to six months, with no client secret anywhere.
  - **The live estate:** the app's function `estateOverview` reads 8 allowlisted read-only Database Hub routes as the app's owner, scoped to the demo's subscription and resource group, cached for 60 seconds, with each SQL Server instance's migration assessment in its inventory (which servers can move to Azure, and to what): a narrow exception to "documented interfaces only" ([ADR 0013's amendment](docs/adr/0013-agent-experience.md#amendment-2026-11-18-the-ga-skill-and-one-narrow-exception)). `useLiveEstate(view)` gives the app's pages the same source.
  - **The image:** `.github/workflows/ask-relay-image.yml` builds and publishes it to GitHub Container Registry, on a push to `main` or `ignite-updates` that changes the relay or the workflow, or by hand (never on a pull request, and never in a fork); `config/versions.json` pins it by digest (`askRelay.image`), and `ask.image` can name your own. The first publish from this public repository made the package public (2026-11-19), and the owner keeps it public and pinned: a public package can't be made private again, only deleted. A fork's or a private copy's package can start private, GitHub's default for a new package. When an image can't be used, `ASK.RELAY` is a **Warning** that says why (no image pinned, an image that doesn't pull without credentials, or a digest that isn't there), and **DONE** stays.
  - **Cost:** at most about $0.11 an hour, only while someone asks (2026-11-19 list prices); the default 5-hour session estimate goes from $15.03 to $15.57, and no ceiling's result changes ([cost management](docs/cost-management.md#dated-example-asks-chat-relay-2026-11-18)).
  - **The security review's fixes** (2026-11-19; it found no way for a viewer's GitHub token to leak): the relay reads request paths as text, so a request such as `GET //x:a` no longer stops it, and logs an unexpected error instead of exiting; a failed start closes its socket on both sides; limits key on the address the ingress appends to `X-Forwarded-For`, a client holds at most 4 sockets and runs 1 new Copilot check at a time, a socket must start within 10 seconds, and only started conversations count against the 20; the page renews the GitHub token before it's needed, one tab at a time, keeps the sign-in through a renewal that fails on the way, and follows other tabs; a sign-in survives a poll that fails on the way; and an account that can't use Copilot here can't send.
  - **Every turn ends with an answer, or says it has none** (2026-11-19, R925, release blocker; found live by the owner on `dbhubfn`, where a turn ended with its query rows and follow-ups and no answer, [ADR 0028's amendment](docs/adr/0028-ask-copilot-in-the-insights-app.md#amendment-2026-11-19-every-turn-ends-with-an-answer-or-says-it-has-none-r925)). At a turn's end, the relay reads the turn's messages back from the session's history and sends any text the page didn't get, once, before `idle`, and logs `chat.turn.end` with counts and lengths only. A turn that ends without text after its last query says "Copilot didn't write an answer" with **Retry**, never a silent finish. The guidance asks for the answer before `suggest_followups`, which reminds Copilot when it hasn't written one. Copilot never gets or shows HTTP codes or routes: when Database Hub's live view isn't available (the estate function's `unavailable`, R924), it gets one plain sentence, the conversation stops calling the live view, and Copilot answers from the history and points to Database Hub's own pages. An independent review's fixes (2026-11-19): a live read where every route failed, or that has no data, counts as no answer, only `unavailable` keeps the live view off for the conversation, and Copilot gets no route names even when it answers; a live call from the last conversation that ends after **New chat** changes nothing in the new one; and `finalTextChars` counts the answer as the dock does, the text after the last query. While Database Hub refuses the app as a whole (every live view `unavailable`, R924), a new conversation starts without the live view and the dock hides its live starters, until any view answers (the owner's question 2, the coordinator's decision). Its re-review's fixes: **New chat** while a session starts closes that session unused once it's ready, so the new conversation's first question starts its own and shows its rows; and the dock follows each later live read while it stays open (`liveOffNow` and `onLiveOffChange` in `src/data/live-estate.ts`). The relay's image built from 87af20c is pinned (`askRelay.image`). Tests in `ask-relay/test/bridge.test.ts` and `fabric/app/src/copilot/` (`copilot.test.tsx`, `tools.test.ts`, `guide.test.ts`).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.Ask.Tests.ps1`, the cost tests, `fabric/app/src/copilot/*.test.ts(x)`, `fabric/app/rayfin/functions/src/estate-core.test.ts`, and `ask-relay`'s own tests.
- **The Insights app opens on Mission Control** (2026-11-19, R927, [ADR 0030](docs/adr/0030-insights-app-mission-control.md); user-directed: "things the Database Hub standalone can't and visually different"): ContosoCare's service map with the latest session's plan as of its end, the "Did we catch it?" scoreboard (each injected fault's signal visible in the telemetry, its response, and its recovery), the business impact lens, a postmortem to download or draft with Copilot, a cinematic replay, and a kiosk, in Microsoft's colors (the owner's choice, 2026-11-20); frontend and read-only only.

#### Changed

- **An update leaves the demo as it found it** (2026-11-19, the coordinator's decision for the owner, provisional until the owner's review; [ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-19-an-update-leaves-the-demo-as-it-found-it-and-a-start-demops1-session-stops-by-itself)). `deploy.ps1` starts what's stopped so its steps reach the hosts, and left all of it running until the nightly stop: about $21 for an update that ended at 11:00 (list prices, West US 3, 2026-11-19). Now a run that ends **DONE** and found the demo stopped stops again what it started, through `stop-demo.ps1`'s stop without the history save or the capacity pause, after waiting at most 15 minutes for work that still needs the hosts (a bootstrap pass, T2's first migration assessment). A new deployment, a demo that was running, a session that uses it, and `-KeepRunning` keep it running, and **DONE** then says what it bills an hour, until when, and that `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` stops it now. `DEPLOY.ENDSTOP` and the status page's **Stop at the end** row say what it did; a stop that fails is a **Warning**, never **NOT DONE**. Since the review of 2026-11-19: a session that starts during the end's wait or its stop keeps the demo (it's read again after the wait, and the stop checks before its first change and before the VMs, `STOP.SESSION`); `start-demo.ps1` records its start for it (`DbHubStartDemoUtc`); Ctrl+C during the stop still ends the run with its DONE, report, and `out/deploy.jsonl`; and a stop that fails leaves tonight's nightly stop in place. Tests in `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.T4Replica.Tests.ps1`, and `Repo.LifecycleScripts.Tests.ps1`. R917.
- **A `start-demo.ps1` session stops by itself** (2026-11-19, the coordinator's decision for the owner, provisional until the owner's review; [ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md#amendment-2026-11-19-start-demops1-schedules-its-own-auto-stop)). A forgotten session billed about $3.11 an hour until the nightly stop. Now `start-demo.ps1` schedules the same auto-stop a Run all session uses, 4 hours after its start (`-Hours`, 0.5 to 24; running it again starts the hours over), and `START.CONTROL.AUTOSTOP` says when, on the console and the status page; it no longer cancels a pending auto-stop and leaves the VMs running. A later Run all, or the Insights app's **Start**, replaces it with its own plan; it waits for a `deploy.ps1` that's still running; `stop-demo.ps1` removes it. Without the session control's role, or until `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the control's runbooks, none is scheduled, and a **Warning** says the nightly stop is the limit, with the fix; a pending backup auto-stop is removed then too (review, 2026-11-19). `capture-t2-golden.ps1` says when it's this auto-stop that holds it back, with `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` first as the fix. Tests in `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.SafetyStop.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Golden.Tests.ps1`. R919.
- **T5's container exists only during a session** (2026-11-19, the coordinator's follow-up on T5's idle cost; the owner's condition that a session never runs without it; the owner approved the two role additions on 2026-11-19; [ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-t5-cosmos-db.md#the-container-only-during-a-session-2026-11-19)). An Azure Cosmos DB account can't be stopped, and a container's throughput can't be taken away, so T5 billed its 400 RU/s around the clock: $31.41 a month at list price in the internal subscriptions most of the demo's users have, which can't use the free tier. Now `start-demo.ps1` (`START.T5.CONTAINER`), Run all's session start, `demo-control.ps1` and the control's run action (`CONTROL.T5.CONTAINER`), and `deploy.ps1`'s T5 step (`DEPLOY.T5.CONTAINER`) create the container through Azure Resource Manager from one definition in `config/versions.json`, which `infra/modules/cosmos01.bicep` uses too; and `stop-demo.ps1` at its end, once T5's metrics were read for the history (`STOP.T5`, a **Pass** instead of **Not supported**), Stop now and the auto-stop, the nightly safety stop and its deferred pass, and the control's daily T4 pass delete it. The account, its database, private endpoint, DNS zone, and T1's role stay, so Database Hub keeps T5 in its estate, and between sessions T5 bills $7.80 a month, plus $0.032 an hour while the demo runs: in an internal subscription, T5's light-use month goes from $31.41 to $8.12, and the `lowest-cost` profile's from $96.16 to $72.87; on the free tier it stays $7.80 (2026-11-19 list prices, West US 3, 10 light-use hours). `estimate-cost.ps1 -T5ContainerLifecycle Session|AlwaysOn` prices either case; signed in, it reads the environment's own. Each session starts with an empty container; `healthy-baseline` writes its documents as it runs.
  - **Never a session without it:** the stops delete the container only when the demo resource group's `DbHubT5Container` tag is `Session`, which `deploy.ps1` sets right after the session control's setup when the control's role can create the container again (`DEPLOY.T5.LIFECYCLE`, and the status page's new row **Container only during a session**), and `grant-safety-stop.ps1` once an Owner granted it (`GRANT.T5.LIFECYCLE`). `stop-demo.ps1` checks the role again first and turns the tag back to `AlwaysOn` when the role can't any more. With `AlwaysOn` (an update by someone who isn't an Owner, whose session control keeps a role without the container's actions) or no tag (a demo deployed before), nothing deletes the container, and `T5.LIFECYCLE`, `STATUS.T5`, and `STOP.T5` say what it bills, with the fix: an Owner runs `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1` once, or `./scripts/deploy.ps1` for an older demo. On the free tier that's a **Pass**.
  - **The roles** (approved by the owner on 2026-11-19): "DbHub Demo Activity Operator", the session control's, gains `Microsoft.DocumentDB/databaseAccounts/sqlDatabases/containers/read`, `/write`, and `/delete`; "DbHub Demo Safety Stop" gains `containers/read` and `containers/delete` ([ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md), [permissions](docs/permissions.md)). Both stay at the demo resource group, where T5's account is the only Cosmos DB account, and neither reaches the account, its keys, its network, or its data. An Owner's `deploy.ps1` updates both definitions before any session relies on them, and `CONTROL.ROLE.DEFINITION` and `SAFETYSTOP.ROLE` say what they added.
  - **What the checks say:** `T5.CONTAINER` is a **Pass** for a missing container between sessions, a **Warning** for one that's missing while sqlvm01 runs or there while the demo is stopped, and a **Fail** only when nothing creates it for a session. `validate.ps1 -Area T5` adds `T5.LIFECYCLE`.
  - **A container a stop can't delete never makes the stop look failed** (the review of ab91ee5): the nightly safety stop, the control's daily T4 stop, and the auto-stop say T5's outcome in their result's own `t5Status`, so their status stays the VMs', T4's, and the replica's, which the cost check of T4's restart reads (R910). `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP` warns when the last nightly run couldn't read or delete the container, with `stop-demo.ps1` and the grant, and a role that lacks the container's actions says the container is kept, without claiming it bills when there's none. The nightly stop reads `sqlvm01` again before it deletes the container and leaves it while a session that started meanwhile runs. `SAFETYSTOP.ROLE` is a **Warning** that names what's missing when the shared role was written back by older code and your run can't write it.
  - Tests in `DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`, `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.SafetyStop.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Cost.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.EstimateCost.Tests.ps1`. R893 (mitigated), R910, R911.
- **A first deployment's cost check counts what the run sets up, and `monthlyCeilingUsd` defaults to 415 for a new configuration** (2026-11-19, the coordinator's decisions, approved by the owner, who chose the $415 default; [ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-19-a-first-deployments-worst-case-and-a-415-default-for-a-new-configuration), [ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md#amendment-a-first-deployment-counts-the-safety-stop-it-sets-up-2026-11-19), [ADR 0027](docs/adr/0027-t5-cosmos-db.md#2026-11-19-session-when-the-session-control-has-no-role-and-what-preflight-prices)). A new deployment of the example configuration ended **NOT DONE** at preflight, as an Owner too: its preflight runs before the nightly safety stop exists, so it counted T4's 7-day restart for the rest of the month, and T5's container all month, about $397 against $375.
  - **The nightly safety stop this run sets up counts:** preflight reads your own permissions, read-only, and when you can assign its role (an Owner), each restart counts as about a day: `PREFLIGHT.COST.T4RESTART` is a **Pass** that says so. When the setup then falls short, `DEPLOY.COST.T4RESTART` is a **Warning**, on the status page's Infrastructure row too, with the worst case without it, the ceiling, and the fix. A Contributor's first deployment counts the rest of the month, as before.
  - **T5's container is session-only when the session control has no role at all** (a Contributor's deployment): Run all can't start a session then, and `start-demo.ps1` creates the container with your own rights. Until an Owner's grant, only `stop-demo.ps1` deletes it then, unless the nightly safety stop's role has the container's actions, so it's priced all month, and `DEPLOY.T5.LIFECYCLE` is a **Warning** that says to run `stop-demo.ps1` after each session (the review of ab91ee5). A control that holds a role without the container's actions stays `AlwaysOn`. `DEPLOY.T5.LIFECYCLE` says which mode applies and why, with `grant-safety-stop.ps1` where it helps.
  - **Preflight prices T5 as the run will record it, and by what deletes it;** when the run ends priced all month after all, `DEPLOY.T5.LIFECYCLE` gives the month's worst case again, against the ceiling, and so do `estimate-cost.ps1` and `T5.LIFECYCLE` for what's recorded.
  - **`init.ps1` and `config/demo.config.example.json` write `monthlyCeilingUsd` 415** (it was 375), and so do the schema's and the template's defaults. A value already in a configuration never changes.
  - **The worst cases** (list prices in USD for West US 3, 2026-11-19, the subscription's Defender plans, private mode, 10 running hours): an Owner's first deployment of the example configuration $149.34 (+$265.66 against $415; $149.02 on the free tier), a Contributor's $398.23 (+$16.77; $374.62 and +$40.38 on the free tier), and the owner's own update $149.34 against its $250 (+$100.66), with Ask's chat relay for the 10 running hours and no Bastion hour (below); $397.34 for both before, with the Bastion hour, a **Fail** against $375. The owner chose $415 from the Contributor's $398.42, with the Bastion hour.
  - Tests in `DbHubDemo.SafetyStop.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Cost.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Config.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.EstimateCost.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Validation.Tests.ps1`. R912, R913.
- **The monthly cost check says the total it compares with the ceiling** (2026-11-19, the docs audit; [ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-19-the-monthly-check-says-its-total-and-no-bastion-hour-by-default)): `PREFLIGHT.COST.MONTHLY`, in preflight, `start-demo.ps1`'s cost guardrail, and `estimate-cost.ps1`, starts with the month's worst case, the ceiling, the margin under or over it, and what it counted: the light-use month, T4's restart hours and their cost, and T5's container, for the running hours (`Session`) or all month (`AlwaysOn`). `estimate-cost.ps1` prints the same in a table, "The monthly check". Tests in `DbHubDemo.Cost.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.EstimateCost.Tests.ps1`.
- **`estimate-cost.ps1` prices a new environment the way `deploy.ps1`'s preflight will** (2026-11-19, the final tests; [ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-19-the-monthly-check-says-its-total-and-no-bastion-hour-by-default)): an Owner who ran it before a first deployment saw about $368, T4's restart for the rest of the month, while preflight then checked $149.54. Signed in, it now prices an environment that doesn't exist yet with what that first run sets up with your rights, from the same functions and read-only probes (`Get-DemoDeployerRolePermission`, the new `Resolve-DemoT4RestartCoverage` that preflight uses too, `Get-DemoT5CostLifecycle -ThisRunDeploys`), and says what it assumed: T4's restart about a day each when you can assign the nightly safety stop's role, otherwise the rest of the month with the grant command, and T5's container for the running hours only or all month. An existing environment is priced by what's in place, as before. Tests in `DbHubDemo.EstimateCost.Tests.ps1`.
- **No Bastion hour in the estimate by default** (2026-11-19, the docs audit, S9): a default deployment and a default session have no Bastion, so `bastionHoursPerSession` in `config/profiles.json` is 0 (it was 1). `start-demo.ps1 -Interactive`, which deploys Bastion, counts it in its cost guardrail for up to `bastionMaxHours`, and `estimate-cost.ps1 -BastionHours` prices such a session. `lowest-cost` goes from $15.73 to $15.53 a session and from $73.95 to $73.76 a light-use month (list prices in USD for West US 3, 2026-11-19; the example configuration, Defender for Cloud not read). Tests in `DbHubDemo.Cost.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.EstimateCost.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`.
- **T5's baseline runs to the session's end** (2026-11-19, R896): Run all used to end T5's activity at its last storm's recovery, so T5 was idle for the rest of the session. A slot can now say it needs time to recover (`recoveryMinutes` in `scenarios/activity-profiles.json`, 0 to 30; all three T5 storm slots have 2): the runner skips it when it couldn't end that long before its profile's end, or when its window began before the profile was set, instead of clipping it. So T5's showcase profile runs the whole session like the other targets', and no storm is running when the session ends. The status page's time for T5's return to its baseline counts from when the Run Command sets its profile, at most 2 minutes after the dispatch (the review of ab91ee5). Tests in the runner's `ProfileSchedulerTests` and `CatalogTests`, and `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`.
- **The runner and T5's missing container** (2026-11-19): an activity on T5 while its container doesn't exist is refused with the reason ("it exists only during a session"), and the profile host tries again every 30 seconds within its window, so the baseline starts as soon as a session creates the container; `reset` and every storm cleanup with no container have nothing to clean (**Pass**); `status` says the container doesn't exist now, without an error. Tests in the runner's `CosmosRunnerTests` and `CosmosRunStoreTests`.
- **Database Hub is generally available** (2026-11-17, Microsoft Ignite; item 6): the README, limitations, walkthrough, presentation, architecture, references, notebooks, and checks say what's generally available (Database Hub, its real-time dashboards, the SSMS and VS Code entry points, bulk monitoring enablement, and the CLI skill) and what's still a preview (Copilot, Database Agents, alerts, SQL Server on Azure VM monitoring, and the telemetry endpoint), and that Database Hub's own views need no capacity. The walkthrough opens at **Overview** and shows **Optimization**, and the limitations no longer say the optimization insights look for quiet targets: that's the older utilization suggestions.
- **T3 at 2 vCores, and a $20 session ceiling** (2026-11-18, the owner's decision; [ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md)): Database Agents doesn't support one-vCore serverless databases, so `lowest-cost`'s T3 goes to 0.5–2 vCores, and `deploy.ps1` scales an existing T3 online (`DEPLOY.T3.SCALE`). `sessionCeilingUsd` defaults to 20; a configuration with exactly 15 gets 20, with a note. A 5-hour `lowest-cost` session comes to $15.03 (it was $12.42) at the 2026-11-18 list prices.
- **The docs pass** (2026-11-19, the owner's request: "Make sure all of the readme and directions and explainations and cost are all documented and optimized as well with the updates"). The README leads with a quick start (`az login`, `deploy.ps1`, what **DONE** lists, Run all, stop, update or delete), a second environment's one line (`init.ps1 -Prefix <prefix> -CreateAdminGroup`), what it costs, what stops by itself, what's deployed and by what, and what stays after `destroy.ps1`. `docs/cost-management.md` starts with what each part costs running and stopped, the sessions and months to quote, how long each part runs and what stops it, the month's worst case with the monthly check's two **Warning** cases, and what's not included (the Fabric capacity, licenses, each viewer's GitHub Copilot plan; the tenant's Defender for Cloud plans are outside the demo's control). The guides say each fact once and link to it: deployment 267 to 120 KB, troubleshooting 204 to 101 KB, the walkthrough 159 to 97 KB, the automation support matrix 110 to 38 KB, cost management 108 to 55 KB, keeping every heading other files link to and every check ID. DONE, the status page, `HUB.AGENT.T3`, and the fixes and help the scripts, the runbooks, and the notebooks print say what works today; the rule lines name the owner-approved exceptions to "Contributor is the minimum" ([implementation report](docs/implementation-report.md)).
- **The presenter's fallback agent uses the GA Database Hub skill** (2026-11-18, [ADR 0013's amendment](docs/adr/0013-agent-experience.md#amendment-2026-11-18-the-ga-skill-and-one-narrow-exception)): `databasehub-cli` from `microsoft/skills-for-fabric` (the `fabric-skills` plugin), pinned to a commit in `config/versions.json` (`agentPlugins`), replaces the preview `microsoft-sql-fdh` plugin. The `microsoft-sql` plugin stays for the diagnostics skills. The [agent guide](fabric/agent/README.md) has the install, the deny rules for the bundle's MCP servers, and how to remove `microsoft-sql-fdh`; the prompt pack's fallback prompts name the new skill. Since 2026-11-19, the walkthrough and the deployment and live-activity notebooks name it too, and the walkthrough and `session-setup.skills` start Copilot CLI with the guide's deny rules; the walkthrough's T1 row says what the skill's references document: SQL Server on Azure VMs are in its inventory but not in its CPU, storage, and memory health.

#### Fixed

- **`destroy.ps1` said an environment being deployed was "deployed by an earlier version of the demo"** (2026-11-19, the final tests' F15; `main` too). A destroy of `dbhubfx` ran while `dbhubfy` was being deployed in the same subscription, before its Control step recorded its Fabric workspaces, and rightly kept both workspaces, but said `rg-dbhubfy-demo` "was deployed by an earlier version of the demo, which doesn't record the workspaces it uses". Now the reason, in the list before the confirmation and in `DESTROY.FABRIC.WORKSPACE` and `DESTROY.FABRIC.CONTROL` (**Warning**), says "is being deployed and hasn't recorded its workspaces yet" while that environment's `deploy.ps1` busy marker is active, and "hasn't finished deploying and hasn't recorded its workspaces yet" when its recorded stage is before Validation; only otherwise an earlier version. What's kept, and the fix, are unchanged. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1`.
- **On a new environment, the status page's T5 row said Run all couldn't start a session while the Control row said Run all was ready** (live, 2026-11-19, F14; branch only). T5's container lifecycle was checked right after the session control's Azure part assigned its role, and a role assignment that was just created can be missing from a read for a while, so the row said the session control had no role yet. `deploy.ps1` now checks it again once the session control is done (with `-StopAfter Infrastructure` too, after its wait for the policy inventory), when this run's grant reports the role in place (`CONTROL.ROLE.A1`) and the first check didn't see it, and reads it up to 3 more times, 20 seconds apart, while it still doesn't show; the new `DEPLOY.T5.LIFECYCLE` replaces the first on the console, the status page, and in DONE. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`.
- **`destroy.ps1` said it kept a golden group that doesn't exist** (2026-11-19, found while destroying `dbhubask`; `main` too). Without `-IncludeGolden` it listed "destroy.ps1 keeps rg-`<prefix>`-golden and its golden snapshots", and `DESTROY.GOLDEN` said the group "stays with 0 golden snapshot(s)", when `az group exists` said it didn't exist. Now it reads that before its list of what goes and what stays: a group that doesn't exist is left out of what it keeps, a line says "rg-`<prefix>`-golden doesn't exist, so there's no golden snapshot.", and `DESTROY.GOLDEN` is a **Pass** that says so, as with `-IncludeGolden`; a group that exists, or whose read fails, keeps the wording it had. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Teardown.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1`.
- **A resumed run's DONE dropped what the earlier run found** (2026-11-19, the final tests' F10; branch only). On `dbhubfn`, the first run ended **NOT DONE** at T2's cleanup, and the plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1` that resumed it ended **DONE** without `HUB.AGENT.T3` under **Please confirm** (selecting T3 for Database Agents, the demo's one manual step) and without the Insights app, whose steps it didn't run again. Now a run whose T1 track an earlier run finished still lists `HUB.AGENT.T3`, and each run's ending saves what a resume skips (the Insights app's and Ask's results, the Hub tenant setting's, the presenters', the Fabric capacity's, the history report's data check, and anything else only a person confirms) in `out/deploy-carry-<resource group>.json`. The next run of the same deployment shows those it didn't check again, in **DONE**, **NOT DONE**, and on the status page, with one line that says from which run; `DEPLOY.CARRIED` lists them. A fresh result always wins, a carried one never changes whether a run is **DONE**, and after `configure-fabric-app.ps1` runs on its own the app's earlier state isn't shown. Tests in `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`.
- **A first deployment could end NOT DONE at T2's sign-in data check after the Arc onboarding, and a rerun didn't check again** (2026-11-19, the final tests' `dbhubfn`, a net-new deployment at 33a1bb6; R923). Right after the onboarding, `deploy.ps1`'s check (the Run Command `DbHubDemo-SignInData`) tried to connect to T2's guest over PowerShell Direct while `archost01`'s own sign-in cleanup, which the onboarding's marker starts, checked it. Its guest part failed to connect, `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING` said only "arcsql01 (guest) couldn't be reached (Running)", no recovery ran, and NOT DONE said to run `./scripts/check-sign-in-data.ps1 -Clean` until it passes, then `./scripts/configure-arc.ps1`, then `./scripts/deploy.ps1`; a plain rerun passed without checking again, because the host was past onboarding. Now, with nothing changed for an update whose onboarding passed before (tests in `Vm.SignInResidue.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.SignInData.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Arc.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`):
  - the check waits for the host's bootstrap pass and holds its lock (`bootstrap.lock`, at most 30 minutes; 20 for `check-sign-in-data.ps1` and the golden capture and test), so the two never sweep the guest at the same time; a lock that isn't free in time sweeps nothing and fails closed;
  - a guest part that fails while the guest runs runs again, up to three more times after pauses of 10, 30, and 60 seconds, with #65's bounded recovery first: one restart of the guest for the run when its channel stays broken (`DEPLOY.T2.GUESTRESTART`; otherwise `DEPLOY.T2.GUESTRETRY` when a later try got through), never counting a guest it didn't reach as clean; a file that the host's cleanup deleted meanwhile no longer fails it;
  - what failed shows: PowerShell Direct's own error and what the recovery did, in `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING` and on the status page's "Sign-in data cleanup" row;
  - the onboarding records on the resource group that its check is owed (`DbHubArcSweepUtc`) until it passes, so NOT DONE's one next step is `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, which runs the cleanup and its check again by itself, whatever the host's state. `check-sign-in-data.ps1` stays the manual tool;
  - since the review of e617f34: the record is only for the nested T2 host. The evaluation-only VM turns its Azure guest agent off after its onboarding, so a check owed there could never run, and every later run would fail; nothing is recorded or checked again there, its own `SignInCleanup` step checks first, and a failed check right after its onboarding names `./scripts/check-sign-in-data.ps1 -VmName arcsql01 -Clean -Force` while that agent still runs. And the onboarding's probe, the onboarding, and its check share one guest restart outside the default parallel path too: `configure-arc.ps1` gives them one key of its own, and `deploy.ps1 -Sequential` a run key, as the parallel path does (tests in `DbHubDemo.Arc.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`);
  - since the live proof on the final tests' `dbhubfx` (net-new at 6787cd4, NOT DONE one step earlier): T2's Arc probe ran inside a pass of `archost01`'s own bootstrap, found `arcsql01` unreachable though it answered a moment later, and gave up; the onboarding never started, configuration went on against a machine that didn't exist, and NOT DONE led with `T2.ARC.SQLEXTENSION` and said to run `configure-arc.ps1`, then `deploy.ps1`. Now the Arc onboarding's own probe, onboarding, and marker hold the host bootstrap's lock too (at most 30 minutes, fail closed; the task the marker starts starts after the release); a probe that finds the guest unreachable, and an onboarding that stopped because the guest didn't answer before it changed anything, try again after pauses of 10, 30, and 60 seconds (`DEPLOY.T2.GUESTRETRY` when it then answers), with #65's one restart for a broken channel; and a guest that still doesn't answer is a **Fail** of `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING` with PowerShell Direct's error, configuration waits, and NOT DONE leads with it, its one Next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` (F12, F13). An Arc machine that's already Connected keeps a **Warning**, and its configuration goes on. T2's relay, which sets T2's activity profile and runs Run all's T2 steps, holds the same lock while its command runs in the guest, for at most 2 minutes, and then runs the command as before (tests in `Vm.ArcOnboard.Tests.ps1`, `Vm.WorkloadSteps.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Arc.Tests.ps1`).
- **`check-sign-in-data.ps1 -Clean` waited on its question where no one could answer** (2026-11-19, the final tests' F9: 7.5 minutes with nothing on a launcher's screen). Where no one can answer a prompt (CI, redirected input, a launcher, or an assistant), `-Clean` without `-Force` now stops at once, checking and changing nothing, as `init.ps1` does: `SECURITY.SIGNIN.CONFIRM`, **Manual action required**, gives the same command with `-Force`. Tests in `DbHubDemo.SignInData.Tests.ps1`.
- **A Contributor's update from `main` stopped at the monthly ceiling, and so did its `start-demo.ps1`** (2026-11-19, the re-review of 0590bbe, Medium; branch only; the coordinator's decision, which the owner can reverse; [ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-19-a-warning-when-only-t5s-container-priced-all-month-puts-the-month-over)). Where the session control holds no role and the nightly safety stop can't delete T5's container, only `stop-demo.ps1` deletes it, so since the review of ab91ee5 the estimate prices it all month: $398.23 for the example configuration, over the 375 or 390 that a configuration from `main` keeps, so preflight failed and `start-demo.ps1` started nothing. Now, when only that puts the month over `monthlyCeilingUsd`, and the month fits with the container for the running hours, `PREFLIGHT.COST.MONTHLY` is a **Warning**, never a **Fail**, in preflight and in `start-demo.ps1`'s guardrail: it names the worst case and the ceiling, what the container adds (about $26.74 a month), why, and three fixes: an Owner's `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1` once (the worst case falls to about $149), a higher `monthlyCeilingUsd`, or `t5Enabled` false; and `stop-demo.ps1` after each session until then. The status page's Preflight row shows it. The configured value and the budget's amount never change, and a month over the ceiling for any other reason still fails (R920). Tests in `DbHubDemo.Cost.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.EstimateCost.Tests.ps1`.
- **The re-review's smaller fixes** (2026-11-19, the re-review of 0590bbe, Low, and the docs audit; branch only):
  - `DEPLOY.T5.LIFECYCLE`'s worst case after the run started from preflight's, which caps T4's restart, even when `DEPLOY.COST.T4RESTART` said the safety stop preflight counted wasn't in place: about $176 instead of $398.22. It starts from the worst case without the safety stop then, and says the same Warning and fixes as `PREFLIGHT.COST.MONTHLY` when the same rule holds.
  - DONE's note for a nightly safety stop whose role is assigned but was written back by older code (R910) said it "can't stop anything until its role is assigned". It now says it still stops the VMs, stops T4, and reads T3, but can't delete T5's container, with the fix.
  - `validate.ps1 -Area Security -ScanPath ./out/venv`, the fix that `SECURITY.SECRETSCAN.OUTPUT`'s Warning names, read the folder but kept the Warning. A folder named in `-ScanPath`, and the dependency trees inside it, are no longer counted as left out.
  - A stop from runbooks published before `t5Status` that only T5's container made `Partial` (the nightly safety stop, the auto-stop, or the control's daily T4 job) voided T4's coverage, so such an environment's preflight could refuse the update that republishes them, every night again. It counts as a stop of T4 now, and `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP` names T5's container instead (R921).
  - The cost check of T4's restart skipped the control's daily T4 stop when `control.enabled` read false, with a fix that said to set it, which `deploy.ps1` ignores (`DEPLOY.CONTROL.ON`). The setting has read true for every configuration since 2026-11-14, so that branch couldn't run; it's gone, and only the control's state decides.
  - Tests in `DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Cost.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.SafetyStop.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.SecretScan.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Validation.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.T4.Tests.ps1`.
- **The Insights app's live views showed an empty estate and "401" when Database Hub refused the app** (2026-11-19, live on `dbhubfn`; R924; branch only). Database Hub answers every route HTTP 401 "Authentication failed for all authenticators" to the token Fabric gives the app's functions, while it accepts a person's own sign-in, so the app's pages read an empty live estate, its **Ready to migrate** card said T2 had no assessment, and Ask's live tool reported 401s. Now, when no route answers, the estate function's answer carries no data, and, when Database Hub refused the app's sign-in on every route, one plain sentence (`unavailable`, "Database Hub's live view isn't available to the app: Database Hub refuses the app's own sign-in"), never a route's error, which stays in the function's log. While every view is refused, the pages show no live parts, and **Live views** says in one line that Database Hub's live data isn't available to Fabric apps yet (the coordinator's decision for the owner; remembered in the browser, so a page doesn't show its live parts reading and then drop them). A failure of another kind says "didn't answer the app just now". A view that comes back without data while another answers shows as partial: its tile says it wasn't read, never a zero or a green **Pass**, and its routes count among those that didn't answer (the review of 069e65f). The security count is partial until both of its views have answered, the warnings and the resources' own checks, on Overview and Findings alike (the re-review of 9625352). The function still asks Database Hub on every read, so the live views come back by themselves once it accepts that sign-in. T2's readiness for each Azure SQL target, with the recommended tier and size, comes from its SQL Server instance's documented migration assessment (`properties.migration.assessment`), which `configure-fabric-app.ps1` reads at every deploy into `dbhub-app.json`; the card shows it with that time when Database Hub isn't read live. The new `FABRIC.APP.ESTATE` is **Not supported**, never a Fail, and names what works: the saved history and T2's readiness in the app, and the live estate in Database Hub. Tests in `fabric/app/src/lib/app.test.ts`, `fabric/app/src/pages/pages.test.tsx`, and `DbHubDemo.FabricApp.Tests.ps1`.
- **A new environment's Insights app copied every Manage request for Run all** (2026-11-19, the final tests' net-new run, `dbhubfn`; R926). `configure-fabric-app.ps1` reads `NB_DatabaseHub_Control`'s IDs before its first `rayfin up`, and `deploy.ps1` put the notebook in Fabric only after the app's step, on both paths, so a new environment's first run, **DONE** or not, left Manage in copy mode with "Manage's function is missing NB_DatabaseHub_Control" (`FABRIC.APP.ACTIONS`), and a resume that skipped the finished Fabric step kept it so. Now the parallel path runs the app's step once more right after the control's Fabric part, when the app's Manage doesn't have the notebook, by this run's records or, on a resume, the state's new `DbHubAppControlNotebook` record; `-Sequential` puts the notebook in Fabric before the app; and the app's own second deploy, for its API, runs only when it completes Manage's settings, so a first deployment still runs `rayfin up` twice. An environment deployed before then gets it on its next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.FabricTrack.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.FabricApp.Tests.ps1`.
- **A second environment with an explicit `-Prefix` got the first one's Fabric workspace** (the final tests' F5, 2026-11-19; `main` too). The README's directions for another prefix, `./scripts/init.ps1 -Prefix <prefix>`, wrote `fabric.workspaceName` "Database Hub Demo", the default prefix's environment's, with `INIT.FABRIC.WORKSPACE` **Pass**, so the next `deploy.ps1` would have put the new environment's lakehouse, history model, report, notebooks, and Insights app in the other's workspace, and `DEPLOY.FABRIC.SHARED` warned only afterwards. Now a new environment gets "Database Hub Demo `<prefix>`" for every prefix but the default, whether `-Prefix` named it or the run picked it, and its control's workspace follows that name; the default prefix's first environment keeps "Database Hub Demo". `INIT.FABRIC.WORKSPACE` is never a **Pass** for a workspace that another demo environment records (one Azure Resource Graph query): a default name gives way to the environment's own (a **Warning** that says so), a `-WorkspaceName` that names one is **Manual action required** with the environment and the fix, and a Resource Graph that can't answer is a **Warning**. `deploy.ps1` stops before it creates anything when the workspace its configuration names is one that another environment records and it doesn't, a configuration edited by hand included: `DEPLOY.FABRIC.SHARED` is a **Fail** in preflight, and in a resumed run before any track, on the console and the status page, with the fix (a `fabric.workspaceName` of its own, or `./scripts/init.ps1 -Force -Prefix <prefix> -WorkspaceName`). An environment that records its workspace keeps it, and so does one deployed before 2026-11-17 that used Fabric, even when another environment records it too: a **Warning** with how to move, never a **Fail**, so its update goes on. Tests in `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R916.
- **A fix that `init.ps1` printed for another prefix wrote the default prefix's configuration** (the final tests' F7, live at 33a1bb6, and F6; 2026-11-19; `main` too). `./scripts/init.ps1 -Prefix dbhubfn` stopped at `INIT.FABRIC.CAPACITY` (three capacities, in a terminal that couldn't take input), and its fix, "Run init.ps1 -Force -CapacityName `<name>`", followed as written, wrote the configuration of `rg-dbhub-demo`, the owner's, which the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` would have updated. Now every command `init.ps1` prints, in a result, a fix, a next step, or its log, names the run's `-Prefix` when it isn't the default and its `-ConfigPath` when it isn't `config/demo.config.json`, and so do `deploy.ps1`'s first-run resume line and **WHAT IF** ending. And `init.ps1 -Force` without `-Prefix` keeps the prefix and environment of the configuration it overwrites, as if `-Prefix` named them, so following any command never moves a file to another environment: `INIT.CONFIG` says so, an environment that isn't yours then writes nothing, and an explicit `-Prefix` still wins. The fix's "or without -NonInteractive to choose", which nobody had passed, says "in a terminal that can take input" instead (F6). Tests in `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`. R916.
- **A Contributor who deployed still couldn't Run all after an Owner ran `grant-safety-stop.ps1`** (2026-11-19, the docs audit): its A3 step gave Key Vault Secrets User on the control vault's three secrets only to the Owner and `control.notebookUserObjectIds`, never to the environment's deployer, whom `deploy.ps1` can't give it without Owner rights. The Owner's one run now gives them to the deployer too, with nothing to edit: the person whose `deploy.ps1` last set up the control vault, as Azure records it on the vault (`systemData.lastModifiedBy`), resolved to one user through Microsoft Graph. `CONTROL.ROLE.DEPLOYER` names them; a GitHub workflow's deployment or a name that doesn't resolve gives nobody, with the fix (R915). Tests in `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`.
- **`destroy.ps1` kept the demo's Fabric workspace of an environment deployed since 2026-11-18** (live, 2026-11-19, `dbhubc6`): "it holds what the demo didn't create: UserDataFunction APP_DatabaseHub_Insights". The Insights app's deployment makes that user data function, for its functions, beside the app and its connector's, and destroy's list of the demo's items didn't have it. The list now takes what the app's backend makes from `config/versions.json` (`fabricApp.backendItems`), and a test holds it to the app's `rayfin.yml`; a first run that recovers an environment counts such a workspace as the demo's too. Run `./scripts/destroy.ps1` again to delete a workspace an older destroy kept (R908). Tests in `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`.
- **The Insights app's first deploy after a destroy would reuse the deleted app** (2026-11-19; `main` too): the Rayfin CLI finds its record in `out/fabric-app/rayfin/.deployments.json` by the workspace's name and reuses its item ID without checking the workspace, so a new workspace with a destroyed one's name got the deleted app's ID, and `rayfin up` would fail. The stage now drops the records of other workspaces before `rayfin up`, and `FABRIC.APP.STAGE` names them (R909). Tests in `DbHubDemo.FabricApp.Tests.ps1`.
- **A failure in T5 alone ended an update NOT DONE, and a resume couldn't get past it** (review of T5, 2026-11-19, Medium). A region that refuses new Azure Cosmos DB accounts, or a Cosmos DB deny that preflight reported only as a Warning, failed the whole template, so the runner, the bootstraps, and Fabric weren't updated; while the account was missing, `DEPLOY.T5.MISSING` turned every resume into a full rerun that failed the same way, and no message mentioned `t5Enabled`. Now, when the run creates T5's account and every failed operation is T5's (`Get-DemoT5OnlyFailure`: its Cosmos DB resources, its nested deployments, its private endpoint and DNS zone; or a policy's refusal naming only T5's resources), the template is deployed again once without T5, and the rest of the run goes on without it and can end **DONE**. `DEPLOY.T5`, a **Warning**, gives Azure's reason and the fix (`t5Enabled` false, or another region), on the console, the Infrastructure step, and T5's rows on the status page. The resource group records it (`DbHubT5Refused`), so a resume leaves T5 out too instead of starting from preflight; the next run that deploys the template tries T5 again, and clears the record once it's created. An account that existed before the run is never left out, and its `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE` fix now says how to deploy without T5. The template's attempts are counted again too: 8, or up to 10 with `t4SkuName`. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T4Fallback.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`.
- **`deploy.ps1 -Sequential` skipped T5's checks when `t4Enabled` was false** (review of T5, 2026-11-19, Low). The sequential T4 stage, which covers T5 too, ended at T4's "off" before T5's account checks and warmup. T5's part now runs first. A test in `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`.
- **`t5Enabled` false didn't get past a policy that denies Azure Cosmos DB** (review of T5, 2026-11-19, High). The schema says to set it false for exactly that, but preflight's `PREFLIGHT.POLICY.RESOURCETYPES` always checked T5's four `Microsoft.DocumentDB` types, so the run still failed preflight and deployed nothing. Now the policy checks take the configuration's types (`Get-DemoResourceTypeList`): with T5 off, T5's types aren't checked, a rule for Cosmos DB alone no longer counts as one that applies, and the policy cache is keyed on it. With T5 on, a policy that denies only T5's types is still a **Fail**, whose fix now says to set `"t5Enabled": false`; when it denies others too, the fix says that leaves T5's out. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Policy.Tests.ps1`.
- **Azure refusing T5's free tier could move T3 off the Azure SQL free offer** (review of T5, 2026-11-19, High). T3's free-offer check ran first and took any "free tier" for its own, which matches both of Azure Cosmos DB's refusals ("Free tier is not supported for Internal subscriptions" and "Free tier has already been applied to another Azure Cosmos DB account in this subscription"). So when Resource Graph missed the account that held the free tier, another deployment took it first, or the subscription's `quotaId` couldn't be read, `deploy.ps1` turned `t3UseFreeLimit` off with a `T3.FREEOFFER` Warning that blamed Azure SQL: a new T3 was created paid, and an existing free T3 moved to paid for good, or the update failed. Now T5's refusal is checked first and counts only in Azure Cosmos DB's own error detail, and T3's check ignores Cosmos DB's details and no longer takes "free tier" for its offer. Unit tests only, with Azure's exact texts and Azure SQL's real refusal, because every subscription the live tests use is internal, where T5 never asks for the free tier (ADR 0027): `DbHubDemo.T4Fallback.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.T3.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.T5.Tests.ps1`.
- **`validate.ps1`'s Security area spent 37 minutes in the Insights app's packages** (live, 2026-11-18, after a deploy that built the app): `SECURITY.SECRETSCAN.OUTPUT` read every file under `out/`, `out/fabric-app/node_modules` included (114,315 files). `Find-DemoSecretLeak` now walks one folder at a time and never enters a dependency tree or package cache, at any depth (`node_modules`, `bower_components`, `jspm_packages`, `.npm`, `.pnpm-store`, `.yarn`, `.nuget`, `__pycache__`, `.venv`, `venv`, `site-packages`, `.tox`, `.gradle`, `.m2`), `.git`, or a linked folder; everything that can hold the repository's or your secrets is still read, the app's `dist` and `rayfin/.env`, logs, transcripts, and configuration included. The result's evidence lists what was left out. A synthetic `out/` with 20,000 package files: 37.9 seconds before, 0.04 after. Since the review of ab91ee5, only `out/fabric-app/node_modules`, the Insights app's build, is left out silently: any other folder it leaves out, such as `out/venv`, makes `SECURITY.SECRETSCAN.OUTPUT` a **Warning** that names it, with `./scripts/validate.ps1 -Area Security -ScanPath <the folder>` to read it. Tests in `DbHubDemo.SecretScan.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Validation.Tests.ps1`.
- **A stopped T4 made `validate.ps1` fail after every session** (live, 2026-11-18): `T4.DATABASE` was a **Fail** ("ServerStoppedError"), and `T4.ENHANCEDMETRICS` called the metrics off, because a stopped flexible server doesn't answer for its databases and parameters. Now `T4.ADMIN`, `T4.DATABASE`, `T4.ENHANCEDMETRICS`, and `T4.MAXCONNECTIONS` on a T4 that isn't Ready (stopped, starting, ...), or whose read answers that it's stopped, are **Warnings** that say so, with how it comes back (`./scripts/start-demo.ps1` or Run all, then `validate.ps1 -Area T4`), as T3 paused and `METRICS.T5` on a deallocated VM are. A running T4 whose database is missing is still a **Fail**. T4's read replica: one that's still there while T4 is stopped or starting, or that's being created or deleted, is a **Warning** with the reason, and a missing one is never a **Fail**. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T4.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.T4Replica.Tests.ps1`.
- **A session's planned times came early on a VM that had just started** (2026-11-19, live on the agent loop's test environment; `main` too, since the session control's guide, 2026-11-14). The control notebook counts each target's beats from `dispatchUtc`, which the runbook stamps when Azure accepts the target's dispatch Run Command, but the runner counts each target's showcase from when `profile set` ran on the VM: 2 minutes 41 seconds later on a sqlvm01 that had just started. So the guide's times for T1, T3, T4, and T5 came that much early, "Now: T3 Respond" printed while T3's CPU was still at 99%, and the `database_agent` finding's window after the response held part of the regression (99% to 37%, where T3's CPU fell to about 5%); a slower start could have turned it into a false **Warning**. Now the dispatch script writes each target's set time on the VM in a `DBHUB-SET` line, and the session's host check moves `dispatchUtc` to it before the session's start writes `running`, so the guide, the live view, and the history count from the runner's own anchor ([ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#2026-11-19-amendment-each-targets-showcase-counts-from-when-its-runner-set-it)). A Run Command still running then (T2's, after a cold start) keeps the accepted time, as before. The next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the runbooks. Tests: `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`.
- **An upgraded T3 cut the Database Agent beat to 20 minutes** (2026-11-18, the independent review of `935eeed..4c7817a`, high; branch only). `database/common/110-demo-control.sql`'s setup only lowered `demo.scenario_control`'s limits, so every T3 set up before the beat kept `plan-regression` at 1200 seconds, and `demo.usp_run_begin` ended the 30-minute beat at 20: the response came about when the agent would first raise its issue, and the notebooks' times and CPU windows fell after it. Setup now raises only that old default to 1800; a limit an operator lowered stays. The next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` applies it. Tests: `SqlScriptTests` and the opt-in `SqlIntegrationTests` over an existing 1200 row.
- **A migration assessment problem ended T2's whole track** (2026-11-18, the review, medium; branch only). `T2.ARC.MIGRATIONASSESSMENT` could be a **Fail**, which skipped T2's monitoring, its host's wait, and its profile, so the run ended **NOT DONE** over an optional, free feature, and `validate.ps1` repeated it. Now anything about turning it on, starting it, or reading it is a **Warning** with the fix, outside the track's failure check and **DONE**. The setting is read again for up to 3 minutes after its update, which Azure accepts before it applies (`az rest` doesn't wait), and a start that fails is tried again every 2 minutes for up to 10 once T2's host is ready: live on 2026-11-18, the first start got HTTP 500 for about 40 minutes, until the Azure extension for SQL Server picked the setting up. Tests: `DbHubDemo.Arc.Tests.ps1` (a policy's deny, the update's wait, a setting that stays off, the retry) and `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`.
- **`stop-demo.ps1` could turn T3's `FORCE_LAST_GOOD_PLAN` back on after you turned it off** (2026-11-18, the review, low; branch only). It set any desired off back to Default through Azure Resource Manager while T3 was awake, a beat's or not, and said a beat had held it, while the other paths restore what the hold recorded. Now, before it stops T1, it releases the hold through T3 itself (`demo.usp_release_auto_tuning_hold`), which restores only what a demo hold recorded, and `STOP.T3.AUTOTUNING` only reads the option. A session cut by the stop without that release is put back at T3's next run, by reset, or by `deploy.ps1`. Tests: `DbHubDemo.T3Agent.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`.
- **One correlation finding per session beat** (2026-11-18): `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` keyed each `correlation` finding by the first 12 characters of its request ID, which every beat of a session shares (`session:` and 4 characters of its ID), so a session kept one finding per target. The key is now the whole request ID.
- **An update still started T2's PowerShell Direct steps while the host checked every step again** ([#65](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/65) follow-up, 2026-11-19). The host's bootstrap writes `health.json` only when a step converges or when its pass ends, so for the minutes an update's new generation checked every step, the host's health still said the pass before's `Ready`, and `deploy.ps1` went on with Arc onboarding and T2's activity profile during that pass; a golden copy's Arc onboarding (`-Force`) didn't read the host's state at all. Now the health read reports a generation that the loader started on the T2 host and whose pass hasn't reported yet as `InProgress`, with the pass's step and `generationPending`, so every wait for the host goes on until that pass reports (the wait for T2's host reads it every minute meanwhile, and says so), and Arc onboarding with `-Force` waits until that pass ends, while the host reports a pending generation, `InProgress`, `RebootPending`, or `NotStarted`, for up to 20 minutes, then goes ahead with the recovery above (from the follow-up's review: it first stopped waiting at the pass's first converging step, which on an existing golden copy is the workload runner's install in the guest). T1's health read is unchanged. With it, from the review of #65's fix: an interrupted restart isn't finished by restarting a guest that still runs its old boot while a golden capture is in progress on the host, and a running guest whose uptime rounds to 0 counts as restarted. Tests in `Vm.WorkloadSteps.Tests.ps1`, `Vm.ArcOnboard.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Arc.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.GuestRestart.Tests.ps1`. R902.
- **A first deployment on a busy Fabric capacity ended NOT DONE** ([#68](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/68); live, 2026-11-19, a new deployment from `main` on a shared F4). The Fabric track runs `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` once to create the history tables, and waited 10 minutes for it; the capacity kept it queued behind other Spark jobs for about 15, so the history model refreshed with no `scenario_runs` table (0xC14700C7), `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.REFRESH` failed, and a second run was needed. Now that first run is waited for up to 45 minutes, and the status page's Fabric row says it's queued or running, with the minutes so far and the bound; then the model refreshes. A run still queued then is followed for up to 45 more minutes, and the model's step runs again once it completes, before the Insights app; the refresh's **Warning** says so, and the first data check's **Not selected** `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.DATA`, from before the model had tables, is dropped (from its review). A run that fails, or never completes, is a **Warning** that says what fills the model, never a **Fail**: the history snapshot at the end of the run (`DEPLOY.FABRIC.HISTORY`) runs it again and refreshes the model. An update doesn't run that first run, so it doesn't wait, and a refresh that fails for another reason stays a **Fail**. Tests in `DbHubDemo.FabricTrack.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Fabric.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`. R903.
- **A session's history could be saved twice after a cancel cut its save off** (2026-11-19; R907, from the review of the Insights app's **Manage**). The app's **Stop now** cancels the control's run, which may be saving a session's history, and then saves that session again, as the next Run all's pre-check does for an earlier session. `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` appended each table under a run ID of its own and wrote the session's `scenario_runs` row last, so a run cut off after some tables left their rows, and the next run saved them again: telemetry and findings twice, which the history model's **Findings** and **Notebook Runs Captured** counted, and, because those saves pass no session plan, each of the session's beats once more as a detected incident window beside its recorded one (**Incidents**). Now every run of one session saves under one run ID, `session-<id>`, and writes each table, and merges the incident windows, only when no earlier run of the session has: the next run completes the save table by table, the session's rows still last, and keeps what the earlier run saved, its beat and `database_agent` findings included. Running the persist cell again in the same run replaces that run's rows in every table, `scenario_runs` too. An analysis without a session is unchanged. Tests in `Fabric.Notebooks.Tests.ps1`, which run the notebook's cells against a lakehouse in memory (Python 3 with pandas).
- **A session's history that a cancel cut off was never completed with its beats, and every later Run all analyzed it again** (2026-11-19; R918, a follow-up of R907). The Insights app's **Stop now** cancels the control's run, which may be saving a session's history, and then saves that session again, as the next Run all's pre-check does for an earlier session. Those saves passed no session plan, because the session's stop replaced its status with one that kept none. So a save cut off before its `scenario_runs` rows never got the session's row or its beats' rows, nor, when it was cut off before its findings, its beats' correlation and its `database_agent` finding; and with no row saying the session was saved, every later Run all and Stop now analyzed it again for 7 days, about a minute of capacity each time. Now the runbooks' stop keeps the session's plan in its status (its window, its targets' dispatch times, T4's tier, the replica-lag beat, and T5's end, from the status copy that `DbHub-Monitor` reads; `planKept` says whether it found it), and both saves rebuild the session's beats from it, as the session's end does: `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` completes the save table by table, the session's row last, and the next Run all finds it saved. A status without the plan (a stop by older runbooks, a stop that didn't find the session's status, or one that a later session's status replaced) gets the session's window from its metrics, with no beats: the history is saved without its beats, as before, but its row says it's saved, so it's analyzed at most once more, and a **Warning** says so once, with `./scripts/deploy.ps1` as the fix for older runbooks; for a history job, whose answer the notebook doesn't read, it says "unless it's saved already". A session stopped during its start, before it had an end, is saved as its end would save it: no beats, and its window ends at its stop. With the plan in the status, Stop now also saves a session whose metrics the vault doesn't have, without T4's and T5's metrics, instead of printing how to save it by hand; and a session's end with no beats is recorded as saved too. `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the runbooks and the notebook. Tests in `Fabric.ControlNotebook.Tests.ps1`, one running the control notebook's Run alls through to the activity notebook's cells against a lakehouse in memory (Python 3 with pandas), and `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`.
- **After `destroy.ps1`, every Save in Database agent settings failed, and nothing said why** (live, 2026-11-20; branch only). The owner's **Save** in Database Hub's **Manage agents** > **Database agent settings** failed with "Database agent changes couldn't be saved. Your changes couldn't be applied. Review the selected databases and their settings, then try again." Its list showed 19 SQL databases, all selected, several of them rows named just ContosoCare, with no server; only 3 of those ContosoCare databases still existed, and the others were T3s that earlier destroys had deleted. A **Save** validates the agent and your Azure write permission for every selected database and fails as a whole when any fails ([enable Database Agents](https://learn.microsoft.com/fabric/database/hub/enable-database-agents), [limitations](https://learn.microsoft.com/fabric/database/hub/database-agents-known-limitations#an-agent-cant-be-saved-or-doesnt-become-active)), the selection is the tenant's, and no API removes a database from it. Messages only: nothing is deleted or changed differently.
  - `destroy.ps1` lists T3's selection among what it keeps, named from the group's `deploymentId` tag before the confirmation; a line before it asks says to uncheck only `azuresql01-<deploymentId>/ContosoCare` there and **Save**, before or after the run, and why, with Learn's link; and once T3 is gone, `DESTROY.HUB.AGENT.T3`, a **Manual action required** that the exit code doesn't count, gives the link and the steps, named from the manifest on a run after the group is gone too. It never says to clear the selection: among look-alike rows, after the run, uncheck one at a time and **Save** until a **Save** succeeds, checking each row again whose **Save** still fails. The golden boot test's group (`purpose` `t2-golden-boot-test`, which `test-t2-golden.ps1` deletes through `destroy.ps1`'s path) has a `deploymentId` tag but never a T3, so it gets neither the line nor `DESTROY.HUB.AGENT.T3` (the review of 5d1f4b0).
  - **Two cases** (the coordinator's decision, 2026-11-20, while the owner is away): the selection is saved for the tenant, and every database starts selected (the panel says "All databases are included by default", with 19 of 19 selected on the owner's screen). Where only you use Database Agents, the steps reset the selection (clear it, then **Save** with nothing selected), then check T3, or, with several `ContosoCare` rows, one at a time with a **Save** each, keeping each that saves. Where others use it too, they leave others' databases selected and add T3; when **Save** fails on one of theirs, such as one you can't write to, ask whoever manages Database Agents in the tenant to add T3, rather than removing theirs.
  - `HUB.AGENT.T3`, on **DONE** and the status page, says first "Required once for the Database Agent beat; everything else works without it" (the owner's request), then both cases, the Agent policy, how to tell T3's row from other environments' ContosoCare rows, that saving works with T3 paused while its issues come only while it's awake, and where the steps are, `docs/database-agents.md`. It stays within the status page's 1,200 characters a line, for the longest region name too.
  - Run all's guide (`NB_DatabaseHub_Control`'s `agent_lines()`) has the once-per-deployment line, which says the same first, then one line for each case. The next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes the notebook.
  - **The setup, documented** (the owner's request, 2026-11-20): [Database Agents for T3](docs/database-agents.md) has every step: Database Hub > **Agent (preview)** > **Manage agents** > **Database agent settings**, **Enabled** on; the two cases; the reset and one row at a time; **Agent policy** (the gear) with **Flag long-running queries**, **Force last known good plans**, and **Flag high CPU**, then **Apply** and **Save**; that **Database agent is active** is about the agent, not each database; the issue about 15 minutes after a condition starts, and its close; Copilot for **Explain why** and **Analyze slow query**; and when to do it again. What was seen live in the owner's tenant on 2026-11-20 says so: the panel's text; rows that show only "ContosoCare", with no server and an empty **Owner** column; 19 `ContosoCare` rows, of which 3 still existed; a save with "0 of 19 selected" that worked, then 3 rows that saved, the 3 that exist, paused ones included, so saving doesn't need T3 awake; saves in quick succession "temporarily limited"; and PostgreSQL (0) and Cosmos DB (0), as Learn's scope says. What's inferred says so: Database Hub's telemetry held the same 19 Azure SQL databases over the last 14 days, so a deleted database stays listed until its telemetry ages out, up to about 7 days after it stopped reporting. It wasn't observed whether a save works again once one drops off, so the docs say to fix the selection now, not to wait. `docs/troubleshooting.md` has a **Database agent settings** section with a row for each. Every mention that called the step optional, or said to do it while T3 is awake, says it this way now.
  - Tests in `DbHubDemo.T3Agent.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Teardown.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Golden.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `Fabric.ControlNotebook.Tests.ps1`.

## [2026-11-18] - Before the Ignite updates

Everything on `main` before the Ignite updates, from Phase 3 (October 2026) to 2026-11-18, as it was recorded then; the [README](README.md) describes the demo as it is now.

**Upgrading an existing demo** (2026-11-16): nothing changes in a deployed environment until you run `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, which updates a clean checkout of `main` from GitHub first (`-NoSelfUpdate` runs the code you have) and needs no edit to your configuration. A newer copy of the scripts without that run says what isn't deployed yet, with `./scripts/deploy.ps1` as the fix, never as a **Fail**. That run sets up T4's read replica, which it deletes at its end, and each session creates again; the session control, an existing one adopted and a configuration's `"enabled": false` overridden; the nightly stop's wait for a running deploy or session; the republished runbooks, notebooks, history model, Insights app, and workload runner. The lakehouse's history tables keep their rows, and later runs append to them. A T2 host that doesn't start because it kept chunk Run Commands (a demo deployed since about 2026-11-13) is brought back by that run, which can take 30 to 60 minutes longer once (R871); a host whose agent stays dead even then is deleted and created again, once, in the same run, about 70 minutes more (R872). A non-Owner's **DONE** prints `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1` for an Owner to run once. Nothing new bills between sessions: the replica bills only while a session or a run has it. P9's GitHub token is no longer used. `destroy.ps1` now deletes everything `deploy.ps1` created, the session control, the Fabric workspaces, and the group `init.ps1` created included, each only when it's this environment's (`-KeepFabric` keeps the workspaces; R874), and never a Fabric workspace another environment uses or might use, which each `deploy.ps1` now records (R877). Without your configuration file (a fresh clone, another PC), the first run writes it from what's deployed when the environment is yours, and never updates one that isn't (2026-11-18, [#60](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/60), R890). The [README](README.md#upgrading-an-existing-demo) has the details.

### Added

- Phase 3: architecture, automation support matrix, draft security model, prerequisites, permissions, and ADRs 0001–0016, including plan Revision 2.
- Phase 4: repository scaffolding.
  - Shared PowerShell module `DbHubDemo`: redacting logger, validation status model, configuration loader with schema defaults, and the subscription guard.
  - Configuration schema, example configuration, and `config/versions.json`.
  - Script and workflow placeholders for later phases.
  - Static validation: PSScriptAnalyzer, Pester, markdownlint, actionlint, gitleaks, and CodeQL when available.
  - .NET 10 workload generator skeleton with xUnit v3 tests and a Dockerfile.
  - Devcontainer, pre-commit hooks, Dependabot, issue and pull request templates, CODEOWNERS, and Copilot instructions.
  - The supplied notebook in `fabric/notebooks/`, with outputs stripped.
- Phase 5: foundation infrastructure.
  - Subscription-scope `infra/main.bicep` that creates the demo resource group, merges existing tags with the required ones, and deploys the network, artifact cache, budget, and an optional lock.
  - Network with four NSGs (ADR 0007), lateral-movement deny rules, and an explicit outbound mode (ADR 0017).
  - Compiled `azuredeploy.json` with a sync check, and PSRule for Azure in CI with justified suppressions.
  - `scripts/preflight.ps1` (P1–P6, region, sizes, quota, policy what-if, cost) and `scripts/estimate-cost.ps1` (live Azure Retail Prices API), with `config/profiles.json` as the single source for profile sizes.
- Phase 6: the VMs, their bootstraps, and the `ContosoCare` database.
  - Bicep for T1 `sqlvm01` (SQL Server 2022 Standard PAYG with the SQL IaaS Agent registration) and the T2 host `archost01`, with Spot, security type, Premium SSD v2 data disks in a configured zone, no data disks in `lowest-cost` (`dataDiskSku` `None`), auto-shutdown, and the bootstrap loader Run Command.
  - The bootstrap loader and resumable SYSTEM scheduled-task state machines for T1 and the T2 host: Hyper-V, NAT and DNS forwarder, a hardened nested guest `arcsql01` built from pinned evaluation media (downloaded with retries that resume), and SQL Server in each install mode.
  - The deterministic `ContosoCare` schema and seed, with per-target Query Store and file-cap settings, and a ScriptDom parse test for every SQL file.
  - Bootstrap bundle delivery in SHA-256-checked Run Command chunks (`New-DemoBootstrapChunk`, `Send-DemoBootstrapBundle`, `Wait-DemoBootstrapLoader`), with no storage account; module functions to build the bundle and generate VM passwords; preflight checks for zones, Premium SSD v2, security types, and Azure Hybrid Benefit.
  - The pinned .NET runtime for the workload runner (ADR 0010).
- Phase 7: Arc onboarding of T2, SQL Server enabled by Azure Arc, and SQL VM monitoring for T1.
  - `scripts/configure-arc.ps1`: waits for the T2 host, onboards the guest with the caller's token as a protected Run Command parameter only, converges the Azure extension for SQL Server (license type PAYG), turns on monitoring, and reports the `T2.ARC.*` checks.
  - `scripts/configure-monitoring.ps1`: merges the `DatabaseWatcheronAzureVM` flag into T1's SQL IaaS Agent settings with every other setting kept, and turns on T2 monitoring.
  - `Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand` and `Get-DemoVmHealth`, the optional host identity, and the opt-in evaluation-only fallback (ADR 0006).
  - The `GuestWebView2` host step: the setup wizard's Azure Login needs the WebView2 runtime, which Windows Server 2022 lacks, so the host installs Microsoft's signed standalone installer in the guest before the wizard step.
  - The T1 `AssociatedServices` step: stops, disables, and uninstalls the image's Analysis Services and Integration Services (owner decision, the lightest default), so unified inventory lists only the engine.
  - The `GuestSignInCleanup` host step: after the wizard, removes the sign-in data that setup leaves on the VM, and turns IE Enhanced Security Configuration back on.
- Phase 11: bounded scenarios and the workload runner.
  - `demo` control objects and bounded scenario procedures in `database/common/110-demo-control.sql` and `120-scenario-procedures.sql`: run records and events, stop flags, free-space checks, a reset that kills only `ContosoCare.Demo/` sessions and verifies no demo transaction is open, and the healthy baseline plus seven incidents (locking with an optional deadlock, CPU, IO, missing index, inefficient query, resource governance, log-rate pressure).
  - `scenarios/<id>/scenario.json` with a JSON schema, `scenarios/activity-profiles.json`, and a never-applied missing-index remediation script.
  - The `ContosoCare.Workload` runner: `run`, `stop`, `reset`, `status`, `profile get|set|run`, `warmup`, and `setup`, with bounds clamped to the scenario maximum and `--not-after`, Windows authentication on T1 and T2, and managed identity for T3.
  - Unit tests for parsing, clamping, profile schedules, tagging, connection strings, schemas, and SQL safety checks, plus opt-in SQL Server integration tests; a repository-root Dockerfile and a local compose file.
- Phase 12: the Fabric notebooks and their import.
  - `NB_DatabaseHub_Deployment` evolved from the supplied template notebook (same name, PySpark kernel, and `LH_DatabaseHub` binding): a parameters cell, narration for every section of the brief, Fabric REST checks with the `pbi` token, Hub telemetry checks with the `kusto` token and a printed-KQL fallback, and results in `validation_results` and `telemetry_snapshots`.
  - New `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity`: activity profiles through an optional, `CONFIRM`-gated `workflow_dispatch` of `demo-control.yml` (P9), recorded in `scenario_runs`; estate pulse, locks and blocking, consumption, T3 log-rate governance, and scenario-window correlation with charts; Database Agent prompts that describe signals; `telemetry_snapshots` and `analysis_findings`.
  - `scripts/configure-fabric.ps1` and the module functions `Get-DemoFabricToken`, `Get-DemoFabricNotebookDefinition`, and `Import-DemoFabricNotebook`: idempotent create or update through the Fabric REST API, with long-running-operation polling and a `-WhatIf` that sends nothing.
- Phase 13 (Milestone A): the workload runner on the VMs.
  - `Publish-DemoWorkloadRunner` (a deterministic, framework-dependent `win-x64` zip, reused while its inputs don't change) and `Send-DemoWorkloadRunner` (the bootstrap chunk path).
  - The runner's SHA-256 and chunk count and the `activityProfile` setting (`baseline` by default) reach the bootstraps through the template.
  - Real `WorkloadRunner` steps on T1 and the T2 host: the runner assembled from verified chunks, the pinned .NET runtime checked against its SHA-512, `setup` on each target, and the SYSTEM task `DbHubDemo-Workload` with the activity profile until the next auto-shutdown. The host waits (`WaitingForGuestSql`) for the guest's SQL Server and installs over PowerShell Direct.
  - `Enable-DemoT3Workload` adds T3 once T1 is in the T3 administrators group, and `Invoke-DemoWorkloadCommand` runs any runner command on T1, T2, or T3 through Run Command.
- Phase 13 (Milestone B): one-click deployment and the demo lifecycle.
  - `deploy.ps1` (`Invoke-DemoDeployment`): ten idempotent stages with `-Resume`, `-FromStage`, `-StopAfter`, and `-WhatIf`, state in the resource group's `DbHub*` tags, the T3 free-offer fallback, and a summary that lists every manual step.
  - `start-demo.ps1` (cost guardrail, T3 resumed or recreated with the readiness gate, the activity profile, warm-up, `-Interactive -ResetAdminPassword`), `stop-demo.ps1` (`-ParkT2`, `-RemoveT3`, `-RemoveBastionOnly`), `status.ps1`, `reset-demo.ps1`, `destroy.ps1` (`-IfExpired`), `demo-control.ps1`, and `setup-github-oidc.ps1`.
