  - On-demand Bastion as a template (`infra/modules/bastion.bicep`), capped at `bastionMaxHours`, with a one-time host password through Run Command that only reaches the clipboard.
  - GitHub Actions: `deploy.yml`, `demo-control.yml` (the ADR 0014 inputs), `destroy.yml`, `warm-up.yml`, the scheduled start, stop, expiry, and Bastion cleanup, and a `live` input on `validate.yml`, all through one reusable workflow and the `demo-setup` composite action with `azure/login` v3 and OpenID Connect.
  - `activityProfileOnBoot` (default `false`): the VMs set the activity profile at boot only on request.
  - The Deploy to Azure button in the README and the deployment guide.
  - Opt-in Azure Key Vault for the one-time host password (owner decision 2026-10-21): `keyVault.enabled` (requires `assignRoles`) deploys `infra/modules/keyvault.bicep` (RBAC authorization, soft delete), grants Key Vault Secrets Officer or Secrets User on the vault only, by role name, stores the password before it's set on the host, and disables it at stop. Off by default; the default path stays Contributor-only with no vault.
- Phase 14: validation and security.
  - `scripts/validate.ps1` (`Invoke-DemoValidation`) runs the live catalog of ADR 0012 through the subscription guard: Preflight, Infrastructure, T1, T2, T3, Hub, Telemetry, Workload, Cost, Security, and Teardown. It writes JSON lines (`out/validate.jsonl` by default) and a summary, and exits 1 for a Fail, or for Manual action required with `-TreatManualAsError`. A deallocated VM gives Warnings with the start command, never a misleading Fail.
  - New checks: `INFRA.*`, `T2.GUEST.ISOLATION`, `WORKLOAD.<target>.BOUNDS`, `COST.BUDGET`, `COST.AUTOSHUTDOWN`, `COST.BASTION` (including a public IP left by an interrupted Bastion delete), `SECURITY.SECRETSCAN.*`, `SECURITY.RBAC.ROLEASSIGNMENTS`, `SECURITY.SECRETS.KEYVAULT`, `SECURITY.NETWORK.*`, and `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL` (Resource Graph); the `cost` and `security` result areas. With Phase 13's Key Vault opt-in (`keyVault.enabled`), the security checks expect its vault and its two Key Vault secrets roles on that vault only.
  - `Find-DemoSecretLeak` scans files, folders, or text with the redaction rules and the session's registered secrets, and returns redacted findings; the scanning procedure for script output, Run Command instance views, deployment history, Actions logs, and notebook exports is in ADR 0009.
  - `.gitleaks.toml`: every default gitleaks rule plus `jwt-any-delimiter`, because the default `jwt` rule misses a token inside an XML element or before `&` in a URL; one allowlist exempts only the public commit SHA pins in `config/versions.json` from the default Sourcegraph rule.
  - Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatibility rules (syntax, commands, and types) for the VM-side scripts in static validation (`tests/PSScriptAnalyzerSettings.Vm.psd1`).
  - The final threat model (`docs/security-model.md`: the nested host's boundary, the token handoff, and the live findings) and the final role matrix (`docs/permissions.md`, with Phase 13's Key Vault opt-in).
- Phase 15: the demo walkthrough, `docs/demo-walkthrough.md`.
  - 15- and 30-minute presenter scripts: one estate, performance, an incident, and the Database Agent with human approval; the 30-minute version adds security and posture, an incident started live, Copilot, and the notebooks.
  - Preparation with readiness gates (Resource Graph, telemetry, and the T3 gate), the persistent-T3 recommendation, cost ceilings without prices, and Bastion only when needed.
  - The documented, scriptable fallback; a cheat sheet for all eight scenarios on T1, T2, and T3; a recovery playbook; and the after-session steps.
  - Aligned with Phase 13 Milestone B and the live state of 2026-10-21: every command uses the real parameters of `start-demo.ps1`, `stop-demo.ps1`, `status.ps1`, `reset-demo.ps1`, `demo-control.ps1`, and `deploy.ps1`, so the interim commands are gone; T1 shows only its database engine, T2 runs SQL Server 2022 Standard pay-as-you-go with monitoring on, and the notebooks are imported, with a fallback while the live-activity notebook hasn't run and while the shared Fabric capacity is paused. Presenters never change their default subscription: the scripts target the configured one.
  - Every Microsoft Learn page it relies on, checked on 2026-10-20 or 2026-10-21. The live rehearsal is pending.
  - Every step carries nine fields (time, presenter action, narration, expected visual and signal, preview fallback, reset, a planned screenshot, and why it matters), and the new convention test `tests/unit/Docs.Walkthrough.Tests.ps1` checks them, the step timings, the screenshot paths, and the absence of image links.
- Phase 16: cleanup testing.
  - `destroy.ps1` records a manifest of names and identities first (`Get-DemoTeardownManifest`, `out/destroy-manifest-<environment>.json`), deletes the subscription deployment record `dbhub-<environment>-main` after the resource group (`DESTROY.DEPLOYMENT`), and ends with a residual check (`Test-DemoTeardownResidue`, `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.*`, summed up in `DESTROY.RESIDUAL`): the group, everything carrying the environment's tags, Arc machines, extensions, and SQL Server instances anywhere, role assignments, the managed identities and their P6 membership, deployment records, and the budget; resources outside the group that name it, and the platform's records about the deleted resources, as evidence. New options `-SkipResidueCheck`, `-ResidueWaitMinutes`, and `-ManifestPath`. A run after an interruption finishes the cleanup: it waits for a group still being deleted (`ResourceGroupBeingDeleted`) and keeps the identities an earlier run recorded.
  - The residual check reads Azure Resource Graph and Microsoft Graph through `Invoke-DemoRestRequest` with tokens verified for the configured tenant and caller (the guard update), never `az rest` or `az ad`, and only after a canary shows they answer for the configured subscription and tenant (Resource Graph trims its answer to what the caller can read); otherwise their checks are **Warnings**, never a Pass, and the manifest records `IdentitiesRecorded` `false`. The identities are looked up in one Microsoft Graph request (`directoryObjects/getByIds`). A residual check that can't run after the deletes is a `DESTROY.RESIDUAL` **Warning**, not an error.
  - Opt-in live teardown tests in `tests/teardown` (`Teardown.Residue.Tests.ps1`, `Invoke-TeardownTest.ps1`), skipped by the static run, and the deployment-stack trial (`Invoke-DeploymentStackTrial.ps1`, with `stack-trial/*.bicep`).
  - `docs/cleanup.md` completed: the commands, the residual checks, tested results, partial failures, Arc cleanup, Defender and policy remnants, the shared demo environment, and the subscription guard.
- Phase 17: final docs and the final report.
  - `docs/troubleshooting.md`: symptoms, causes, and fixes from sign-in to GitHub Actions, including a subscription guard that reports another subscription, tenant, or account (no change to your default subscription is needed), an optional isolated Azure CLI sign-in for shared machines, and the fallbacks at a glance.
  - `docs/references.md`: the official sources, grouped by topic.
  - The final report at the top of `docs/implementation-report.md`: what was built, the live status per target, the acceptance checklist, owner actions, cost against both ceilings, the security summary, the open risks, the presentation layer (Phases 18 and 19, in progress), and the pull request merge order.
- Phase 18: the presentation layer, part 1.
  - The Real-Time Dashboard as code, `fabric/realtime/`: `RTD_DatabaseHub_LiveView`, a KQL dashboard item definition (schema version 52) with Estate pulse, Incident, and Resources pages, Time range and Target parameters, a one-minute refresh, and color only for status, over the documented telemetry endpoint with each viewer's identity. `Export-DemoRealTimeDashboard` generates it from `dashboard.spec.psd1` and shared KQL, with deterministic element IDs and no real IDs.
  - One copy of the analysis KQL: `scripts/modules/DbHubDemo/Kql/Live.*.kql` hold the live-activity notebook's queries byte for byte, and a test fails if the notebook, the files, or the generated JSON drift.
  - `Import-DemoFabricItem`, the generic Fabric item import (List Items, Create Item or Update Item Definition, long-running operations, `-WhatIf`), with a Get Item Definition read-back; `Get-DemoFabricDashboardDefinition`; and `configure-fabric.ps1 -ImportDashboard` (`FABRIC.IMPORT.RTD_DATABASEHUB_LIVEVIEW`, `FABRIC.READBACK.RTD_DATABASEHUB_LIVEVIEW`).
  - `Test-DemoRealTimeDashboardData`, the tile data check and a deploy step: each tile's KQL runs read-only against the telemetry endpoint for the last 24 hours with a Kusto token for the configured subscription's account (`FABRIC.DASHBOARD.TILE.*`, `FABRIC.DASHBOARD.DATA`), after an import or alone with `configure-fabric.ps1 -CheckDashboardData`. Live on 2026-10-21: imported, read back identical in content, and 14 of 14 tiles populated.
  - Optional prerequisite P11 and `Test-DemoFabricViewerLicense` (`PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.VIEWERLICENSE`) in `preflight.ps1` and `configure-fabric.ps1`: a **Warning** with the viewer licensing rule for Power BI content when the workspace's capacity is smaller than F64, or its SKU can't be read.
  - `docs/presentation.md`: Database Hub, the Real-Time Dashboard, the history report, and the Fabric app, with when to use each, what each viewer needs, and the cost note.
  - The walkthrough's 30-minute script gains beat 5: Database Hub's **Create SQL monitors from template**, run live, next to the dashboard as code; the other beats are re-timed to keep 30 minutes, with preparation, recovery, and cleanup steps for both dashboards.
  - Automation support matrix rows 99–104, and row 34 updated.
- Phase 19: the Power BI history report and its semantic model ([`fabric/powerbi/`](fabric/powerbi/README.md)). The demo's Fabric app is a separate item: a Fabric Apps (preview) item built with the Rayfin SDK and CLI (Phase 19b).
  - The live-activity notebook prepares the report tables: `report_date_utc` on all five tables, the new `incident_windows` table (requested scenario windows plus detected signal episodes from `signal_episodes()`, merged by key), single-file writes, and `OPTIMIZE` past 200 files; the scenario catalog adds `resource-governance` and `log-rate-pressure`, and the deployment notebook's purge cell covers the new table.
  - `SM_DatabaseHub_History` as TMDL: Direct Lake on OneLake over the five tables, five calculated dimensions, 16 relationships, and 103 measures with descriptions, display folders, and format strings; the OneLake source is an all-zero placeholder.
  - `RPT_DatabaseHub_History` as PBIR, written by a deterministic Node.js generator (`fabric/powerbi/generator`) from the spec's Design Brief: four pages with 10, 8, 11, and 10 visuals, the DbHub Cockpit Dark theme, drillthrough to the incident page, a synced history period, and the Explorer reset bookmark.
  - `scripts/configure-powerbi.ps1`, an unattended deploy step with `-WhatIf`: the subscription guard, then the model (`Import-DemoFabricItem`), its refresh (`Invoke-DemoSemanticModelRefresh`), the data check (`Test-DemoSemanticModelData`: every measure with DAX, again per telemetry bin, incident, and signal when blank over the whole history, and a **Warning** when still blank because no scenario ran yet), and the report (`Get-DemoFabricReportDefinition`, `Test-DemoPowerBIReport`), with `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.*` and `FABRIC.REPORT.*` records in `out/configure-powerbi.jsonl`. `-CheckModelData` runs the data check alone. It creates no app: the org app shell planned in the spec was dropped (2026-10-22).
  - `Get-DemoPowerBIToken`, for the Power BI REST API audience, and `Find-DemoSemanticModel` (Get Datasets In Group), which finds the deployed model for `-CheckModelData` and `-SkipModel`.
  - Automation support matrix rows 105–108.
- Phase 19b: the demo's Fabric app, the Insights app `APP_DatabaseHub_Insights`: a read-only [Fabric App (preview)](https://learn.microsoft.com/fabric/apps/overview) over `SM_DatabaseHub_History` ([`fabric/app/`](fabric/app/README.md)).
  - React and the Rayfin SDK 1.36.2, started from the Fabric Apps data app template (MIT): the Estate, Incidents, Readiness, and Live links pages; seven custom SVG visuals (estate constellation, activity clock, ridgelines, incident replay with a scrubber, before-and-after spotlight, wait flow as a Sankey or sunburst, and readiness rings); rule-based insight cards; show and copy the DAX, a data table, CSV download and copy, refresh, and focus mode on every visual; three themes (AA contrast tested), a motion switch, and the view kept in the app for the portal's item frame.
  - One read-only connector, `history` (`fabric-semanticmodel`, `executeQuery` only, delegated), with a 14-query DAX catalog, row bounds, and a 5-minute cache; `rayfin.yml` holds only `${...}` placeholders and sets `assetAccess: protected` explicitly.
  - **Investigate with the Database Agent:** ready-made prompts for Database Hub's built-in Database Agent, written from the data on screen. The Incidents page has them per incident window (a button on each window, **Ask the agent** on the replay, and a card under it), with the window's target, resource name, UTC window, peak, baseline, and ratio; Estate and Readiness have a prompt library (estate health, the top waits per target, blocking chains, and what changed since the baseline; Readiness adds telemetry coverage). Copy, Copy all, and **Open Database Hub** in a new tab (`ctid` from the deploy-time settings); the labels Preview, "the agent proposes; you approve each action", and "investigation only"; and a switch to the same prompts for the GitHub Copilot CLI and VS Code fallback with the `microsoft-sql-fdh` and `microsoft-sql` plugins. The agent is UI-only, so the user pastes; the app never sends or runs anything. A new query, `target-names`, reads the resource names from the telemetry snapshots, and the deploy step writes the resource group name to `dbhub-app.json`.
  - `scripts/configure-fabric-app.ps1`, an unattended, idempotent deploy step: the guard, the workspace and an Active capacity, the items by name (`Resolve-DemoFabricAppItem`), a Git-ignored stage (`Sync-DemoFabricAppStage`), the prerequisites (`Test-DemoFabricAppPrerequisite`: Node.js, the Rayfin CLI's sign-in, and the Fabric Apps and Execute Queries tenant settings), `rayfin up` (`Invoke-DemoFabricAppDeployment`), and the connector and hosting checks (`Test-DemoFabricApp`), as `FABRIC.APP.*` records in `out/configure-fabric-app.jsonl`. `-WhatIf` plans offline, `-DryRun` runs `rayfin up --dry-run`, and `-CheckOnly` checks the deployed app.
  - Vitest unit, page, and theme tests, a production license check, the `FABRICAPP` static validation check, the **Fabric App** CI job (Node.js 24, `actions/setup-node` pinned by SHA), and `DbHubDemo.FabricApp.Tests.ps1`.
  - Optional prerequisites P12–P14 and automation support matrix rows 121–125.
- Phase 20: the fast redeploy, parallel tracks plus a T2 golden snapshot ([ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md)).
  - `deploy.ps1` runs in parallel by default: Preflight and Infrastructure, then the T1, T2, and Fabric tracks at once, each in its own process with its own subscription guard, then the validation and `DEPLOY.TIMING` (per track, each target's ready time, and the total). `-Sequential` keeps the old pipeline; `-WaitForT2` waits for a T2 that builds in the background (`DEPLOY.T2.BACKGROUND`).
  - The T2 golden snapshot: `scripts/capture-t2-golden.ps1` (preconditions, an incremental snapshot with `DenyAll` in `rg-<prefix>-golden`, lifetime tags, and a disk check), `scripts/test-t2-golden.ps1 -Onboard` (a quarantined copy, the Arc identity reset, SQL Server running, a fresh onboarding with PAYG and monitoring, and the destroy path with zero residue), and the restore (`t2Source` `auto`, `golden`, or `build`; `PREFLIGHT.T2.SOURCE`): the template creates the host's OS disk from the snapshot, and the T2 track rotates the passwords, releases the air gap, resets the Arc identity, and onboards.
  - `destroy.ps1 -IncludeGolden` and `-WithoutGolden`: the golden group stays unless asked, and an environment's `PaygWizard` install is never deleted without a boot-verified snapshot (`DESTROY.GOLDEN.GUARD`).
  - `STATUS.T2.GOLDEN`, the snapshot's monthly cost in `estimate-cost.ps1`, preflight, and `start-demo.ps1`'s guardrail (Retail Prices API at run time), and the golden group in `status.ps1`'s month-to-date cost.
  - The Fabric track deploys the history report (`configure-powerbi.ps1`) and, when it exists, the Fabric app (`configure-fabric-app.ps1`, Phase 19b); `configure-fabric.ps1 -RunLiveActivity` runs the live-activity notebook on demand (`Invoke-DemoFabricNotebookRun`).
  - Automation support matrix rows 111–120.
- Phase 21: `docs/images/readme/`, the history report's four pages for the README, redacted (the notebook run ID is masked) and stripped of metadata.
- Phase 24: T4, an Azure Database for PostgreSQL flexible server (Burstable B1ms, PostgreSQL 18, Microsoft Entra only, virtual network integration) with its own bounded scenarios (`connection-surge` and `idle-in-transaction` are new; `healthy-baseline`, `cpu-pressure`, `locking-blocking`, and `io-pressure` run on it too), the same runner interface as T1 to T3, a T4 track in the parallel deploy, start and stop with the demo, `STATUS.T4` with the enhanced metrics and `STATUS.T4.LIVE`, `Test-DemoT4`, `HUB.ESTATE.T4`, `METRICS.T4`, the cost estimate with its Defender plans and the 7-day restart, and validate's `T4` area ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md)).
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): T4's size and zone are settings. `t4SkuName` is `Standard_B1ms` by default, or `Standard_B2s`, the documented fallback when Azure refuses B1ms with `SkuNotAvailable` (as West US 3 did although its capability list included B1ms), or `Standard_D2ds_v5`, the documented General Purpose option the owner chose after West US 3 refused B2s too; the tier follows the size. `t4AvailabilityZone` applies only when T4 is created. A refused size is a **Fail** in `deploy.ps1` (`DEPLOY.T4.SKU`) and preflight (`PREFLIGHT.T4.SKU`; one more try in another zone is a **Warning**) that names the next option; nothing changes the size on its own. `Test-DemoT4`, preflight's capability check, and the cost estimate use the configured size (General Purpose per vCore-hour), with its 7-day restart worst case, and `STATUS.T4` says what a stopped T4's automatic restart would bill per hour (R441).
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): `status.ps1` reports the VMs' disks against the profile (`STATUS.DISKS`, a **Warning** naming each drifted disk).
- Phase 25b: the profile host's heartbeat. `profile run` writes `C:\ProgramData\DbHubDemo\host-lanes.json` (`--host-state-file`) with each target lane's state; `status` adds a `host` node to its JSON (written last, so a Run Command's output tail keeps it) and a `Host:` line, `profile get --json` adds the node too, and validation reports it as `WORKLOAD.<target>.HOST` ([ADR 0012](docs/adr/0012-validation-model.md#phase-25b-the-profile-host-lane-2026-10-26)).
- Phase 25b: eight bounded scenarios, one per wait family ([ADR 0010](docs/adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md#the-eight-new-scenarios-and-their-bounds), [scenarios](scenarios/README.md)): `blocking-chain` (a chain 3 to 6 sessions deep under one head blocker, plus a table lock from lock escalation), `deadlock-storm` (pairs that deadlock round after round), `tempdb-contention` (`PAGELATCH` waits in tempdb), `memory-grant-pressure` (large grants with the `MIN_GRANT_PERCENT` hint, so sessions queue on `RESOURCE_SEMAPHORE`), `parallel-scans` (parallel plans chosen by cost: `CXPACKET`, `CXCONSUMER`), `log-flush-waits` (one commit per row: `WRITELOG`), `slow-consumer` (a client that reads slowly: `ASYNC_NETWORK_IO`), and `capacity-max` (every scheduler busy and the storage at its cap at once, 20 minutes at most). Each has its T-SQL procedure and run events, a runner orchestration, a `scenario.json` and README, and unit and opt-in SQL Server integration tests; `incident:<scenario>` reaches them through `demo-control.ps1`, the workflow, and the notebook. Architecture deviation 28.
- Phase 25b: the Database Agent prompt pack covers the new scenarios: an in-Hub and a skills prompt each, with the schema's scenario list and the index; the walkthrough has a cheat sheet for each.
- Phase 25b: the live-activity notebook knows the new scenarios: Buffer Latch, Memory, Parallelism, Tran Log IO, and Network IO wait families from `SqlServerWaitStats` and deadlocks from `Number of Deadlocks/sec`, as incident-window rules (`EPISODE_WAIT_SECONDS`), charts, and findings.
- Phase 25c: the optional on-demand activity control ([ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md); off by default, `control.enabled`). A Fabric notebook, `fabric/control/NB_DatabaseHub_Control.ipynb`, sends one request (a scenario per target and a duration) to an Azure Automation webhook and shows the run live; the Windows PowerShell 5.1 runbooks in `automation/runbooks/` (`DbHub-Control`, `DbHub-Stop`) validate it against the scenario catalog's allow-lists, refuse it while another run is active, create the run's auto-stop first, start what's stopped, and set each target's profile through asynchronous managed Run Command; Stop now, the auto-stop, and a daily T4 safety stop go through Azure Resource Manager only.
- Phase 25c: `scripts/setup-activity-control.ps1` (`Invoke-DemoActivityControlSetup`) sets it up with Contributor in `rg-<prefix>-control` (`infra/control/control.bicep` and `webhook.bicep`): the vault's network mode adapts to the tenant's policy (`control.vaultNetworkMode` `auto`, `public`, or `private`): a public vault and the notebook in a folder of the existing workspace, or a private vault and the notebook in a "DBHub Control" workspace with a managed private endpoint that the script creates and approves. `-GrantRole` (an Owner) creates the custom role "DbHub Demo Activity Operator" and its assignments A1-A3, exactly as approved; `-RotateWebhook` and `-Remove` too, and `-WhatIf` everywhere.
- Phase 25c: `validate.ps1` (Workload area, `CONTROL.*`) and `status.ps1` (`STATUS.CONTROL`) check the control; `estimate-cost.ps1` prices its vault operations and job minutes when it's on; the redaction rule `automation-webhook` and the gitleaks rule `azure-automation-webhook` catch a webhook URL; static validation builds and lints `infra/control/*.bicep` and checks `automation/` with the Windows PowerShell 5.1 rules. Automation support matrix rows 126–131, prerequisite P15.
- Phase 26a: the Fabric workspace and lakehouse by name ([ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-fabric-resources-by-name.md)). `fabric.workspaceName`, `fabric.lakehouseName`, `fabric.capacityName`, and `fabric.createIfMissing` replace the IDs, which become optional overrides. `Resolve-DemoFabricConfig` finds them through documented Fabric REST calls and creates what's missing: the workspace on the named Active capacity, a workspace without a capacity assigned to it, and the lakehouse with lakehouse schemas (`FABRIC.RESOLVE.*`). It never creates or changes a capacity, and scripts that only read never create anything.
- Phase 26a: `status.ps1` shows the Fabric workspace, the lakehouse, and the capacity's state (`STATUS.FABRIC`), and preflight's `PREFLIGHT.P5.WORKSPACE` checks the workspace by name, or the capacity deploy would create it on. [Configuration](docs/configuration.md) explains the Fabric settings.
- Phase 26a: `configure-fabric.ps1 -NewNotebooksOnly`, which `deploy.ps1` passes while `fabric.importNotebooks` is false: a notebook that isn't in the workspace yet is imported without asking, and one that is stays as it is. When the live-activity notebook was just created, or its lakehouse was, it runs once, so the history tables exist before the model's refresh.
- Phase 26b: the first run from a clone ([ADR 0023](docs/adr/0023-first-run-automation.md)).
  - `scripts/init.ps1` (`Initialize-DemoConfig`) writes `config/demo.config.json` from your Azure CLI sign-in: `az account list`, the one call before the subscription guard, then the guard, Microsoft Graph `/me`, P1, the Fabric capacity and workspace, the P6 group, and T2's mode, asking only when there's a choice. It writes only a Git-ignored, schema-validated file, never over one without `-Force` (backup in `out/config-backup`), and prints the next steps; `-WhatIf` and `-NonInteractive`.
  - `init.ps1 -CreateAdminGroup` creates the P6 security group with you as its owner through Microsoft Graph, or prints an administrator's steps where your tenant doesn't allow it.
  - `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn` (`Connect-DemoRayfinCli`) runs the Rayfin CLI's one-time sign-in in your console; it's **Not supported** in CI.
  - `fabric.manageCapacity` (default false): `start-demo.ps1` resumes a paused F capacity that's yours alone and records it in the resource group tag `DbHubFabricCapacityResumed`; `stop-demo.ps1` and `destroy.ps1` pause only what that tag records; `status.ps1` shows it (`START.`, `STOP.`, `DESTROY.`, and `STATUS.FABRIC.CAPACITY`). The API version of `Microsoft.Fabric/capacities` is pinned in `config/versions.json`.
  - `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory`, or `fabric.saveHistoryOnStop`, runs `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` to save the session's history before anything pauses the capacity (`STOP.FABRIC.SAVEHISTORY`, at most 15 minutes).
  - The README's **Open in GitHub Codespaces** button, and `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`. Automation support matrix rows 150–155.
- Phase 26c: ready for a public repository ([ADR 0022](docs/adr/0022-public-repository-and-github-actions-hardening.md)). `setup-github-oidc.ps1 -ConfigureGitHub` (opt-in) creates the GitHub environment with you as its required reviewer, deployments from the default branch only, and its four secrets, through the GitHub CLI; `-ScheduleEnvironment` adds a schedule environment without reviewers for scheduled runs only. `scripts/setup-github-repo.ps1` (opt-in, `-WhatIf`, `-Template`), for after the repository is public: a ruleset on the default branch (pull requests with 0 required approvals, no force-push, no deletion), secret scanning with push protection, private vulnerability reporting, and Dependabot alerts; what GitHub refuses on a private repository is **Manual action required**.
- Phase 26c: `Register-DemoLogMask`: in GitHub Actions only, it masks identifiers with `::add-mask::`, and the module's log lines, job summaries, and `out/*.jsonl` results write them, any other GUID, and the servers' random suffix as `***`. A **Deployment problem** issue form that asks for `status.ps1`'s output with IDs replaced, and the README's "Getting help".
- Phase 26d: no operator token left anywhere ([ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#phase-26d-amendment-no-operator-token-left-anywhere-2026-10-27)). `vm/bootstrap/Invoke-DemoSignInResidue.ps1` finds token-shaped values in setup's log folders, temp folders, PowerShell history and transcripts, the Run Command handlers' and agents' folders, event logs, and the sign-in caches (archives in setup's log folders expanded and checked inside, a `.cab` with `expand.exe`), on a VM and in the T2 guest, and reports counts and locations only; `./scripts/check-sign-in-data.ps1` (`SECURITY.SIGNIN.<VM>`) runs it, `-Clean` removes what it finds. It runs after every interactive sign-in and Arc onboarding, gates the golden capture (`GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SIGNIN`), and scans each golden copy (`GOLDEN.TEST.SIGNIN`); the Security log is never cleared on its own (**Manual action required** with the command), and clearing another log is a **Warning**. `capture-t2-golden.ps1 -WipeFreeSpace` zeroes, compacts, and trims free space before a capture; `Enter-DemoKeepAwake` and `Exit-DemoKeepAwake` keep the PC awake during golden runs; `test-t2-golden.ps1 -KeepOlderSnapshots`. The README's "Your sign-in and tokens".
- Phase 28: one command, one guided stop ([ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md#amendments-2026-10-29-phase-28-one-command-one-guided-stop), [deployment](docs/deployment.md#one-command-one-guided-stop-phase-28)).
  - `deploy.ps1` with no configuration runs the first run itself (`Initialize-DemoFirstRun`, as `init.ps1 -CreateAdminGroup`), and stops **NOT DONE** with the fix when something needs a person.
  - The `PaygWizard` guided stop in the same run: the host's request with a time limit, on-demand Bastion, Arc onboarding first, SQL Server setup pre-filled from a configuration file (`/ConfigurationFile`, `UIMODE` Normal, no secret) with logon tasks in the guest and on the host (Virtual Machine Connection), the guest's one automatic sign-in, one unmissable prompt (a banner, a beep, the console window flashing, the portal page in the browser, reminders every 5 minutes, and `HUMAN ACTION REQUIRED: DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDSTOP`), the host's check of the result (Standard, the default instance, the Azure extension) with the documented fix for a wrong install, and T2 finished by itself (`vm/hyperv-guest/GuestSetup.ps1`, `vm/bootstrap/Set-DemoGuidedSetupRequest.ps1`, the host's `GuidedSetup` step; [ADR 0006](docs/adr/0006-sql-editions-and-licensing.md#amendment-one-command-one-guided-stop-phase-28-2026-10-29)).
  - The guest signs itself in once for the setup step only: Windows automatic sign-in with `AutoLogonCount` 1 and a temporary password kept only as the LSA `DefaultPassword` secret, cleaned up in a `finally` and on every host pass; the sign-in data scan fails on any leftover and blocks a golden capture; `STATUS.T2.AUTOSIGNIN` in `status.ps1` ([ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#phase-28-amendment-the-guests-one-automatic-sign-in-and-the-portal-password-reset-2026-10-29)).
  - Without a working clipboard and the Key Vault opt-in, the host password isn't set or shown: the prompt, the status page, and the handoff line give the portal's **Help** > **Reset password** path, the handover is recorded, and the password is rotated afterward.
  - A live status page: the progress model `out/deploy-progress.json`, written atomically by `deploy.ps1` and every track, and `out/deploy-status.html`, regenerated at least every 15 seconds (a meta refresh, no JavaScript, every message HTML-encoded and redacted), with one banner in four states and every step per track; `./scripts/status.ps1 -Watch` shows the same in a console.
  - One ending: **DONE** (Database Hub shows every target: `HUB.ESTATE.T1`–`T4`, `TELEMETRY.T1`–`T3`, `METRICS.T4`, T2's Standard with PAYG and monitoring, T1's inventory, and the required Fabric items, `FABRIC.ITEMS.REQUIRED`), with T4 off and `Developer` mode said plainly and the optional Insights app listed with how to add it; or **NOT DONE**, with the single next action and the exact command. A plain rerun continues an unfinished deployment.
  - `deploy.ps1` keeps the PC awake for the whole run, prints what to expect (the timeline and when a person is needed), warns sessions that can't take keyboard input and GitHub Codespaces users about the idle timeout, and records the console's transcript in `out/logs`.
  - `./scripts/collect-diagnostics.ps1` (`Export-DemoDiagnosticBundle`, `DIAG.BUNDLE`): a redacted, secret-scanned zip of the results, the progress model, the transcripts, and the saved instance views for a **Deployment problem** issue, whose form asks for it and for how the run ended.
  - `-NoWaitForT2`, `-NoStatusPage`, and `-NoBrowser` on `deploy.ps1`; preflight's `-DeferPolicyInventory`; the Run Command watchdog (`DEPLOY.WATCHDOG_<VM>`, `DEPLOY.EXTENSIONS_<VM>`); the README's "Running it with an AI assistant"; automation support matrix rows 160–168; and the tests `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`, and `Vm.GuidedSetup.Tests.ps1`.
- Phase 28 follow-up: `./scripts/destroy.ps1 -ResidueOnly [-ManifestPath <file>]` runs only the residual check again, read-only, with the manifest an earlier destroy wrote (`DESTROY.MANIFEST`, the `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.*` checks, `DESTROY.RESIDUAL`, in `out/destroy-residue-<environment>.jsonl`); it deletes nothing and asks nothing ([cleanup](docs/cleanup.md#check-again-later-read-only)). `status.ps1` reports the demo's leftover managed Run Commands on its VMs (`STATUS.RUNCOMMANDS`, a **Warning** with the `az vm run-command delete` commands), and `start-demo.ps1` deletes the on-demand control's finished ones on the VMs that run (`START.CONTROL.RUNCOMMANDS`, a **Warning** when a delete fails, never a **Fail**).
- The showcase (the owner's request, 2026-11-03): **Run all** fills the Database Hub visuals that can fill within one session.
  - The `showcase` activity profile: the baseline plus a 30-minute cycle of bounded incidents on all four targets, at most two at a time per target and staggered across targets, 90 minutes by default and 120 at most ([what fills each Database Hub visual, and when](docs/database-hub-visuals.md#what-fills-each-database-hub-visual-and-when)).
  - Activity profiles take an `anchor` (`profileStart` starts the cycle when the profile is set), a slot's `cycles` (`all`, `odd`, `even`, or a list), and a T4 slot's `tier` (`profile set --tier Burstable|GeneralPurpose`).
  - Request stamps, so the newest request wins: `profile set`, `stop --all`, and `reset` take `--if-newer <time> --request-id <id>`; an older request is a **Pass** that changes nothing. `demo.activity_profile` gains `requested_at` and `request_id`, and `demo.usp_profile_set` compares and writes atomically; on T1, a lock file guards T3's and T4's profile file.
  - `profile run` raises its own priority to above normal on Windows, so a CPU incident on `sqlvm01` doesn't starve its T3 and T4 lanes, and records it in `host-lanes.json`.
  - The scenario `plan-regression` (T3 only): one query's plan regresses, and automatic tuning's `FORCE_LAST_GOOD_PLAN`, on by Azure SQL Database's default, forces the earlier plan back; the showcase runs it in its third cycle. `reset` unforces its plan (`forced-plan-unforced`).
  - Prompts `showcase.in-hub.md`, `showcase.skills.md`, `plan-regression.in-hub.md`, and `plan-regression.skills.md`, with their rows in the live coverage record.
  - The README's "What fills each Database Hub visual, and when", visual by visual and target by target, with each threshold, the slot that fills it, and when it will and won't populate, and "The baseline run: why it matters, and when it doesn't"; deployment's "Update an existing environment"; cost management's dated examples for business hours (with two optional upgrades) and for one showcase session; and the limitations of one session.
  - **Run all runs the showcase on T1 to T4** (2026-11-04): `DbHub-Control` accepts `showcase` (capped at its 120 minutes) and `off` per target; a profile takes no intensity or concurrency, and a run of `off` alone is refused. It sends `profile set --name showcase` with the run's end as `--not-after`, and T4's `--tier` and `--vcores` from its size; setup bakes the profiles into the runbooks' settings and the notebook's catalog. The notebook's defaults: `CONFIRM = True`, the showcase on T1 to T4, `DURATION_MINUTES = 90`, `WATCH_MINUTES = 30`. A second Run all while a run is active is refused: Stop now first, or wait for the run to end ([ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md#amendments-2026-11-04-run-all-runs-the-showcase-and-t1s-plugin-counts-only-a-fresh-status)).
  - `profile set --vcores <n>` (1 to 64, showcase and `business-day`): T4's CPU-bound slots scale to the same load per vCore, so `cpu-pressure` runs 6 sessions on `Standard_D4ds_v5` instead of 3, never above the scenario's maximum of 16 ([ADR 0010](docs/adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md)).
  - `missing-index` on T4 (2026-11-04): the runner sends its two lookups as top-level parameterized statements on columns without an index, so Query Store captures them and autonomous tuning can recommend indexes in the Azure portal; the showcase runs it on General Purpose at minutes 20-29. The walkthrough's "T4 autonomous tuning in the Azure portal" shows the path (Database Hub **Estate** → T4 → **Open in…** → **Intelligent Performance** > **Autonomous tuning**) and `az postgres flexible-server autonomous-tuning`.
  - `t4AutonomousTuning` (unset means on) and the T4 checks `PREFLIGHT.T4.TUNING`, `PREFLIGHT.T4.SIZE`, and `DEPLOY.T4.SIZE` ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-04-user-directed-general-purpose-standard_d4ds_v5-by-default-with-autonomous-tuning)).
- The Insights app's sign-in from `deploy.ps1` (2026-11-05): in a session that can take keyboard input, when the Rayfin CLI isn't signed in to the demo's tenant, `deploy.ps1` starts the pinned CLI's `rayfin login --tenant <tenantId>` at the beginning of the run, before preflight, in a console window of its own on Windows, and goes on at once; only the app's step waits for it, at most 15 minutes from its start (`DEPLOY.FABRIC.APPSIGNIN`, the status page's **Sign-in for the Insights app** row). It's ended when the tracks are done, when the run ends, or on Ctrl+C, and it never starts in CI ([ADR 0023](docs/adr/0023-first-run-automation.md#amendment-the-rayfin-clis-sign-in-from-deployps1-2026-11-05)).
- `PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.APPSETTINGS` (2026-11-05): preflight reads the Insights app's tenant settings, **Fabric Apps (preview)** (P12) and **Dataset Execute Queries REST API** (P13), through the Fabric admin API: **Pass** when both are on for the organization, otherwise a **Warning** with the portal steps, also when only a Fabric administrator could read them; never a **Fail** ([ADR 0012](docs/adr/0012-validation-model.md#2026-11-05-the-insights-app-in-every-deployment)).
- **The nightly safety stop, in every deployment** (user-directed, 2026-11-05: "Can we have safety stops for all the databases?"; [ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md)):
  - Every evening at `autoShutdownTime` (19:00 Pacific by default), `DbHub-Stop -Scope Nightly` deallocates `sqlvm01` and T2's VM unless they're deallocated or deallocating, and stops T4 when it's running, waiting up to 10 minutes for one that's starting, so a T4 that Azure starts again 7 days after a stop runs at most until that evening. A 409 Conflict from the VMs' DevTest Labs auto-shutdown at the same minute counts as done once the VM reads deallocating. 30 minutes later, or T3's auto-pause delay plus 15 minutes when that's longer, `-Scope T3Check` reads T3's status through Azure Resource Manager only, which doesn't wake it, and reports Paused, Pausing, Missing, AutoPauseOff, TooEarly, Awake, or Unread. It never starts anything, never connects to a database, and never uses Run Command; the VMs' auto-shutdown stays.
  - `deploy.ps1` sets it up on every run, Warnings only (`SAFETYSTOP.RESOURCES`, `.RUNBOOK`, `.SCHEDULE`, `.ROLE`): `infra/safetystop/safetystop.bicep` (the identity `id-<prefix>-<env>-safetystop` and the Automation account `aa-<prefix>-<env>-safetystop`, Basic, local authentication off, in the demo resource group), `DbHub-Stop` with its own settings, the schedules `dbhub-safety-stop` and `dbhub-safety-t3check`, and the custom role "DbHub Demo Safety Stop" (7 read and stop actions) at the demo resource group, created and assigned when the deployer may: an owner-approved exception to ADR 0008. Otherwise `SAFETYSTOP.ROLE` is a **Warning** with one command for an Owner, `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1` (new), and the run still ends **DONE**, which says that T4 isn't stopped after its restart until then.
  - `status.ps1` (`STATUS.SAFETYSTOP`) and `validate.ps1 -Area Cost` (`COST.SAFETYSTOP`) show its times, its role, what its last run stopped, and what its last T3 check found; the jobs are in the account's job history. `destroy.ps1` deletes it with the group and leaves the unassigned role definition, with the command to delete it (`DESTROY.SAFETYSTOP.ROLE`); `SECURITY.RBAC.ROLEASSIGNMENTS` accepts its role at exactly the demo group.
  - The README's "What stops or pauses on its own", one list with times: the VMs' auto-shutdown, the nightly safety stop and its T3 check, T3's auto-pause, the profiles' end, a notebook run's auto-stop, the control's T4 safety stop, Bastion's removal, and the optional GitHub schedules. Prerequisite P16, ADR 0024, and amendments to ADRs 0008, 0012, 0016, 0019, and 0020.
  - Not proven live.
- **T2's `JumpstartImage` install mode** (user-directed, 2026-11-05: a one-click deployment that public users can run in their own subscriptions with no person, reusing Arc Jumpstart's process and images; [ADR 0006](docs/adr/0006-sql-editions-and-licensing.md)): the T2 host makes its guest from [Arc Jumpstart](https://github.com/Azure/arc_jumpstart_docs/blob/71a945c619f78acb441c6470d76a9b58347169fe/docs/azure_jumpstart_arcbox/ITPro/_index.md) ArcBox's prebuilt SQL Server 2022 Standard guest disk, `ArcBox-SQL-STD.vhdx` (Windows Server 2022 Standard, not an evaluation), instead of building it, and T2 keeps Database Hub's Estate, Performance, and the Database Agent's findings with license type PAYG.
  - `vm/hyperv-guest/GuestJumpstart.ps1` and the host steps `GuestSource`, `GuestImage`, `GuestFirstBoot`, and `JumpstartImageCheck`: a resumable download (34 GiB) hashed while it streams, verified against the blob's Content-MD5 and length (fail closed) and compared with the pin `media.jumpstartArcBoxSqlStd` in `config/versions.json` (a newer image is accepted after the checks, with a **Warning** that names it); a Generation 2 guest whose network adapter stays disconnected until its first boot is done; the first boot over PowerShell Direct (a random password in place of the published one, the name `arcsql01` and UTC, SQL Server's server name, `BUILTIN\Administrators` as sysadmin, SQL logins disabled, `AdventureWorksLT2022`, Integration Services, Launchpad, and `C:\Jumpstart` removed, any Arc identity reset); and the image checks before Arc onboarding (Standard, SQL Server 2022, the default instance, sysadmin). Nothing falls back to another mode by itself: a failure ends **NOT DONE** with the command to retry or to choose another mode.
  - The space check counts the image, a 20 GiB growth allowance, and the margin (about 64 GiB on the host's 128 GiB OS disk in `lowest-cost`), not the image's 100 GiB maximum, and `status.ps1` warns early (`STATUS.T2.DISKSPACE`, under 20 GiB free). `STATUS.T2.IMAGE` says whether the guest came from the tested image or a newer one; health.json adds the guest's source, the image record, the free space, and the guest's Windows license.
  - The evaluation guard for the build modes' guests: when 30 or fewer evaluation days are left, at most once a day and never while an activity profile runs, the host extends the guest's Windows evaluation with `slmgr.vbs /rearm`, restarts it, and activates it with `/ato`; with no rearm left, a warning in the host's log and `health.json` to rebuild T2.
  - `PREFLIGHT.T2.MODE` fails `JumpstartImage` with `t2Mode` `EvaluationOnlyAzureVm`, whose T2 isn't a Hyper-V host, and the evaluation-only bootstrap stops at its new first step `InstallMode` with the same fix.
  - Golden snapshots work in `JumpstartImage` mode, as an optional speed-up: a snapshot of a guest that isn't an evaluation is tagged `dbhubEvalExpiresUtc` `none` and never expires ([ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md)).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/Vm.JumpstartImage.Tests.ps1` (the plan, the download decisions, the credential source, the first boot, the checks, the disk rule, and the evaluation guard), and updated install-mode, golden, teardown, and track tests.
- **The history report's snapshot at the end of every deployment** (user-directed, 2026-11-06: "Can we add a step that calls the acitivty notebook one time after deployment so it starts to populate data?"; [ADR 0014](docs/adr/0014-notebook-role-and-capacity.md#amendment-2026-11-06-one-analyze-only-run-at-the-end-of-each-deployment)): last in every `deploy.ps1` run that reaches its validation, after **DONE**'s checks and the switch to the run's activity profile, `Invoke-DemoHistorySnapshotStep` runs `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` once, analyze-only (`CONFIRM` and `STOP_ACTIVITY` false, `SAVE_RESULTS` true, a 1,440-minute window, and the demo's `RESOURCE_GROUP`), as the person who ran `deploy.ps1`, for at most 10 minutes, then refreshes `SM_DatabaseHub_History` once (Direct Lake framing, at most 5 minutes), so the history report shows the run's data. Before, the report was blank after a deployment: the Fabric track's first run of the notebook comes before any target sends telemetry. `-Sequential` does the same.
  - Never blocking: its one record, `DEPLOY.FABRIC.HISTORY`, is **Pass**, a **Warning**, or **Not selected**, never a **Fail** or **Manual action required**, so it never changes **DONE**, `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`'s status, `DEPLOY.TIMING`'s clock, or the exit code. A paused capacity (never resumed, paused, or scaled), a job still running after 10 minutes (it goes on in Fabric), a deduplicated run, a failed notebook, a refresh that didn't pass, or an exception is a **Warning** with the reason and the fix; only Ctrl+C goes through.
  - Its own status-page row, **History report's snapshot**, last under **Validation** and marked "(doesn't block DONE)": **Done**, **Action needed**, **Failed** (in red, for an error), or **Skipped**. What didn't work is on the console at once, then in the **Needs attention** box, **DONE** and **NOT DONE**, and `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`'s evidence.
  - **Not selected** with `-WhatIf`, with `-SkipFabric`, in GitHub Actions (the demo has no supported Fabric setup for the workflow's identity), when **DONE**'s checks didn't run or their telemetry checks didn't pass, and when the Fabric track didn't create the lakehouse or import the notebook. A resumed run with nothing left to do takes it again, which only adds what happened since.
  - Partial data, said plainly: the VMs start the profile's incidents only after **DONE**'s checks, so incident measures such as **Blocking Incidents** stay blank until incidents have run and the notebook runs again (`./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory`, or `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1 -SkipHubChecks -SkipNotebookImport -RunLiveActivity`).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.HistorySnapshot.Tests.ps1` (new), and updated track, lifecycle, and progress tests. The README, the architecture, deployment, troubleshooting, cost management, the automation support matrix (row 183), the Fabric and history report guides, and ADRs 0012, 0014, and 0018 say so. Not proven live yet.
- **`AGENTS.md`, for an AI agent asked to set up, deploy, upgrade, or try the demo** (user-directed, 2026-11-09): run `./scripts/init.ps1` when there's no configuration, then `./scripts/deploy.ps1`, which also upgrades an older demo; never assemble a partial setup by hand or leave a target out; report **DONE** or **NOT DONE** with its reason and the exact next command; and which steps only a person does (`az login`, the Insights app's sign-in and browser confirmation). `.github/copilot-instructions.md` points to it, and the README's assistant section says the same.
- **`deploy.ps1` updates itself from GitHub before it runs** (2026-11-09, the owner's choice: "Update itself: fast-forward a clean checkout to the latest main, then run"): a tester's assistant re-ran an older demo from a clone pulled earlier that day and hit a refusal that was already fixed on `main`, because `deploy.ps1` ran the code in its folder and never asked GitHub. Now, before the transcript, the configuration, or any Azure call, it fetches origin's `main` (at most 20 seconds, never a credential prompt). A clean checkout of `main` that tracks `origin/main` and is behind it fast-forwards (`git merge --ff-only`), says from which commit to which and how many commits came from GitHub, and runs again in a fresh `pwsh`, in the same console, with exactly the same parameters, and exits with that run's code. The restarted run never updates again, and shows the update as the first note in the banner and on the status page. Otherwise (local changes, another branch or a detached HEAD, diverged, offline, no Git, a downloaded zip, `-WhatIf`) `DEPLOY.SELFUPDATE` is a **Warning** with the reason and the exact command, and the run goes on with the local code. `-NoSelfUpdate` or `DBHUB_NO_SELF_UPDATE=1` turns it off, and CI skips it. On a resume, the existing check then sends a changed `vm/` bundle to the VMs. Tests: `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.SelfUpdate.Tests.ps1`, with git mocked for every case, and real git against a local bare repository with a real restart. Docs: the README, deployment, troubleshooting, and `AGENTS.md`, which tells an agent not to pull or switch branches itself ([R780](docs/implementation-report.md#risk-register) to R784).
- **T4's read replica, created for each session** (user-directed, 2026-11-13, "Postgres HA/DR": add the replica to the one-click deployment, account for its lag and PostgreSQL's health in the activity notebook and the Insights app, efficiently and with manageable cost, for new users and for those who deployed before; [ADR 0025](docs/adr/0025-t4-read-replica.md)). Database Hub's PostgreSQL dashboard charts **Read replica lag**, which stayed empty: T4 had no replica.
  - **The replica:** `pgreplica01-<deploymentId>`, a same-region read replica of T4 (`infra/replica/pgreplica01.bicep`), in T4's subnet and private DNS zone, Microsoft Entra only, at T4's actual size. `deploy.ps1` creates it in the T4 track (`DEPLOY.T4.REPLICA`), and **DONE** needs its estate and metrics (`HUB.ESTATE.T4.REPLICA`, `METRICS.T4.REPLICA`). `start-demo.ps1` creates it for each session once T4 runs, about 9 minutes within the warm-up (`START.T4.REPLICA`; `-SkipT4Replica` leaves it out), and `stop-demo.ps1` deletes it before T4 stops (`STOP.T4.REPLICA`), so it bills only during sessions and has no 7-day restart. `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4.REPLICA`), `validate.ps1`, `reset-demo.ps1`, and `destroy.ps1` handle it.
  - **Its size falls back like T4's:** only `SkuNotAvailable` moves it on, to T4's size in another zone, then the documented sizes with at least T4's vCores, after a refused attempt's `Dropping` shell is gone. Every change is a **Warning** with both hourly prices and the difference (`DEPLOY.T4.REPLICA.FALLBACK`, `START.T4.REPLICA.FALLBACK`), on the console, the status page, and **DONE**. When Azure refuses every size, `deploy.ps1` ends **NOT DONE** with the fix, and `start-demo.ps1` starts the session without it, with a **Warning**. A Burstable T4 is **Not supported**, with the fix.
  - **Azure's power order** (`SourceReplicasStartStopOrder`, live): a stopped replica starts before T4, and a running one stops after T4, in `deploy.ps1`, `start-demo.ps1`, the nightly safety stop, and the on-demand control, whose roles already allow it. The control never creates a replica, and a run without one says so plainly (since 2026-11-14, a Run all session creates and deletes it, below).
  - **Older environments:** until a plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1` adds the replica, `start-demo.ps1`, `stop-demo.ps1`, `status.ps1`, and `validate.ps1` say "T4's read replica isn't deployed yet: run ./scripts/deploy.ps1" and change nothing.
  - **The `replica-lag` scenario:** a bounded T4 incident (10 minutes by default, 15 at most) in which a `ContosoCare.Demo/replica-lag` report on the replica holds back the replay of T4's changes, so Read replica lag climbs to about `holdSeconds` (240 by default), then drops: `./scripts/demo-control.ps1 -Target T4 -Scenario incident:replica-lag -Duration 10`. The runner takes `--replica-server` (`profile run --t4-replica-server` on T1), and `stop` and `reset` end the demo's sessions on the replica too.
  - **Cost:** the estimate counts the replica's compute, storage, and Defender plans for a session's running hours only, $0.361 an hour without Defender at `Standard_D4ds_v5` list prices (dated example, 2026-11-13): the default session goes from $10.61 to $12.42, and a first deployment's worst-case month from $312.21 to $315.82, both within the ceilings. `estimate-cost.ps1` shows the replica's share of a session, and a session over `sessionCeilingUsd` names it and `-SkipT4Replica`. T4's one-time move to `Standard_D4ds_v5` is still decided without it.
  - **The history:** `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` and `deploy.ps1`'s history snapshot read T4's and the replica's Azure Monitor metrics before the replica is deleted (a deleted replica's metrics are gone, live) and pass them to `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` as `POSTGRES_METRICS`. The notebook's new PostgreSQL section keeps them in `telemetry_snapshots` and `analysis_findings` (a lag of 60 seconds or more is a Warning), `SM_DatabaseHub_History` adds them, and the Insights app's new **PostgreSQL** page charts the replica's lag and T4's health. When the notebook run doesn't save them, for example while the Fabric capacity is paused, `stop-demo.ps1` keeps them in `out/history-pending` and says so (`STOP.T4.HISTORY.PENDING`), and `configure-fabric.ps1 -RunLiveActivity -PostgresMetricsPath <file>` adds them later; without that parameter, `-RunLiveActivity` sends T4's current metrics too.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.T4Replica.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.PostgresHistory.Tests.ps1` (new), the runner's `ReplicaLagTests`, and updated cost, preflight, track, lifecycle, teardown, runbook, control, VM, notebook, and app tests. Docs: the README, ADR 0025, deployment, cost management, limitations, troubleshooting, the walkthrough, the automation support matrix, and the Fabric guides ([R824](docs/implementation-report.md#risk-register) to R831).
  - **Live findings, fixed the same day:**
    - A new deployment's replica went out while T4 was still restarting for `max_connections`, because the restart's pending flag cleared first, and Azure refused it (`ReadReplicaServerInUnexpectedState`). The replica now waits for T4 to read Ready, and a refusal of that kind is retried at the same size, at most 3 times, never taken for a size refusal.
    - An environment's upgrade resumed past the new Replica step, which the status page showed as "Finished in an earlier run". A resume now runs it until the environment has the replica.
    - After a replica Azure refused, the Replica step still recorded that the environment had the replica's lifecycle, so the next resume skipped it too. The record now waits until the replica is created (or isn't supported, on a Burstable T4), and the resume runs the step again on T4's row.
    - An upgrade that resumed an unfinished run skipped the template, so T1's settings, which came from the older template's outputs, didn't have the replica's name, and the runner refused `replica-lag` ("T4's read replica isn't known"). A resume now adds the name and FQDN to T1's settings from those outputs, as the template does.
    - The replica's metric reader stopped T4's Replica step right after the replica was created (a private directory prefix over the 20 characters allowed), and dropped every point (a list piped whole), so `METRICS.T4.REPLICA` would always have read no data; both are fixed, with a test that runs the reader.
    - `stop-demo.ps1` said the replica "isn't there, so there's nothing to delete" after deleting it itself; it now says it was deleted.
    - `demo-control.ps1` said **Pass** for `replica-lag` twice while T1's profile host couldn't run it: once while the safety stop's 19:30 pass had stopped T4 and its replica, and once while T1's settings didn't name the replica. Only the runner's log on `sqlvm01` said so. Now it sends `replica-lag` only when T4 and its replica are **Ready** and set up, and otherwise says why with the fix: `CONTROL.T4` is a **Warning** naming `start-demo.ps1` or `deploy.ps1`, or **Not supported** on a Burstable T4. `sqlvm01` refuses to store the profile when T1's settings don't name the replica, and `demo-control.ps1` reports that as a **Warning** naming `deploy.ps1`. The on-demand control's dispatches reach the same refusal on `sqlvm01`, but the control doesn't wait for their result, so its output doesn't show it yet: the notebook orchestration's design covers that.
- **The nightly stop waits for a `deploy.ps1` that's still running, and still stops the demo the same night** (2026-11-14, the coordinator's decision for the user, who reviews it before any merge; [ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md#amendment-the-busy-marker-so-the-nightly-stop-doesnt-cut-off-a-run-2026-11-14)). Live on 2026-11-13, the 19:00 stop deallocated a new user's VMs and stopped T4 in the middle of `deploy.ps1`, and the 19:30 check stopped T4 and its replica after the next run's checks; a first deployment takes about 91 minutes, so one started after about 17:30 met it, on `main` too.
  - **The busy marker:** while `deploy.ps1` runs, a process of its own, with its own subscription guard, keeps the demo resource group's tags `DbHubBusyUntilUtc` (60 minutes ahead, moved on every 10 minutes) and `DbHubBusyBy` (`deploy`). `DbHub-Stop`'s `Nightly`, `T3Check`, and `T4` passes then change nothing and say `Deferred`, with who's busy and until when; the job completes. A marker more than 3 hours ahead, expired, or not a time is ignored, and an abandoned run's marker expires within an hour. The safety stop's role doesn't change: it already reads the group.
  - **The same night:** when a pass falls inside the run, `dbhub-safety-deferred` runs `DbHub-Stop -Scope Nightly` hourly from the marker's end until the next morning, and the VMs' DevTest Labs auto-shutdown is moved past the run (read, then written back whole with the new time; never disabled), and moved again after a template sets it back. A run that ends after the stop's time keeps the demo up for 60 minutes; then the deferred pass and the auto-shutdown stop it. `DEPLOY.NIGHTLYSTOP` (a **Warning**), **DONE** and **NOT DONE**, and the status page's new **Nightly stop** row say when, with `./scripts/start-demo.ps1` to keep it running tonight and `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` to stop it now. A run that ends before the stop puts everything back; Ctrl+C expires the marker at once.
  - **Sessions and status:** `start-demo.ps1` (`START.NIGHTLYSTOP`) takes over from a run the stop waited for, but leaves a `deploy.ps1` that's still running alone; `stop-demo.ps1` (`STOP.NIGHTLYSTOP`) settles it too; both make no Azure call without a marker. `status.ps1`'s new `STATUS.NIGHTLYSTOP` shows the marker, the deferred pass, and a moved auto-shutdown, and warns about one that no run explains. `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP` and the cost estimate's restart reading count a `Deferred` run as no failure.
  - **Existing users:** the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` publishes the new `DbHub-Stop`, and the control's runbooks, as it does whenever they differ. Nothing to do by hand.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.SafetyStop.Tests.ps1` (a fake Azure with the group's tags and the VMs' auto-shutdown schedules), `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`. Docs: the README, ADR 0024, troubleshooting, deployment, and `AGENTS.md`.
- **The showcase runs the `replica-lag` beat by itself** (2026-11-14, for the notebook orchestration's one Run all; [ADR 0010](docs/adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md)): in T4's even cycles, from minute 20 for 9 minutes, holding each report on the replica for 180 seconds, so Database Hub's Read replica lag climbs to about 3 minutes twice, 50 minutes after T4's profile is set. A slot's new `replica` flag decides it on T1's profile host, which knows whether T4's read replica is configured (`--t4-replica-server`): a host without one keeps `missing-index` in every cycle, and one with it runs `missing-index` in the odd cycles only. The next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` sends T1 the new runner. Tests: `CatalogTests` and `ProfileSchedulerTests`.
- **The control's runbook runs a whole session** (2026-11-14, [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md), approved by the coordinator for the user, who reviews it before any merge): `session` (the showcase on every target, the busy marker, T4's read replica as soon as T4 is Ready at T4's size or the next one Azure accepts, the replica-lag windows, and a backup auto-stop), `capture` (T4's and the replica's metrics), and `stop` with a session (the metrics first, then the VMs, the replica deleted, T4, and the marker cleared), each step in plain words with its fix in the control vault's `dbhub-session-status`, the metrics in `dbhub-session-metrics`. The control's role gets five actions, and the new **DbHub Demo Session Writer** two, at the two session secrets only; missing grants are **Warnings** with `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1`, which now grants them with the safety stop's. Tests: `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1` (with Windows PowerShell 5.1 cross-checks) and `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`.
- **Every `deploy.ps1` sets up the session control** (2026-11-14, [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#in-every-deployment)), for a new environment and an existing one, so the control notebook's Run all is there after one deploy: the Azure part beside the nightly safety stop while the tracks run, the notebook after the Fabric track (nothing in Fabric changes while the capacity isn't Active, and the next run does it), and never a **Fail**. `DEPLOY.CONTROL`, the status page's **Session control** row, and **DONE** say where Run all is, or what it still needs and who does it. A control set up before is adopted as it is; an older configuration's `"enabled": false` no longer turns it off (`DEPLOY.CONTROL.ON`). A new control's workspace is named after the demo's ("Database Hub Demo Control"), so environments in one tenant don't share "DBHub Control"; `control.roleNameSuffix` gives an environment its own two custom roles, which the whole subscription otherwise shares. Preflight's P2 registers `Microsoft.Automation`, `Microsoft.KeyVault`, and `Microsoft.ManagedIdentity`. `start-demo.ps1` and `stop-demo.ps1` remove a session's backup auto-stop too. A role that already has exactly its approved actions isn't written again, so a Contributor's run says nothing about it. Tests: `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`.
- **Run all in the control notebook runs a whole demo session** (2026-11-14, [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md); user-directed: "I just want to go to one notebook and execute one thing"). `NB_DatabaseHub_Control` needs nothing edited, and no `CONFIRM`. It says when the session ends and why: before the VMs' auto-shutdown, in whole 30-minute showcase cycles, with `autoShutdownTime` as the fix; it never checks, starts, or pauses the Fabric capacity, which runs it. Then it starts the session through the control's runbook and follows each step. It prints the presenter's guide: Database Hub's Overview, Estate, and Performance, the Insights app, the minute each incident shows, and the Database Agent's prompts with this session's names. At the session's end it stops it through the runbook and, once the telemetry has arrived, runs `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` in its own Spark session (a reference run in the demo workspace), with T4's and the replica's metrics, so the history gets the session's row and its beats. No second Spark job starts, so even an F2 or F4 capacity has nothing to refuse: `deploy.ps1` attaches the demo's lakehouse as the notebook's default lakehouse for that (`CONTROL.FABRIC.LAKEHOUSE`), and without it the history runs as a job, whose refusal by a full capacity (HTTP 430) the notebook prints with the fix ([ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#the-historys-spark-session-2026-11-14)). A Run all during a session follows that session rather than starting another; one after a session whose history wasn't saved saves it right after its own start is sent. Stop now and Reset stay as their own cells. `deploy.ps1` fills the notebook's environment values, the showcase's slots, and the session's rules when it imports it. Tests: `Fabric.ControlNotebook.Tests.ps1` runs the notebook's code against a fake vault, runbook, `notebookutils`, Fabric, and clock, and `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1` the lakehouse binding.

### Changed

- **`destroy.ps1` deletes everything `deploy.ps1` created for the environment** (2026-11-17, the project's coordinator, from the user's standing preferences: one command deletes it all). It used to leave the session control's resource group, both Fabric workspaces, the T3 administrators' group, and the custom roles, without a word. Now it reads, before it asks, what else is this environment's, lists what goes and what stays in its confirmation (`-Force` skips it), and deletes each part only when it can tell: the session control, when its runbooks serve this environment's resource group (`DESTROY.CONTROL`); the demo's Fabric workspace, when every item in it is one the demo creates, and the control's, when its control notebook serves this environment, with its managed private endpoint (`DESTROY.FABRIC.WORKSPACE`, `DESTROY.FABRIC.CONTROL`); the T3 group, when init.ps1 created it for this environment (`DESTROY.ENTRA.GROUP`); and a custom role definition, once no role assignment holds it (`DESTROY.SAFETYSTOP.ROLE`, and the control's in `DESTROY.CONTROL.ROLES`). Anything else stays, and its result says why. `-KeepFabric` keeps the workspaces and the history. A part that fails is a **Warning** with its fix while the others go on, and a rerun finishes them. The Fabric capacity and your licenses are never deleted. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Teardown.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`. R874.
- **`destroy.ps1` never deletes a Fabric workspace another environment might use** (2026-11-17, the coordinator's decision; branch only). Two environments can name the same workspace, and R874's checks read only what's in it. Now each `deploy.ps1` records the workspaces its environment uses on its resource group, on every run, so an upgrade records them too (`DbHubFabricWorkspaceId`, `DbHubControlWorkspaceId`), and warns when another environment records the same one (`DEPLOY.FABRIC.SHARED`, a **Warning** that doesn't block; the fix is each environment's own `fabric.workspaceName` and `control.fabricWorkspaceName`). `destroy.ps1` deletes a workspace only when one Azure Resource Graph query across the subscriptions you can read finds no other demo environment that records it, and none without the record, since one deployed before then might use it; the golden group and the session controls' groups don't count. Otherwise, and when Resource Graph can't be read, the workspace stays, as a **Warning** that names the other environment, with the fix. With one environment, everything still goes in one command. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`. R877.
- **`deploy.ps1` deletes T4's read replica at its end, unless a session uses it** (2026-11-16, the project's coordinator for the user, who can reverse it before the merge; [ADR 0025](docs/adr/0025-t4-read-replica.md#amendment-deployps1-deletes-the-replica-at-its-end-2026-11-16)). A merge reaches every existing demo at its next `deploy.ps1`, and the run left the replica it created running, or stopped by the nightly safety stop, billing until a session's `stop-demo.ps1`. Now, last in every run, a **NOT DONE** one or one that stopped at a **Fail** included, once **DONE**'s checks and the history snapshot have read it, the replica is deleted (`DEPLOY.T4.REPLICA.END`, about 2 minutes), and the status page's new row **T4's read replica at the end** and **DONE**'s or **NOT DONE**'s notes say so. A replica a session uses stays, with a **Pass** that says whose and until when: a Run all session's, while its auto-stop is still to come, or a `start-demo.ps1` session's, whose mark, the replica's `DbHubSessionUntilUtc` tag until the VMs' next auto-shutdown, the session gives a new replica in its template's tags, a running one before the warm-up, and one it started once it's Ready. When the run can't tell (the control's auto-stop unreadable, or the replica being created or started) or the delete fails, the replica stays with a **Warning** and the fix: `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` when no session runs, or the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`. `-WhatIf` deletes nothing. A run's replica bills under $0.50 at the 2026-11-13 list prices. Tests: `DbHubDemo.T4Replica.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. Docs: the README (with a new section, **Upgrading an existing demo**), ADR 0025, the architecture, deployment, cost management, troubleshooting, and `AGENTS.md`.
- **The session control's role grant says what it changed** (2026-11-16, for the owner's own upgrade, whose control's operator role has `main`'s 22 actions): an existing role is updated in place, under its own ID, so its assignments stay and none is added twice, and `CONTROL.ROLE.DEFINITION` says so, with the actions it added or removed; a new one says it was created. Test: `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1` (the default, unsuffixed path).
- **Power BI viewer licenses are assumed** (2026-11-14; the user: "Make the assumption that the PBI licenses are sorted out"): `PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.VIEWERLICENSE` is **Not selected**, with the viewer licensing rule, and reads nothing, where it read the capacity's SKU and warned on every deployment below F64. `Test-DemoFabricViewerLicense` takes only the configuration now, and nothing in the demo detects, assigns, or works around licenses. The README (P11), prerequisites, presentation, permissions, limitations, the walkthrough, the automation support matrix, and ADR 0012 say so. Tests: `DbHubDemo.FabricItem.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.HubReadiness.Tests.ps1`.
- **`deploy.ps1` republishes the on-demand control's runbooks** (2026-11-13, so one `./scripts/deploy.ps1` gives an older demo's control the read replica's power order, as it does the nightly safety stop's): when the control is set up and its three runbooks differ from this code, they're published again (`CONTROL.RUNBOOKS`). When that fails, for example without rights on its Automation account, it's a **Warning** on the console, the status page, and **DONE** or **NOT DONE**, with what fails until then and the exact command, `./scripts/setup-activity-control.ps1`.
- **The Insights app deploys with your `az login`** (user-directed, 2026-11-09: "They should sign into Az login and then have everything deployed. It is okay if rayfin needs another sign in but prefer not to"). `configure-fabric-app.ps1` gives each Rayfin CLI command an Azure CLI token for its own audience as `RAYFIN_TOKEN`, in that command's process only: the Fabric API for `rayfin up` and `rayfin up status`, and the Power BI API for the connector's queries. So `deploy.ps1` opens no browser and waits for no sign-in, and its sign-in row is **Not selected**, "Not needed". When Fabric refuses the token for `rayfin up`, the new `FABRIC.APP.TOKEN` is a **Warning** with the reason, and the Rayfin CLI's own sign-in deploys instead. The refusal is recorded in `out/fabric-app-auth.json`, so the next run starts the browser sign-in at its beginning; `-SignIn` adds the app at once, and `-RayfinSignIn` keeps the old way. Live on 2026-11-09, `rayfin up` took the Azure CLI's token, but the connector's direct route (Power BI's `executeDaxQueries`) refused the Power BI one with HTTP 401, and the check passed only through that PC's cached Rayfin sign-in: on a new tester's machine `FABRIC.APP.CONNECTOR` would have been a **Fail** and the browser check open on every first deploy. Now the app's queries then run through Power BI's Execute Queries with the same token, which took it for all 14 queries live, never through the Rayfin CLI's own sign-in (that only with `-RayfinSignIn`); the evidence names the route, Execute Queries' one query per request and missing row bound are handled (the app's bound is compared in the script), and nothing is recorded for a later run, so no browser opens because of it. When Execute Queries refuses the token too, `FABRIC.APP.CONNECTOR` is a **Fail** with the fix for Power BI's answer. `FABRIC.APP.BROWSER` is now a note (**Not selected**, "Not needed") when the app is deployed and every query it runs returned its data under your identity, and the note says exactly what was verified and that the app's own route in the browser can't be checked from a script; it no longer claims the queries went "through the connector the app uses". The README, `AGENTS.md`, the FAQ, the deployment guide, `deploy.ps1`'s help, the start banner, `init.ps1`'s next steps, and the status page's sign-in row no longer say a browser opens at the start of every run ([ADR 0023](docs/adr/0023-first-run-automation.md#amendment-the-azure-clis-tokens-for-the-rayfin-cli-2026-11-09-user-directed); R729 to R735).
- **One click, with no SQL Server setup wizard, for a first deployment, a redeploy, and an upgrade** (user-directed, 2026-11-09: "They should not need to manually do a wizard. They should sign into Az login and then have everything deployed."): the first run no longer asks how T2 gets SQL Server (`JumpstartImage`, unless `init.ps1 -T2SqlInstallMode Developer`), and `PaygWizard` is retired from the one-click deployment: a configuration that says `PaygWizard`, the default an older `init.ps1` wrote before 2026-11-05, runs as `JumpstartImage`, with one line at the start and on the status page; no configuration value leads to the setup wizard. On an existing environment, the T2 host deletes a guest built in `PaygWizard` mode whose setup wizard was never finished (only on positive evidence: no SQL Server instance, no setup running, setup never completed, no guided stop waiting) and makes T2 again from Arc Jumpstart's image, about 27 minutes plus about 33 for Arc onboarding, and `deploy.ps1` deletes the old guest's Arc resources (`DEPLOY.T2.REBUILD`); a finished `PaygWizard` guest is kept as it is (`DEPLOY.T2.MODE`), and so is its golden snapshot. When the image can't be used, the fix lists a rerun, then `ProductKey` or `Developer` ([ADR 0006](docs/adr/0006-sql-editions-and-licensing.md#amendment-older-demos-upgrade-in-one-click-2026-11-09); [an older demo](docs/deployment.md#an-older-demo-a-plain-deployps1-upgrades-it-2026-11-09)).
- **T4 (PostgreSQL) is part of every deployment** (user-directed, 2026-11-09: "making sure postgres is deployed with the deployment and not being an optional component"): `t4Enabled: false` is ignored, with one line at the start and on the status page; **DONE** always needs T4's estate and metrics; the size fallback's cost check and autonomous tuning only report (**Warning**), so neither leaves T4 out; no `None` or `Refused` record leaves it out any more, so every run tries the sizes again (`DEPLOY.T4.RETRY`); only when Azure refuses every size does the run deploy the rest and end **NOT DONE** with the fix, a Region access request or `t4SkuName` (`DEPLOY.T4.SKU`, `DEPLOY.T4.OFF`); and an environment without T4, such as one from before Phase 24, gets it on the next plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1` (`DEPLOY.T4.MISSING` when it would resume) ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-09-user-directed-t4-is-part-of-every-deployment)).
- **A `t4SkuName` Azure refuses falls back too, cheapest first, and every automatic size change says what it cost** (user-directed, 2026-11-09: "Yes, fall back automatically, cheapest first, with a Warning (Recommended)", then "make sure to add a notification of it though to say what you tried to deploy and what was deployed included cost difference"). When Azure refuses a size `t4SkuName` sets with `SkuNotAvailable` while T4's server doesn't exist, `deploy.ps1` tries it once more in another zone the region lists, then the next size Azure accepts, cheapest first: the sizes that cost as much or more, then the cheaper ones, nearest first (`Standard_B1ms`: `Standard_B2s`, `Standard_D2ds_v5`, `Standard_D4ds_v5`, `Standard_D4ads_v5`; a 4-vCore size tries its peer first), each after its cost check, which only reports. Without `t4SkuName`, the order is as it was. Every automatic size change, the default's included, ends with `DEPLOY.T4.FALLBACK`, a **Warning** that never blocks **DONE**: the size asked for and every size Azure refused, with the zones tried, the size deployed, and T4's monthly estimate at both, light use and worst case, with the difference in USD, labeled as estimates from the Azure Retail Prices API. The console says it, the status page's Infrastructure and T4 rows (`T4.SIZE`) and the **DONE** or **NOT DONE** summary show it, and `status.ps1` says it afterwards (`STATUS.T4.SIZE`, priced again). The resource group's new `DbHubT4Refused` tag records what was refused with `DbHubT4Size`, so later runs keep that server's size, with no resize, restart, or refusal again, while the configuration asks for the size Azure refused; a `t4SkuName` that names another size moves T4 to it. Only a refusal of every size ends the run **NOT DONE**, naming every size. Preflight makes a refused or unlisted `t4SkuName` a **Warning** and starts where the region allows ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-09-user-directed-a-size-t4skuname-sets-falls-back-too-and-every-automatic-size-change-says-what-it-cost)).
- **The first deployment's times are measured** (live, 2026-11-06): a clean first deployment from a fresh clone of `main`, in the default `JumpstartImage` mode (West US 3, `lowest-cost`), ended **DONE** in 91 minutes with nobody needed: preflight about 4.5 minutes, the template 12, T1's bootstraps about 6, T2's host build about 27 (the image's download and verification, its first boot and cleanup, and the image check), T2's Arc onboarding with the SQL Server extension, PAYG, and monitoring about 33, and the validation about 4. In an earlier run, the 34.1 GiB image downloaded and verified in about 11 minutes, about 51 MiB/s, bound by the host's disk. The README, the deployment guide, troubleshooting, the FAQ, limitations, cost management, the architecture, ADRs 0003, 0006, 0018, and 0023, the deployment notebook, the start banner, the status page's T2 label, and `init.ps1`'s next steps give these times instead of the estimates (about 1 to 2 hours, and 30 to 60 or 20 to 40 minutes for T2); estimates stay, labeled, for what isn't measured. The deployment guide and troubleshooting list the results a clean first deployment shows besides **Pass**, none of which blocks **DONE**: T4's `max_connections` waiting for a restart, a chunk Run Command that Azure was still deleting, T3 without the free offer, the history report empty until the notebook's first snapshots, and the Insights app's one-time browser confirmation.
- **`t2SqlInstallMode` defaults to `JumpstartImage`** (user-directed, 2026-11-05; `PaygWizard` before): `init.ps1` and the first run offer it first, also without keyboard input, and `PaygWizard`, `ProductKey`, and `Developer` stay as opt-in modes. **An existing environment:** a configuration that names its mode keeps it; one without `t2SqlInstallMode` now gets `JumpstartImage`, so its `PaygWizard` golden snapshot isn't used and its existing host refuses the new mode (`GuestSource`) with the fix: add `"t2SqlInstallMode": "PaygWizard"` to keep it, or destroy and deploy again for the new mode.
  - **No T2 step needs a person, and no Bastion is needed to deploy, in the default mode;** Bastion stays optional for interactive access, and `PREFLIGHT.P3.PUBLICIP`'s message says so. The golden snapshot is optional, and `destroy.ps1`'s golden guard stays `PaygWizard`-only.
