  - A first deployment is estimated at about 1 to 2 hours in `JumpstartImage` mode (not measured yet), against about 2 to 3 hours in `PaygWizard` mode; the start banner, the status page's T2 steps, and the T2 phase estimate (30 to 60 minutes for the host, the download, and the first boot) follow the mode.
  - The README (At a glance, the steps, now 9, the guided stop as an opt-in, the modes, Codespaces, the AI assistant, automated vs manual, prerequisites, the quick start, cost, clean up, and sign-in), the docs, the ADRs, and the VM READMEs follow, with dated cost examples from the Azure Retail Prices API (2026-11-05): no new Azure resource, a free download into Azure, Bastion about $0.20 an hour no longer needed to deploy, and the golden snapshot at most $6.40 a month in the estimate ([cost management](docs/cost-management.md#t2s-jumpstartimage-mode-2026-11-05)).
- The README starts with "Deploy it, step by step", right after "At a glance": where the demo deploys (your subscription and resource groups, your Fabric workspace, and where you run the scripts), what to line up first, and ten numbered steps from getting the code to removing it.
- Phase 13: `stop-demo.ps1` and the scheduled stop set every activity profile to `off`, and the VMs no longer restart the configured profile at every boot, so a VM start alone doesn't keep T3's serverless database awake (R183).
- Phase 13: Run Commands busy with a policy-installed extension, such as an `MDE.Windows` update, are retried for about 12.5 minutes instead of about 4.5, and a deallocated VM fails at once with a message to start it (R49).
- Phase 13: after a template deployment, T1's monitoring check treats the SQL IaaS extension's `Updating` state as a wait, not a failure.
- Phase 13: `docs/deployment.md` keeps a by-hand fallback next to Phase 7's `GuestWebView2` step (the WebView2 standalone x64 installer, signature checked; the bootstrapper hangs behind the T2 NSG), offers `deploy.ps1 -Resume` after the setup wizard, and documents the stale Run Command retry.
- Phase 13 guard update (2026-10-21, coordinator decision for the owner): the tooling no longer depends on the Azure CLI's shared default subscription, and nobody needs to change it.
  - `Assert-DemoAzContext` (`PREFLIGHT.GUARD.CONTEXT`) checks `az account show --subscription <id>` (tenant, and the new optional `azureAccount`; a **Warning** when it isn't set), then runs read-only, time-limited canaries: an Azure Resource Manager read, `az rest` on the subscription's fully qualified URL, and Resource Manager, Fabric, Kusto, and Microsoft Graph tokens whose `tid` and `oid` claims are checked locally. It fails closed, names the failed check, never mentions `az account set`, and caches the validated context for the process.
  - `Invoke-DemoAz` adds `--subscription` to `az rest` and token requests too, and refuses `--ids`, the `{subscriptionId}` placeholder, any other subscription in any argument, `az ad`, `az rest` outside `https://management.azure.com/subscriptions/<id>`, token requests with `--tenant`, role assignments by `--assignee` or without `--assignee-principal-type`, `config set`, and `cloud set`; writes and token requests wait for the guard. A token request has a 120-second time limit (`Invoke-DemoAzCli -TimeoutSeconds` stops the whole process tree).
  - Tokens come from `Get-DemoAccessToken` (`--subscription`, never `--tenant`, which takes the default account's identity). Microsoft Graph (the P6 checks, T1's group membership, principal types), Resource Graph, and built-in and management group policy definitions go through `Invoke-DemoRestRequest` with a verified token, because `az rest` and `az ad` use the default account for those URLs.
  - The GitHub workflows set `azureAccount` to `AZURE_CLIENT_ID`; `docs/prerequisites.md` documents the optional separate `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR`.
- Phase 14: `COST.AUTOSHUTDOWN` reads the auto-shutdown schedules by resource group, name, and type instead of `--ids`, which bypasses the `--subscription` that `Invoke-DemoAz` adds and which Phase 13's guard update refuses.
- Phase 14: when the subscription guard fails, `validate.ps1`'s remediation points to the guard's own message instead of telling you to fix the Azure CLI context; with Phase 13's guard update, you don't need to change your default subscription.
- Phase 14, with Phase 13's guard update: `validate.ps1` gives the guard the configured `azureAccount`, and the commands its remediations suggest name `--subscription`; `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL` suggests `az resource delete` by name and type instead of `--ids`.
- Phase 17: `README.md` is the front door (what the demo is, prerequisites, the one deploy command, running a demo, cleanup, and cost). The FAQ, limitations (preview disclosures first), cost management (dated examples, and the Fabric capacity outside the ceiling, which the notebooks, the Real-Time Dashboard, and the Power BI report need), and prerequisites (a checklist, and the pinned .NET SDK) are final, and the other docs reflect the live results of 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21.
- Phase 17: sign-in never touches the shared default subscription: the docs say to sign in only when needed and to press Enter at the `az login` subscription selector, and troubleshooting lists the subscription guard's checks with their fixes.
- Phase 17: the automation support matrix's final pass justifies every manual step with a Microsoft Learn citation and adds rows for `GuestWebView2`, `GuestSignInCleanup`, T1's `AssociatedServices`, and the presenter-driven Hub UI; the notebook import row records the live import and the paused-capacity case; the Azure CLI sign-in is listed as a by-design manual step; rows 99 and later are reserved for the presentation layer.
- Phase 17: `configure-monitoring.ps1 -Target T3` explains that T3's monitoring is its extended property, which `deploy.ps1`'s T3 stage sets, instead of pointing to Phase 9; its unit test checks that result instead of the old `TODO(phase9)` hook.
- Phase 17: the walkthrough's "What's ready" table lists `validate.ps1` as implemented, now that Phase 14 is merged.
- Phase 17: `config/demo.config.example.json` has all-zero placeholders for the Fabric workspace and lakehouse IDs, so a fork never points at the owner's workspace. By the owner's decision the IDs live only in the notebooks; the docs say "the demo workspace (its ID is in the notebook metadata)".
- Phase 17: the README, FAQ, troubleshooting, limitations, and cost management state no deployment or start times; Phase 20 adds the measured numbers.
- Phase 18: `Import-DemoFabricNotebook` runs on the generic `Import-DemoFabricItem`, with the same records and messages; a paused workspace capacity (HTTP 404 `CapacityNotActive`) is **Manual action required** for every Fabric item import, as Phase 12 made it for notebooks.
- Phase 18: `Invoke-DemoHubReadiness` adds `PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.VIEWERLICENSE` after the P5 checks, reusing the capacity SKU that `FABRIC.P5.CAPACITY` read.
- Phase 19: `Get-DemoAccessToken` also accepts the Power BI REST API audience, `https://analysis.windows.net/powerbi/api`, as the only resource with a path; the tenant, caller, time-limit, and SecureString checks are unchanged.
- Phase 19: `config/versions.json` pins Node.js 26 and the Power BI authoring and Desktop CLIs (`@microsoft/powerbi-report-authoring-cli` 0.5.0, `@microsoft/powerbi-desktop-bridge-cli` 1.0.0).
- Phase 16: `docs/cleanup.md`, ADRs 0002 and 0016, and the implementation report record the test environment's live results of 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22: the measured time to ready per target, T2's onboarding after the DNS fix, the stop, the destroy with its residual check, and the cost of the cycle.
- Phase 19b: `config/versions.json` records the Fabric App's pins (`fabricApp`: the Rayfin CLI and SDK 1.36.2, Node.js 20, 22, or 24 with 24 in CI, the template commit, the item and connector names, and the two tenant settings) and `actions/setup-node` v7.1.0; `tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1` gains the `FabricApp` check.
- Phase 19b: `docs/presentation.md`, prerequisites, permissions, limitations, the security model, cost management, and troubleshooting cover the Insights app: history versus live, opening it from its workspace item, the one-time Rayfin CLI sign-in, viewer access, capacity units, and the hosted URL's preview sign-in limitation.
- Phase 20: preflight reuses a complete policy inventory while the assignments that apply are unchanged (`-PolicyCacheHours`, 168 in the scripts; the reused results say so), and reads the region's SKUs in one Resource SKUs REST call (seconds, where each `az vm list-skus` took about 100 seconds).
- Phase 20: `start-demo.ps1` starts both VMs at once, and `-WarmUpMinutes` no longer blocks: the activity runs in the background (`START.WARMUP`), and `-WaitForWarmUp`, which the scheduled workflows pass, keeps the wait and the telemetry check. A golden snapshot's source host gets its air-gapped guest back (`T2.GOLDEN.SOURCE`).
- Phase 20: the Arc onboarding decision never deletes an Azure machine that's Connected while the guest's agent has no identity (`StaleMachineConnected`, **Fail**): another server checks in under that name.
- Phase 20: a restored guest's Release starts SQL Server right away instead of waiting for its delayed automatic start.
- Phase 21: the README's front door, below the opening statement: what's deployed, with a Mermaid diagram; one table of every step, automated or manual, with the documented reason, the time, and who does it; the prerequisites and how each is checked; screenshots and real command output, redacted; a quick start with measured times (Phase 20's redeploy with the T2 golden snapshot, 38.8 minutes end to end with no manual step, next to Phase 16's sequential fresh build); and the four presentation surfaces.
- Phase 22: a faster parallel redeploy ([ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md#amendments-2026-10-23-phase-22-the-follow-up-overlaps)). The track processes start right after Preflight, so their subscription guards run while the template deploys, and wait for its result through a private handoff directory; the bundle and the runner are built while Preflight runs. A golden T2 starts its Restore as soon as the template has deployed `archost01`, and its bundle and runner travel while the Release runs (`Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand -WhileRunning`). T1's bundle and runner go in one wave (`Send-DemoChunkSet`), T1's monitoring is merged right after its loader, and T1 joins the T3 group during the template and sets T3 up first (new T1 step `T3Setup`, stage T3; `DEPLOY.T3.EARLYSETUP` **Warning** when the T3 step had to run it again). Measured live on 2026-10-23: 63.7 minutes end to end, with no check **Fail**: T1 and T3 ready at +26.8 minutes (9.5 sooner than Phase 20) and T2 at +61.7, after about 29.5 minutes of the SQL Server extension's download in its guest (R72); the 30-minute target isn't met.
- Phase 22: the README's at-a-glance line, status, and quick-start times take Phase 22's measured redeploy (Phase 20's 38.8 minutes stays as the comparison, and the repeat start stays 481 seconds), and a new "In progress (not yet available)" section lists T4 on Azure Database for PostgreSQL flexible server (Phase 24), an on-demand activity notebook triggered through Azure Automation, and more scenario types (Phase 25b). Deployment tells operators to keep the machine awake while `deploy.ps1` runs.
- Phase 25c: the Phase 25b proposal note became [ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md), with the owner's decisions of 2026-10-26; [ADR 0002](docs/adr/0002-resource-group-topology.md#phase-25c-addendum-the-on-demand-activity-control-2026-10-26) records that `rg-<prefix>-control` also holds the control. The README explains the control and why it may need a second workspace (a managed private endpoint turns off starter pools for its whole workspace), and the "In progress" list says what's built but not proven live. Permissions, prerequisites, security model, cost management, cleanup, deployment, architecture, limitations, troubleshooting, and references cover it.
- Phase 25c: `deploy.ps1` grants the control's A1 again after the Infrastructure stage when `assignRoles` and `control.enabled` are true, and otherwise lists the `-GrantRole` command in `DEPLOY.SUMMARY` as **Manual action required**; the Security area accepts A1 at the demo group. `stop-demo.ps1` removes a notebook run's pending auto-stop (`STOP.CONTROL.AUTOSTOP`), and `start-demo.ps1` cancels one, because a local session takes over, with a **Warning** that says until when the VMs now run (`START.CONTROL.AUTOSTOP`); it also waits up to 10 minutes for a run's `DbHub-Stop` job that's queued or running, and never stops it (`START.CONTROL.STOPJOB`). `Import-DemoFabricNotebook` and `Import-DemoFabricItem` take `-FolderId`.
- Phase 26a: both notebooks hold no Fabric IDs. Their parameters cell has empty `WORKSPACE_ID` and `LAKEHOUSE_ID`, which mean the session's own workspace and default lakehouse; the import binds the uploaded copy to the resolved ones, and every scripted run passes them (`Invoke-DemoFabricNotebookRun`). Tests fail on a Fabric ID anywhere in the repository.
- Phase 26a: `configure-fabric.ps1`, `configure-powerbi.ps1`, `configure-fabric-app.ps1`, `validate.ps1`, and `setup-activity-control.ps1` resolve the workspace by name first; the example configuration holds names only.
- Phase 26a: both notebooks' `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` defaults to the public repository, `fabric-database-hub-demo`, and the import sets it and the live-activity notebook's `GITHUB_ENVIRONMENT` from `github.repository` and `github.environment` unless the repository is still the example's placeholder.
- Phase 26b: the README's quick start is the seamless first run (`az login`, `init.ps1 -CreateAdminGroup`, `preflight.ps1`, `deploy.ps1`), and its "Automated vs manual" table, prerequisites, deployment, and the automation support matrix say what `init.ps1`, `-CreateAdminGroup`, `-SignIn`, `fabric.manageCapacity`, and `-SaveHistory` automate, and what stays manual and why. The dev container pins Node.js 24 (`node:2`), PowerShell 7.4 (`powershell:2`), the .NET SDK, and Bicep to `config/versions.json`, and asks for a 4-core machine (`hostRequirements`).
- Phase 26c: the live workflows read `AZURE_CLIENT_ID`, `AZURE_TENANT_ID`, `AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`, and `DBHUB_CONFIG_JSON` from **environment secrets** instead of variables, mask every ID before `azure/login`, request an OpenID Connect token only in the job that signs in, pass `secrets: inherit`, and use the schedule environment only for scheduled runs. `setup-github-oidc.ps1` prints `gh secret set --env` commands instead of `gh variable set`.
- Phase 26c: `SECURITY.md` (private vulnerability reporting, and what a public repository means for logs and artifacts), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (the repository's rules and validation loop), and `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` (Contributor Covenant 2.1, with reporting and enforcement). Internal policy, initiative, and resource-group names in code comments and test fixtures became the facts they stood for, and the issue templates, module manifests, and example configuration name the public repository, `fabric-database-hub-demo`.
- Phase 26d: Arc onboarding uses the host identity `id-<prefix>-<env>-arc-onboard` whenever it's attached (`assignRoles`): `configure-arc.ps1`, the golden restore, and `test-t2-golden.ps1 -Onboard`, whose test group gets its own; `-UseHostIdentity` requires it and `-UseCallerToken` skips it. The agent in the guest takes the token from a protected config file (`azcmagent connect --config`), zeroed and deleted right after, and an authorization refusal with the host identity is retried while its role assignment propagates. `GuestSignInCleanup` runs in every install mode and waits while setup runs. The docs describe setup's sign-in data only in neutral words (R74).
- Phase 26e: `docs/implementation-report.md` is a concise public report: the demo's state, the acceptance checklist, the measured times, a risk register that keeps every risk number the repository cites, and a short entry per phase. The docs, the README, and this changelog name phases instead of pull request numbers, describe the tenant's policies and governance automation without their internal names, and leave out operator-specific details, and the Deploy to Azure links point to the public repository, `MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo`. No file names the archive repository any more: the JSON schemas' `$id` URLs and the Real-Time Dashboard's element IDs, name-based UUIDs of the repository's URL, follow the public repository (`RealTimeDashboard.json` regenerated), and a conventions test keeps the old name out. The neutral-wording test covers the implementation report too.
- Phase 26f: the Run Commands that receive the one-time host password or the SQL Server product key clear the VM's Windows PowerShell log when it recorded the value (Run Command passes protected parameters as arguments), reported as a **Warning**, and turn off their session's further PowerShell engine events; the sign-in data sweep also finds product-key-shaped values, so `-Clean` removes older entries. Callers accept the Warning as success ([ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#phase-26d-amendment-no-operator-token-left-anywhere-2026-10-27)).
- Phase 25d: the README, the implementation report, ADR 0010 and ADR 0020, and limitations say the eight Phase 25b scenarios and the on-demand activity control are proven live (2026-10-27), with their measured times and what isn't proven yet; the README's "In progress" section is gone, because nothing is left in it.
- Phase 28: `deploy.ps1` waits for T2 by default in every install mode, outside CI (`-T2WaitMinutes` 180, from 120; `-WaitForT2` is accepted but needed only in CI), and continues an unfinished deployment when it's run again with no parameters. Its exit code is 0 only when the run ends **DONE**: 1 when **NOT DONE** with a **Fail**, 2 otherwise; GitHub Actions keeps the earlier codes and inputs.
- Phase 28: with `networkMode` `private`, the parallel run's policy inventory runs beside the template and the tracks instead of before them; with `auto` or `public`, nothing changes (`public` keeps it first, so a policy that modifies SQL public access still stops the run before anything is created).
- Phase 28: T4's size falls back from `Standard_B1ms` to `Standard_B2s`, then to no T4 (a **Warning**, and **DONE** says so), when `t4SkuName` isn't set; preflight reads the region's sizes with `az postgres flexible-server list-skus`, and the size used is recorded in the tag `DbHubT4Sku`. `t4SkuName` has no schema default any more, so a size set on purpose is told apart and never changed ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-10-29-the-burstable-fallback-phase-28)).
- Phase 28: chunk Run Command deletes aren't waited for (one bounded cleanup at the end reports leftovers as a **Warning**), each VM's extensions settle before its artifacts and its loader, a watchdog saves a stuck VM's instance view and restarts it once, and a stall sends the rest one at a time ([ADR 0003](docs/adr/0003-t2-host-size-security-type-bootstrap.md#phase-28-amendment-chunk-hygiene-that-doesnt-stall-2026-10-29); R49, R336).
- Phase 28: the host password's rotation by `stop-demo.ps1` is a **Warning** with the portal's way, not a **Fail**, when it can't run on a running host; the record stays, so the next stop or start tries again. `start-demo.ps1 -Interactive -ResetAdminPassword` without a clipboard records the handover too.
- Phase 28: the README's "Deploy it, step by step" is one command and the guided stop, with the timings, the options (`ProductKey`, `Developer`), GitHub Codespaces, and the Insights app marked optional; deployment, troubleshooting ("A first run that's slow or doesn't finish", "Run Commands stuck in Deleting"), the security model, limitations, the FAQ, the architecture, and ADRs 0003, 0006, 0009, 0011, 0012, 0018, 0019, and 0023 say what changed. ADR 0018's clock now includes T4.
- Phase 28 follow-up: T4 falls back to General Purpose when Burstable is refused (the owner's decision, 2026-11-02; [ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-02-the-general-purpose-fallback-phase-28-follow-up)). It applies when `t4SkuName` isn't set and Azure refuses both `Standard_B1ms` and `Standard_B2s` with `SkuNotAvailable`.
  - **The fallback:** `deploy.ps1` creates T4 as General Purpose `Standard_D2ds_v5`, where it had finished without T4. A quota limit, a policy denial, or a transient error fails the template as before. A `t4SkuName` you set always wins, and `t4Enabled: false` turns T4 off.
  - **The cost check:** right before it creates the server, `deploy.ps1` prices it from the Azure Retail Prices API: the worst case, T4 running every hour of the month (a stopped server starts again on its own after seven days), with its storage and the Defender plans a tenant policy enforces, against `monthlyCeilingUsd`, and a session against `sessionCeilingUsd`. Over either, or without a price, T4 is left out with a note that names the ceiling and the value to raise it to. `deploy.ps1`'s preflight prices the same step ahead (`PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK`), starting from the record; a standalone `preflight.ps1` doesn't read the record. `Get-DemoCostEstimate -PriceCache` lets the estimates share one set of price reads.
  - **Said loudly:** the console, the status page's Infrastructure step, and the **DONE** or **NOT DONE** banner say "T4 created as General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5 because Burstable sizes were refused here", with the hourly list price the check read, once the server exists. Until then, the banner says the fallback chose `Standard_D2ds_v5` but T4 isn't created yet, and why.
  - **The restart date:** `stop-demo.ps1` and `status.ps1` give the date and time Azure starts a stopped T4 again (the stop plus seven days), what it then bills at its size, and whether the on-demand control's daily safety stop stops it again, read from its schedule.
  - **Kept:** `DbHubT4Sku` records `Standard_D2ds_v5`, or `Refused` when Azure refuses that too. A redeploy keeps the size and never tries B1ms again. An existing server keeps its own size, whatever the record says, unless `t4SkuName` is set, and a record that doesn't match it is cleared, so a size you set and later removed is never called the fallback's. A `None` record from Phase 28 gets the fallback, cost check first, on the next run that deploys the template. `start-demo.ps1`'s recreation of T3 no longer tries to create a T4 that the record leaves out.
  - **Not proven live:** the owner chose to merge after the independent review and a free preflight check (2026-11-02: `PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK` **Pass** in West US 3, priced from the Retail Prices API with prices read live on 2026-11-02, within both ceilings). The create path (B1ms, then B2s refused, then `Standard_D2ds_v5` created) and keeping the size on a redeploy haven't run live; the first real fallback run should be watched (R601).
  - **The docs:** the README, cost management, deployment, troubleshooting, limitations, the architecture, the automation support matrix, and ADRs 0018 and 0019 say what changed. The template's descriptions say that storage, backups, and high availability are valid on both tiers, and `azuredeploy.json` was rebuilt.
- Phase 28 follow-up: `business-day` is the default activity profile, so Database Hub has something to show (the owner's decision, 2026-11-03; [ADR 0010](docs/adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md#activity-profiles-business-day-by-default-2026-11-03)). A tester found Performance's problem panels empty (**High CPU resources over time**, **CPU spikes over time**, **Databases limited by service tier**): they list only resources with that problem, and `baseline` never causes one.
  - **The default:** the schema, the example configuration that `init.ps1` copies, `demo-control.yml`, and the live-activity notebook. An explicit `"activityProfile": "baseline"` or `-ActivityProfile baseline` still wins; a configuration that `init.ps1` wrote before keeps its `baseline`. `Get-DemoConfig` leaves `activityProfile` unset, so a run without it says once, on the console and as `PREFLIGHT.ACTIVITYPROFILE`, `DEPLOY.ACTIVITYPROFILE`, or `START.ACTIVITYPROFILE`, that it uses the new default; no script writes the configuration.
  - **No incident slows a run's own Run Commands:** `deploy.ps1` (both paths) and `start-demo.ps1` set `baseline` first and switch to `business-day` once their own Run Commands on a VM are done, after **DONE**'s checks or at the warm-up's start, with one managed Run Command per VM, `DbHubDemo-Profile`, both VMs within 3 minutes (`DEPLOY.INCIDENTS_*`, `START.INCIDENTS_*`; never a **Fail**). A run that's stopped (Ctrl+C), fails, or ends **NOT DONE** still switches; the first two without waiting. A VM is forgotten only once Azure accepts its switch request, so a Ctrl+C in the middle of the switch leaves the VMs it hadn't reached to that send, which says what it sent. A VM that's already running when the run starts gets `baseline` first, without waiting, so an incident an earlier session left running ends early in the run. A VM the run starts gets it as soon as it runs, so a VM that boots doesn't resume a stored `business-day` under the run's own Run Commands: T3's and T4's, files on T1, at once, and T1's or T2's tried again there every 15 seconds while its SQL Server, or T2's guest, starts, for at most 10 minutes. The run's own Run Commands there wait for it (`START.INTERIM_*`, `DEPLOY.INTERIM_*`: **Pass**, **Not selected**, or a **Warning** with the commands when it isn't set in time). The template's install and boot profile is `baseline` in place of a profile with incidents. **DONE**'s requirements are unchanged.
  - **`stop-demo.ps1` stops on time:** the profiles go off on both VMs at once, waited for at most 3 minutes (`Stop-DemoEnvironment -ProfileOffWaitSeconds`, 180 by default), then the guest's clean shutdown within the same bound, then the deallocation anyway; a profile that isn't off in time is a **Warning** that says the VM was deallocated anyway, which stops the incident with it, and so is a guest shutdown that runs out of time, also when `archost01` answers 409 Conflict until the bound.
  - **The messages:** **DONE**, `start-demo.ps1`, and the status page add an `Activity:` line (what runs, where it shows, and how to choose `baseline`), and `status.ps1`'s `STATUS.PROFILE_*` says the target's hourly incidents or how to choose `business-day`; `STATUS.RUNCOMMANDS` counts `DbHubDemo-Profile` as the scripts' own.
  - **The cost:** the estimate counts `business-day`'s T3 incidents at the maximum vCores (`-ActivityProfile`; `T3BusinessDaySession` and `T3BusinessDayMonthly`), priced from the Retail Prices API at run time. With the shipped `t3ActiveShareOfMaxVcores` of 1.0 that adds nothing, and the checks say so: the ceilings and the dated examples are unchanged ([cost management](docs/cost-management.md#activity-profiles-business-day-phase-28-follow-up)). The check for the new default, a dated estimate (2026-11-03, West US 3, list prices from the Azure Retail Prices API; `lowest-cost`, private networking, all five attributed Defender plans): $9.49 a session and $105.82 for the month as the ceiling check counts it with T4 on B1ms, $10.30 and $223.35 on the `Standard_D2ds_v5` fallback, the same for `baseline`, all **Pass** against $15 and $250; what no estimate counts (T3 between its minimum and maximum vCores, and the extra disk operations) is at most $0.51 a session and $1.01 a light-use month.
  - **Validation while incidents run:** **DONE**'s checks read Database Hub, Azure, and Fabric, never a target through its load, and run before the switch. In `validate.ps1`, a runner status read (`WORKLOAD.<target>.COMMAND`) or a `T3.PROPERTY` T-SQL check that fails the way load makes reads fail (a time-out, a 409, a lock, or a resource limit) while one of `business-day`'s incidents is due on that target, or on T1 for the reads `sqlvm01` makes for T3 and T4, and a host lane (`.HOST`) that fails while the target's own status says it runs `business-day` and an incident is due, are a **Warning** that names the incident and when to run the area again, not a **Fail**. The VM's relay reads that profile from the runner's whole status, because the last 1,800 characters it keeps hold the host node but, once the status lists two runs, not the profile. A fault no incident causes (settings.json missing, no runner, a refused login, a host whose process ended), any failure the check doesn't recognize, and a property that's read and wrong stay a **Fail**.
  - **The notebooks run on Run all** (the owner, 2026-11-03: "We should not default to dry runs anywhere"): `CONFIRM = True` by default in `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` and `NB_DatabaseHub_Deployment` ([ADR 0014](docs/adr/0014-notebook-role-and-capacity.md#amendment-2026-11-03-no-dry-run-defaults)). **Run all** of the live-activity notebook sends `business-day` on every target for 240 minutes (`DURATION_MINUTES`, 30 before), through `demo-control.yml` with P9, or prints the link and command without it; `STOP_ACTIVITY` and `PURGE_DEMO_TABLES` stay `False`, so **Run all** never stops activity or drops a table, and `CONFIRM = False` gives a read-only run. The scripts' own notebook runs still pass `CONFIRM` false. The history report's empty-state text and `STOP.FABRIC.SAVEHISTORY`'s manual step say so.
  - **The docs:** the README (the activity profile, the cost note, "Each session", and "See it", with `./scripts/demo-control.ps1 -Target T2 -Scenario incident:cpu-pressure -Duration 25` for an incident right now), the demo walkthrough (the presenter flow, the hourly schedule, and the troubleshooting row for empty problem panels), deployment, cost management, the automation support matrix, `fabric/README.md`, and ADRs 0010, 0011, 0014, and 0016; the notebooks' text, with outputs stripped. `azuredeploy.json` was rebuilt for the parameter's description.
  - **Not proven live:** `business-day` hasn't run end to end in a deployment yet, and which panel lists each incident is expected, not observed; nor have the switch, the interim step on VMs a run starts with its retries and wait, the host lane's **Warning** during an incident, the bounded stop, or the notebooks' new defaults (R603 to R616).
- The showcase (2026-11-03): T4's `max_connections` is 50 on every size (it was the size's default: 50, 429, or 859), so `connection-surge` fills Database Hub's **Failed connections** on every size ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-03-max_connections-at-50-on-every-size-and-connection-surge-past-the-limit-the-showcase)). It's a static parameter: T1's T4 step, and `-Sequential`'s T4 stage, restart T4 when the value waits for a restart (`DEPLOY.T4.RESTART`), and `T4.MAXCONNECTIONS` is a **Warning** until it's in effect. On an existing environment, `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure` applies it ([deployment](docs/deployment.md#update-an-existing-environment)).
- The showcase (2026-11-03): `connection-surge` holds the server's last free slots for 5 seconds at most and makes a few attempts past the limit, which the server refuses: at most 5 refused attempts in any minute and 30 in a run, within the scenario's maximum and as `ContosoCare.Demo/` sessions to `contosocare` only. Only "too many connections" (SQLSTATE 53300) counts; a sign-in failure stops the surge, so nothing looks like a password attack. T4's other lanes retry a 53300 refusal as transient. Not proven live yet.
- **T4 is General Purpose `Standard_D4ds_v5` (4 vCores) by default, with autonomous tuning** (user-directed, 2026-11-04; `Standard_B1ms` before): autonomous tuning needs at least 4 vCores, and Query Store shouldn't run on Burstable. With tuning on, the template sets `pg_qs.query_capture_mode` `TOP`, `pg_qs.parameters_capture_mode` `capture_first_sample`, and `index_tuning.analysis_interval` 60 before `index_tuning.mode` `REPORT` (all dynamic). Without `t4SkuName`, a refused size moves to another zone, then `Standard_D4ads_v5`, then `Standard_D2ds_v5` with tuning off after its cost check, or no T4; **DONE** says which size and whether tuning is on. An existing T4 whose size the configuration doesn't set moves to the default at the next `deploy.ps1`, with a restart (`DEPLOY.T4.SIZE`); `t4SkuName` keeps a size. The record tag is `DbHubT4Size`. Not proven live.
- **`monthlyCeilingUsd` defaults to $375** (user-directed, 2026-11-04; $250 before), for the 4-vCore T4 and its 7-day restart: where the daily T4 safety stop is fully in place (its schedule enabled, the A1 role, the last `DbHub-Stop` job not failed), each restart counts as about a day; otherwise the rest of the month, with a **Warning** naming the missing piece and its fix. The budget follows the ceiling at the next `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure`; a configuration that sets it keeps its value. To stay cheap: `t4SkuName` `Standard_B1ms`, `t4Enabled` false, or a lower `monthlyCeilingUsd` ([ADR 0016](docs/adr/0016-cost-profiles.md#2026-11-04-user-directed-the-4-vcore-t4-default-and-a-375-ceiling), [cost management](docs/cost-management.md#dated-example-the-4-vcore-t4-default-and-the-375-ceiling-2026-11-04)).
- The README's High CPU row: T1 and T2 "may not appear; observed intermittent" (2026-11-04: `arcsql01`'s `AvgCPUPercent` read 81, then 0, while `SQLProcessCPUPercent` stayed at 84-88), until the Hub itself is checked.
- **The Insights app is part of every deployment** (user-directed, 2026-11-05; it was optional): `deploy.ps1` always tries to create `APP_DatabaseHub_Insights`, and a failure never stops the run. The app's step contains everything in it, so an exception, a timeout, a network error, or a sign-in that was skipped, failed, or timed out becomes the app's own record (`DEPLOY.FABRIC.APP`), the Fabric track's outcome still comes only from the notebooks, the dashboard, the model, and the report, and the other tracks go on. Its records are never a **Fail** (**Manual action required** with `reportedStatus` and `nonBlocking` in the evidence), so the run still ends **DONE**, and a network error there never asks for a rerun of the whole deployment. The status page shows the app's step as **Failed**, in red, with the actual error (for example the CLI's exit code and message, or the tenant setting that's off) and the fix, marked "(doesn't block DONE)", and repeats it in a red **Needs attention** box under the banner, also once the run is **Done**; `status.ps1 -Watch` and the **DONE** and **NOT DONE** banners say the same, as `Failed (doesn't block DONE): the Insights app. ...`. It used to show **Skipped**, "Optional". `deploy.ps1 -Sequential` runs the app's step too. The README (with the presentation surfaces and the cost), the app's guide, deployment, prerequisites (P12 to P14 now have their own section), permissions, troubleshooting, presentation, limitations, the security model, cost management, the automation support matrix (rows 173 and 174), and ADRs 0012, 0018, and 0023 say so; the app's capacity units stay outside the cost ceilings and every estimate ([ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md#amendments-2026-11-05-the-insights-app-in-every-deployment)). Not proven live yet.
- **T4's restart worst case counts the nightly safety stop first** (2026-11-05): with its schedule, its role, and a last run that didn't fail, each restart counts as about a day, 96 hours or $34.18 a month at `Standard_D4ds_v5` (list price, West US 3, 2026-11-05), so a deployment with its role in place is no longer counted at the rest of the month (720 hours, $256.32) by default; the control's daily T4 safety stop still counts too. The fix in the **Warning** is now `./scripts/deploy.ps1` or `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1`. `stop-demo.ps1` (`STOP.T4.STOP`) and `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4`) say what stops a restarted T4 again, and the estimate adds the safety stop's job minutes (120 a month, $0 within the 500 included). Dated examples: `lowest-cost` $90.07, `demo-day` $117.55, and `enhanced-performance` $172.39 a month with T4's restart, against $375 ([cost management](docs/cost-management.md#dated-example-the-nightly-safety-stop-2026-11-05)).
- **The three 2026-11-05 changes together** (the Insights app in every deployment, the nightly safety stop, and T2's `JumpstartImage` default): the README and the docs describe one default deployment, whose only steps for a person after `az login` are the Insights app's browser sign-in at the start of a run in a terminal you type in and, when the deployer isn't an Owner, one command for an Owner, `./scripts/grant-safety-stop.ps1`. `init.ps1`'s next steps, the start banner's expectations, and `PREFLIGHT.T2.MODE` say that no T2 step needs a person, rather than no step. One dated cost example for the default deployment, with each profile and T4's restart ([cost management](docs/cost-management.md#dated-example-the-default-deployment-2026-11-05)), replaces the README's 2026-10-21 one, and the FAQ, the presentation guide, and the architecture's decision table say $375 a month. The Insights app's risks are R669 to R674 (R662 to R667 on its branch), and the automation support matrix's rows are 173 and 174 for the Insights app, 175 and 176 for the nightly safety stop, and 177 to 180 for `JumpstartImage`.
- **The nightly safety stop's T3 check stops T4 too** (user-directed, 2026-11-09: "Yes, fix the status check and add the T4 re-stop (Recommended)"): Azure starts a stopped T4 again 7 days after its stop, and the stop's own T4 stop ended a few minutes after the stop's time, so a week later the restart came just after that evening's stop, which found T4 still stopped, and T4 would bill until the next evening, about a day (live: the stop of 2026-11-07 00:01Z ended at 00:03Z, so the restart falls 3 minutes after the stop of 2026-11-13). `DbHub-Stop -Scope T3Check` now stops a running T4 after it reads T3, as `-Scope Nightly` does, waiting up to 10 minutes for one that's starting. It stays stop-only with the same role, which already allows the PostgreSQL stop, and T3 is still only read through Azure Resource Manager. Its result line adds `t4` and `failed`; a T4 it couldn't stop fails its job, and `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP` says "its last T3 check couldn't stop T4". `STOP.T4.STOP` and `STATUS.T4` name the run that stops a restarted T4, with its time (`Get-DemoSafetyStopRunAfter`). A restart just after the T3 check still waits for the next evening's stop, so an idle T4 alternates between about half an hour and about a day, and the cost estimate's worst case stays about a day per restart. To skip one night, turn off both schedules ([ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md#amendment-link-scopes-read-one-by-one-and-t4-at-the-t3-check-2026-11-09)).
- **A copy of this repository spends no GitHub Actions minutes unless its owner opts in** (2026-11-14, the coordinator, under the user's rule that nothing beyond Azure and Fabric is required; [ADR 0022](docs/adr/0022-public-repository-and-github-actions-hardening.md#amendment-2026-11-14-a-copy-spends-no-actions-minutes-unless-its-owner-opts-in)): `validate.yml`'s static checks run on a push or a pull request only in a public repository, where standard GitHub-hosted runners are free, or when the repository variable `STATIC_CHECKS_ENABLED` is `true`, as CodeQL does with `CODE_SCANNING_ENABLED`. A private copy made with **Use this template** used to run them on every push and pull request, Dependabot's included, at about 30 job-minutes each. A run by hand always runs them, and this public repository's CI is unchanged. The README, prerequisites (P7), the FAQ, the deployment guide, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and `SECURITY.md` say so. Tests: `Repo.LifecycleScripts.Tests.ps1` (every job that a push or a pull request starts is by hand only or opt-in in a private copy).
- **The README is short again** (2026-11-17, user-directed: "Optimize the readme"): about 4,100 words instead of 24,800, with **Start here**, what's deployed and by what, what bills, the quick start, day to day, what you control, what stops or pauses on its own, upgrading, deleting everything, and help. The details moved, and none was dropped: every step, option, command, and timing, T2's install modes, GitHub Codespaces, an AI assistant's part, and each upgrade change to [deployment](docs/deployment.md#deploy-it-step-by-step); automated vs manual to the [automation support matrix](docs/automation-support-matrix.md#automated-vs-manual); every prerequisite with how it's checked to [prerequisites](docs/prerequisites.md#each-prerequisite-and-how-its-checked); the parts at a glance, with the diagram, to [architecture](docs/architecture.md#at-a-glance); what fills each Database Hub visual, and the baseline run, to a new page, [Database Hub's visuals](docs/database-hub-visuals.md); the screenshots to [presentation surfaces](docs/presentation.md#screenshots); Run all's internals to [the control notebook's guide](fabric/control/README.md#how-it-works); the cost summary and every schedule that stops the demo to [cost management](docs/cost-management.md#at-a-glance); your sign-in and tokens to the [security model](docs/security-model.md#your-sign-in-and-tokens); and the dated status summary to the [implementation report](docs/implementation-report.md#the-readmes-status-summary-2026-10-27-to-2026-11-09). The links to the moved sections, in 17 files, point at their new places, and a test now checks that every link's fragment, and every `docs/<page>.md#<section>` a script prints, names a heading in its target as GitHub makes them (`Repo.Conventions.Tests.ps1`).

### Removed

- **The live-activity notebook's GitHub path** (2026-11-14, [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md); the user: GitHub Actions aren't needed for demoing). `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` no longer sends activity requests through `demo-control.yml` with the P9 token, with no default and no fallback: gone are section 3's request, the stop-all cell, and their parameters (`CONFIRM`, `STOP_ACTIVITY`, `KEY_VAULT_URL`, `PROFILE`, the `SIM_*` and `GITHUB_*` names). It analyzes and saves only, and records a session's row and beats when the control notebook starts it. P9 is retired; a user can delete its secret and revoke the token. `demo-control.yml` stays for people who run it from GitHub by hand. The scripts that run the notebook still send `CONFIRM` and `STOP_ACTIVITY` false for a copy imported before this change. Tests: `Fabric.Notebooks.Tests.ps1`.
- **The code that started a guided stop** (R725; the cleanup after #41, 2026-11-09): no configuration reaches it since `PaygWizard` runs as `JumpstartImage` (2026-11-09, user-directed: no SQL Server setup wizard). Gone: the guided stop with its prompt, reminders, Bastion wait, and host-password hand-over, the arming of the host's request, the guided stop's steps on the status page, and `New-DemoT4RecordedOffResult`, for a record that left T4 out, which no record does since T4 is part of every deployment. What cleans up an earlier guided stop's leftovers stays (`DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDLEFTOVER`, `STATUS.T2.AUTOSIGNIN`, and the host's cleanup of the guest's automatic sign-in). Help and docs no longer offer T4's opt-out or the `PaygWizard` path as a choice: `deploy.ps1`'s and `status.ps1`'s help, the README, the guides, and the VM folders' READMEs.

### Fixed

- **An update of an existing demo could fail T2 with "The Hyper-V socket target process has ended"** ([#65](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/65); live, 2026-11-18, the user's demo and a test environment; `main` too). An update's new bootstrap bundle makes `archost01` check every step again, and its sign-in cleanup check holds a long PowerShell Direct session in the guest (78 seconds on the user's demo). `deploy.ps1`'s own T2 steps ran meanwhile: Arc onboarding's probe and marker for a guest restored from the golden snapshot, and T2's activity profile. A session started during that check got its PowerShell process in the guest only when the check's session ended, and that process ended 0.15 seconds later, so Arc onboarding failed. On the user's demo it lasted about 2 seconds; on the test environment, more than 20 minutes, until the guest restarted. Now every step that uses PowerShell Direct after an update, Arc onboarding, T2's activity profile, and the session control's T2 commands (Run all), and the host's own bootstrap, clear it by themselves: `archost01` probes the guest three times, 10 seconds apart, and runs the step again once it answers (`DEPLOY.T2.GUESTRETRY`, a **Warning**); when it stays broken while the guest runs with a healthy heartbeat, it shuts the guest down cleanly, starts it, waits until it answers, and runs the step again (`DEPLOY.T2.GUESTRESTART`, a **Warning** that says what broke and that the restart cleared it). A guest that started less than 5 minutes ago is waited for, never restarted, and nothing is restarted during a golden capture on this host; a guest restored from the golden snapshot, whose capture marker names its source, is restarted like any other. At most one restart per `deploy.ps1` run (one an hour for the session control, once per bundle for the bootstrap), recorded in `guest-recovery.json` on the host and in its health; still broken after that is a **Fail** with the fix. A restart whose own process ended before it started the guest again (Ctrl+C, a deleted or timed-out Run Command) is finished by the host's bootstrap within 5 minutes, or by the next recovery or Arc probe, once the guest is off if it was still shutting down, never for a guest that's off for another reason; a host with an older bundle fails as before. The status page has a row for it, and **DONE** a note. Tests in `Vm.ArcOnboard.Tests.ps1`, `Vm.WorkloadSteps.Tests.ps1`, `Vm.GoldenGuest.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.GuestRestart.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Arc.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`. R892.
- **A session said its activity went out when a target's profile set had failed, and an older workload runner's refusal went unseen** ([#28](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/28), [#21](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/21); live on 2026-11-04 and in the review of PR #20; `main` too). Each VM's Run Command sets its targets' profiles on its own, so a `runner profile set` that failed there (the 547 of 2026-11-04 on T1 and T2), or a runner from before the session control that refuses `showcase`, `--tier`, or `--vcores`, showed only in that Run Command's output. The job said Dispatched, and the target kept its earlier profile while the VMs billed. Now that Run Command reads the result line each target's set writes, names the targets whose set failed in a `DBHUB-DISPATCH` line, and ends with exit code 1, which Azure records as Failed. The session's health check reads it at the end of the session's start and at each `DbHub-Monitor` re-check: the control notebook's session status gets a `Host` **Warning** for each such target, with what failed and the command that shows the Run Command's output, or, for an older runner, that `./scripts/deploy.ps1` updates it. The session's other targets go on. The user's decision for #21: catch it at run time, with no setup-time check. It's all in the runbooks, which `./scripts/deploy.ps1` republishes, and it works with the VM-side scripts an older demo has. Tests in `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`, some running the generated script in a child process. R661, R883.
- **A control run that couldn't send any activity stopped the whole demo** ([#27](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/27); the confirmation review of PR #20; `main` too). It deallocated both VMs and stopped T4, a VM that ran before the run and a T4 the request didn't name included, so a presenter's running VM could stop early. Now it stops only what it started itself: the VMs it started, T4 when it started it, and the read replica its session asked for (the user's decision). Its result, on the notebook's session status (a `Stop` **Warning**) and in the job's output, says what it left running, that the nightly safety stop still stops that at its time, and how to stop everything now: `STOP_NOW = True` and **Run all**, or `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1`. Tests in `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1`. R884.
- **T1's monitoring check could restart a healthy SQL IaaS Agent at every run** ([#22](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/22); the review of PR #20; `main` too). Without the agent's `MainServiceLastReportedTime`, a status counted as fresh only 30 minutes after the VM's provisioning time, which the control's own Run Commands move, so a Running plugin could be restarted at each warm run and each `DbHub-Monitor` re-check, each a short gap in T1's telemetry. Now a Running plugin without that time is taken at its word and left alone, in the runbooks, `start-demo.ps1`, and `deploy.ps1`; another state without it keeps the 30-minute rule, and a status with the time is judged by it, as before. Tests in `Automation.Runbooks.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R656, R885.
- **The cost check took the control's T4 safety stop as working when its last run couldn't stop T4** ([#23](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/23); the review of PR #20; `main` too). It read the last `DbHub-Stop` job's status, and a stop whose T4 stop Azure refused still ends Completed, with `Partial` in its result line, so T4's 7-day restart counted as about a day. Now that stop counts only when its `DBHUB-STOP` line says Stopped, Skipped, Deferred, or NoEnvironment. With `Partial`, `Error`, or no readable line it doesn't, and unless the nightly safety stop covers T4, the cost check counts the rest of the month and says why (`PREFLIGHT.COST.T4RESTART`). The nightly safety stop already read its own result line. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T4.Tests.ps1`. R886.
- **T4's size and tuning checks had gaps without preflight, and with an explicit `t4SkuName`** ([#24](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/24); the review of PR #20; `main` too). `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure` skips preflight, so `t4AutonomousTuning` true with a size that can't run it deployed with tuning silently off; `PREFLIGHT.T4.SIZE` didn't say that an explicit `t4SkuName` moves an existing T4; and `preflight.ps1` on its own read the raw configuration, so after a refused scale it announced a scale that won't happen and priced `Standard_D4ds_v5`. Now a run that deploys the template without preflight checks T4's tuning first (`DEPLOY.T4.TUNING`), and its **Fail** stops the run before anything changes, on the console and the status page; `PREFLIGHT.T4.SIZE` says when T4 moves to the size `t4SkuName` sets; and preflight reads the size an earlier run recorded, so it prices the size T4 keeps or gets. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R887.
- **DONE and `status.ps1` called a T4 that kept its size after a refused scale the fallback's creation** ([#25](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/25); the review of PR #20; `main` too). A refused scale's record (`DbHubT4Size`) read like the fallback's, so they said "T4 created as ... because Azure refused the 4-vCore sizes here". Now `DbHubT4Refused` marks the size as kept, and **DONE**, `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4.SIZE`), and T4's rows say "T4 kept its size", with the size Azure refused to scale it to. As before, the record holds only while `t4SkuName` isn't set, so setting it to that size, the fix the notice gives, moves T4 at the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1`. A record from before this change can't be told apart and reads as before. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T4Fallback.Tests.ps1`. R888.
- **`deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure` created T4 without a cost check** ([#26](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/26); the confirmation review of PR #20; `main` too). The template step priced a scale-up of an existing T4 and the fallback's own sizes, but not T4's creation at the default or configured size, which preflight prices, so a run that skipped preflight could create a 4-vCore T4 over `monthlyCeilingUsd`. Now a run without preflight (`-FromStage`, or a resume past it) prices T4's creation as it prices a scale-up: at the size it creates, with the safety stops' restart coverage, against both ceilings. Over either, or without a price, `DEPLOY.T4.SIZE` is a **Warning** that names the ceiling to raise and the next cheaper `t4SkuName`, on the console, on the status page's Infrastructure step, and in **DONE**; T4 is created anyway, because T4 is part of every deployment (the user's decision). When Azure refuses that size, the size the fallback creates instead is priced too, unless the fallback's own check prices it. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T4Fallback.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`. R889.
- **A first run without a configuration file could change an existing environment, or update someone else's** ([#60](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/60); found 2026-11-18 in the pull-safety check of `main`; `main` too, since the first-run automation). With no `config/demo.config.json` (a fresh clone, a common way to get an update; another PC; a lost file), `./scripts/deploy.ps1`'s first run and `./scripts/init.ps1` wrote the defaults for the prefix `dbhub` without checking whether that environment existed, so the next `deploy.ps1` applied them to it: a new administrators' group became T3's and T4's Microsoft Entra administrator, and the network mode, schedule, ceilings, sizes, owner tag, budget address, and Fabric workspaces went back to the defaults; in someone else's environment, your settings and your group. Now the first run reads the subscription first, read-only (`INIT.EXISTING`). An environment that's yours (its owner tag or budget address is your sign-in, or you own the group that administers its T3 and T4) gets its configuration from what's deployed, its deployment record and its resource group's tags, its administrators' group kept and its Fabric workspaces and their capacity found by the IDs it records, so the update changes nothing you didn't ask for; the settings Azure doesn't record keep their defaults, listed. Its session control keeps its own custom roles (`control.roleNameSuffix`, read from the role its identity holds at the demo resource group; with more than one, the one named after its prefix, else the one assigned last, and the message says so), so an environment with roles of its own never gets the shared ones back, which another environment's control holds. The notebooks of its own workspace are republished, as an update does: `fabric.importNotebooks` is true for the workspace it records, for one that holds only what the demo creates (the rule `destroy.ps1` deletes it by), or for one the demo's notebooks are already in, since the demo imported them there before; what else such a workspace holds stays as it is. Any other workspace keeps a first run's false, and the message says why. One that isn't yours, or can't be told apart, is never updated by a first run: `deploy.ps1` deploys a new environment with the next free prefix (`dbhub2`, ...), its own group, and its own workspace, and `init.ps1 -Prefix` stops with **Manual action required** (`-OwnerEmail` with the environment's budget address claims it). A read that fails writes nothing. The console, the start banner, and the status page say which. A configuration file that exists changes nothing. Tests in `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, and `Repo.LifecycleScripts.Tests.ps1`; `destroy.ps1`'s list of the demo's items is shared (`Get-DemoFabricDemoItem`), and `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1` still covers it. R890.
- **`demo-control.ps1` could fail to set T3's or T4's activity with "Access to the path is denied"** (live, 2026-11-17, during T4's business-day connection-surge incident; `main` too). On Windows, replacing the profile file is refused while another handle has it open, and the profile host reads it every 10 seconds. The runner now tries the replace again for about 3 seconds while the file is in use, and the host's heartbeat file does the same. Before, a resend worked (R882).
- **`demo-control.ps1`'s Pass said "(0 check(s) passed)"**: its runner commands write no records to count, so the message now gives a count only when there are some.
- **A T2 host whose VM agent stayed dead ended every `deploy.ps1` NOT DONE** (live, 2026-11-17, mn's test host, whose stuck chunk Run Commands a deallocate didn't clear). The only way out was to rebuild T2 by hand: `./scripts/destroy.ps1`, then `./scripts/deploy.ps1`. Now, when the host's agent stays down and the watchdog's deallocate and start don't bring it back, the T2 track deletes `archost01`, its disks, and its network interface, creates it again from its own template with the values its last deployment recorded, and runs T2's steps again on it, once per run (the user's decision: "Automatically: deploy.ps1 deletes and recreates only the T2 host, once, when its agent stays dead"). T1, T3, T4, and Fabric aren't touched. `DEPLOY.T2.HOSTREBUILD` says what happened, **DONE** has a note, and the status page has a "Rebuild of archost01" row; a rebuild that fails is a **Fail** with its fix. A host the state records but Azure doesn't have is created again at the next run's start (`DEPLOY.T2.HOSTMISSING`). `./scripts/status.ps1` names the host's Run Commands that Azure is still deleting (`STATUS.RUNCOMMANDS`), with `./scripts/deploy.ps1` as the fix. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T2HostRebuild.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Progress.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R872.
- **An existing demo whose T2 host Azure kept in an operation, with no power state and its VM agent Not Ready, ended every `deploy.ps1` NOT DONE** ([#63](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/63); live, 2026-11-18, an existing user's update from 3881ea1, after a tenant job deallocated and started the demo's VMs; `main` too). The run counted such a host as idle and sent it chunk Run Commands that stayed **Pending**, and a bootstrap generation that an earlier run, cut short, had left recorded (`DbHubT2BuildUtc`) kept the watchdog from the host on every later run. Now that host counts as Azure's work in progress, so nothing is sent to it, and once it has stayed down for 20 minutes, read by read and never on one read, the watchdog deallocates and starts it once with no restart, as for a running host whose agent isn't Ready (R871), and rebuilds it in the same run if its agent stays down (R872). A host that comes Ready, or that Azure or someone stops, meanwhile gets nothing. A recorded generation older than T2's longest build (180 minutes, `deploy.ps1 -T2WaitMinutes`) no longer holds the watchdog back. Before, the way out was to deallocate and start the host, then run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again.
- **A `deploy.ps1` run could end NOT DONE with "Not every target finished" after every track had finished** (live, 2026-11-17, mn's upgrade; since the busy marker of 2026-11-14; branch only). The busy marker's refresher writes the resource group's tags every 10 minutes from its own process, outside the lock the parallel tracks share, and two tag merges at the same moment can lose one of them: its refresh landed 0.6 seconds from T2's last track record, so `DbHubTrackT2` stayed one step behind and the ending read T2 as unfinished. A rerun finishes it. Now every write of the state (`Set-DemoDeploymentState`) takes that lock, so the writes of a run's processes never overlap. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R879.
- **A track's own state write that failed still made a finished run NOT DONE, and the next run started over** (2026-11-17, the coordinator's follow-up to R879; `main` too). The ending reads each track's record from the resource group's tags, so a record that a track couldn't write ("its state wasn't recorded") left the run **NOT DONE** for a track whose `DEPLOY.TRACK` result was **Pass**, and the next run, which found the stage recorded as finished, ran every track again. Now, right before it decides DONE, the run's ending records again, in one write under the tracks' lock, every track that finished in this run (its result **Pass**, with its last step) and whose record is behind, and says so in one line. It writes nothing when every record is right, never records a track that didn't finish, and judges by the records as read when the write fails. The rule that a run after a finished one starts fresh is unchanged. Tests in `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`. R880.
- **A VM's stuck setup named the step where the VM belongs** (live, 2026-11-17, mn's rerun; `main` too, since Phase 28). `DEPLOY.EXTENSIONS_*` read "the run can't continue The bootstrap loader's setup until it finishes"; it now names the VM: "the run can't continue sqlvm01's setup until it finishes". A test in `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`. R881.
- **`DEPLOY.VMSTART` told the person to start a VM that Azure was still starting** (2026-11-17, the coordinator's question on a dead start; `main` too). A host whose VM agent never gets ready keeps its start going for up to an hour (R871), so `deploy.ps1`'s 15-minute wait ended **NOT DONE** with "Start archost01 in the Azure portal, or run ./scripts/start-demo.ps1", which doesn't help, and a rerun during that start sent a second start after its wait. Now a start that Azure is still working on gets no second start, and `DEPLOY.VMSTART` says so, with the fix: don't start it yourself, and run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again once Azure has ended the start; that run's T2 track brings the host back, or builds it again once (R871, R872). It doesn't rebuild the host at this step: the agent can't be read before the VM runs, and a slow start can be a healthy one. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R878.
- **An existing demo's `deploy.ps1` ended NOT DONE while T4's read replica was being created** (live, 2026-11-17; branch only). The replica's creation keeps T4 Updating for about 9 minutes, and T1's T4 step, which a rerun reaches early, read that as a stopped server: `WORKLOAD.T4.ENABLE` was **Manual action required** with "Start it with start-demo.ps1". Now T1's T4 step waits while T4 is Updating, Starting, Restarting, or Provisioning, at most 20 minutes; a stopped T4 is reported at once, as before. Tests in `DbHubDemo.T4.Tests.ps1`. R873.
- **`destroy.ps1` kept the session control's empty resource group** (live, 2026-11-17; branch only). Right after the control's Automation account was deleted, Azure still listed its three runbooks for a moment, so the group was kept as "holding 3 other resources". The account's own runbooks no longer count. Tests in `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1`. R875.
- **With `fabric.manageCapacity` true, `destroy.ps1` paused the capacity before deleting the Fabric workspaces** (2026-11-17; branch only), so those deletes met a paused capacity and ended in a **Warning**. It now pauses a capacity that `start-demo.ps1` resumed after the Fabric workspaces, and still before the resource group. The default never calls the capacity. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Destroy.Tests.ps1`. R876.
- **The T2 host stopped starting after a deploy: its VM agent never got its goal state** (live, 2026-11-13 to 17, every test environment's T2 host; `main` too). With 8 chunk Run Commands of up to 711 KB at once, Azure ended their deletes on the T2 host with `InternalExecutionError`. They stayed on the VM, marked for deletion, and Azure refuses a delete while the VM is deallocated. Then the host's next start, after a stop or the nightly auto-shutdown, never got its goal state: Windows ran, the agent stayed Not Ready, and the start ended `VMStartTimedOut` after about an hour, with no T2 activity. Every T2 host start from 2026-10-21 to 2026-11-11 worked with the same transfer, so the change most likely came with Azure. Now `deploy.ps1` sends the T2 host's chunks one at a time, and each chunk's Run Command is deleted, and the delete confirmed, before the next goes; a delete Azure hasn't finished in 5 minutes is sent once more, and after a second one, the watchdog takes over. The watchdog no longer restarts a T2 host whose VM agent isn't Ready or that lists chunk Run Commands: it sends their deletes, then deallocates and starts the host once, which completes them (live: the agent was Ready 1.7 minutes after the start), and the run goes on. Chunks an earlier run left that Azure still hasn't deleted when the host's artifacts have arrived are cleared the same way, right away. `deploy.ps1` sends nothing to a VM whose agent isn't Ready: live, chunks sent to such a host stayed in Creating, and no deallocate removed them. `stop-demo.ps1` sends the deletes of a running VM's chunk Run Commands right before it deallocates the VM. An older demo whose host doesn't start gets it back with its upgrade, `./scripts/deploy.ps1`; [troubleshooting](docs/troubleshooting.md#a-hosts-vm-agent-that-isnt-ready) has the manual steps. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Bundle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R871.
- **`stop-demo.ps1` left a VM running when its last operation had failed** (live, 2026-11-16; `main` too). The T2 host's start had ended `VMStartTimedOut`, so its instance view showed no power state, though Windows ran and the VM billed. The stop waited 900 seconds for a power state that never came, sent no deallocate, ended with exit 0 and "archost01 reported no power state for 900 seconds", and its T2 profile line said the VM "isn't running". Now a VM that shows no power state because its provisioning failed is deallocated at once, which Azure accepts, and the stop says why; one that Azure is still working on is waited for as before. The nightly safety stop already sent its deallocate whatever the power state. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`. R868.
- **A refused Microsoft Graph sign-in told you to create a new T3 admin group** (live, 2026-11-16; `main` too). After the tenant's sign-in policies changed, Graph refused the Azure CLI's token (HTTP 401, Continuous Access Evaluation: "InteractionRequired ... TokenCreatedWithOutdatedPolicies"), and `PREFLIGHT.P6.GROUP` failed with the fix for a group that doesn't exist: "Create an Entra security group you own in the demo tenant and set t3AdminGroupObjectId", which would change a working configuration. Now only a group Graph doesn't find gets that fix; a refused sign-in says so and asks you to run `az login` again, with nothing to change; any other error asks you to check that you can read the group. A test in `DbHubDemo.Preflight.Tests.ps1`. R867.
- **A start already in progress got a second start, and a VM the watchdog left deallocating was called running and billing** (live, 2026-11-16; `main` has the first). After `deploy.ps1` asked Azure to start a stopped VM, the VM read stopped for minutes while Azure worked on it (archost01: 12 minutes), and the wait sent a second start 19 seconds after the first, so every start showed twice in the activity log. Now the start this run asked for is waited for while the VM's provisioning state says Azure is still working on it, and one more start is sent only if that start ends without the VM running. And when the watchdog's recovery left a VM deallocating, a NOT DONE's ending listed it with what runs and bills; now it says that VM is deallocating, and that a deallocated VM doesn't bill for compute. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`. R866.
- **A managed private endpoint that Fabric failed to provision kept Run all from the session's secrets for good** (live, 2026-11-16, a new user's first deploy; `main`'s `setup-activity-control.ps1` too). Fabric's write of the control workspace's endpoint to the vault ended BadRequest ("linkIdentifier cannot be null"), and the endpoint read Failed, so the vault never got a connection: the run waited 10 minutes and ended **DONE** with "isn't approved yet (Fabric creates it in a few minutes)", and neither fix it offered could work, since a rerun found the endpoint by name and waited again, and there was nothing to approve. Now a Failed endpoint, from an earlier run or during this one's wait, is deleted (Managed Private Endpoints - Delete) and created again, once per run, then approved as before; if Fabric fails it again, the step says so, and that the next run replaces it. A creation that Fabric answers with a server error (5xx) is read back and tried again, at most twice, after 60 and 120 seconds (live, 2026-11-17: the replacement's creation got HTTP 500 seconds after the delete, and the same creation succeeded two minutes later); the list is read before each new try, so an endpoint Fabric made anyway isn't created twice. Tests in `DbHubDemo.ActivityControl.Tests.ps1`. R865, R869.
- **A host that stopped or failed during a Run all session went unsaid** (live, 2026-11-16; [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#2026-11-16-amendment-a-host-that-stops-during-a-session)). archost01's start ended `VMStartTimedOut` 53 minutes into a session, and T2's Run Command failed with it, while the session's status still said T2's activity went out. Now the session's start checks the VMs its activity went out to and its dispatch Run Commands, and `DbHub-Monitor`'s 30-minute re-checks look again while it runs, with Azure Resource Manager reads only. A VM that stopped, a provisioning failure since the session's start, or a Run Command that failed is a **Warning** in the session's status, with the fix, and Run all's live view, which reads the status every 5 minutes, prints it. The re-checks read the status back from the control account's new variable `DbHubSessionStatus`, because the vault's can't be read through Azure Resource Manager; they never write over a newer status, and a missing variable is skipped. No new role. Since 2026-11-17, a host that runs with its VM agent not Ready 15 minutes after its activity went out is a **Warning** too, from the same read (live: the T2 host's agent never got its goal state after a session's start, and the status said T2's activity went out for the whole session); a host that drives several targets says "have".
- **Run all's history save lost a session's history on an F4, and held the capacity 30 minutes for nothing** (live, 2026-11-16, a new user's first Run all; [ADR 0026](docs/adr/0026-one-run-all-session.md#2026-11-16-amendment-the-historys-reference-run-and-its-job)). The reference run of `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` stopped at its first chart: Fabric couldn't parse the cell's result after its secret redaction ("Unrecognized token 'REDACTED'"). The fallback job was then queued until Run all's own Spark session ended, and Run all waited 30 minutes for it. Now, in a reference run, the activity notebook prints one line for each table and chart, and `POSTGRES_METRICS` is never the vault secret's own text. Run all looks at the job once and never waits for it: it says the job is queued and where its result shows, and, run from the notebook editor, ends its own Spark session as its last step so Fabric starts the job. A job the capacity refuses (HTTP 430) stays for the next Run all, which saves it first.
- **One call that got no answer held a deploy.ps1 run with no end, and kept the demo awake** (live, 2026-11-16, on a new user's first deploy; `main` too). An `az rest` read of a Run Command's status got no answer for over 2 hours: `az rest` has no time limit of its own, so T1's track waited with no end, the chunk's own 15-minute limit was never checked, and the run's busy marker kept renewing, which moved the VMs' auto-shutdown. Now `az rest` stops after 5 minutes unless its caller sets a limit, and a stopped call is a failed call, so the step's own limits and retries apply. The module's Fabric, Power BI, Resource Manager, Graph, and telemetry requests also bound each read of an answer: PowerShell 7.4's `-TimeoutSec` ends at the answer's headers. The busy marker stops renewing when no step of the run has changed for 60 minutes, or after 6 hours, so a stuck run lets the nightly stop and the auto-shutdown go on; the console and the Nightly stop row say so. Every notebook request already had a time limit, and static tests in `Repo.Conventions.Tests.ps1` keep every web call and notebook request bounded. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Guard.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.SafetyStop.Tests.ps1`. R862.
- **A Fabric or Power BI token that expired during a long step failed a first deployment** (live, 2026-11-16, on a new user's first deploy; `main` too). `configure-powerbi.ps1` got its tokens once; the Direct Lake refresh then retried for 8 minutes, and the report import got HTTP 401 `TokenExpired`, so the history report wasn't created and the run ended **NOT DONE**. The Azure CLI hands back its cached token until about 5 minutes before it expires, so any step that held one across a long wait could send it expired. Now every Fabric and Power BI request renews a token from `Get-DemoFabricToken` or `Get-DemoPowerBIToken` that expires within 4 minutes before sending it, and a refused one once, then sends the request again: HTTP 401, or Power BI's HTTP 403 with `X-PowerBI-Error-Info` `TokenExpired` or `InvalidToken`, which is how Power BI refuses a token. Each renewal is remembered, so callers that still hold the old token don't ask the Azure CLI again; a token the Azure CLI hands back unchanged isn't sent again, and a token a caller brought, or a 403 for a permission, is left as it is. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Fabric.Tests.ps1`; live, an invalid token for each service got one renewal, then HTTP 200. R861.
- **A chunk name Azure kept marked for deletion restarted the T2 host** (live, 2026-11-16, on the upgrade test environment; `main` too). The bootstrap bundle and the workload runner travel in chunk Run Commands with fixed names, `DbHubDemo-Chunk-<sha12>-<nnn>`. Azure had ended the deletes of 17 of them on the T2 host with an internal error and kept their names since that morning, so it refused a rerun's chunk with one of those names ("since it is marked for deletion"); the transfer failed, and the watchdog restarted the VM, which Azure then left unfinished. Now such a chunk runs at once under a fresh name, `<name>-yyyyMMddHHmmss`, the rule named Run Commands follow since 2026-11-13, and `DEPLOY.RUNCOMMAND.STUCK_<VM>`'s **Warning** says so. The VM writes the same chunk file, the cleanups still delete the fresh one, and the other chunks being sent are left alone. When the VM wouldn't have room for the set under Azure's limit of 25 (it listed 23 there: leftover chunks of an earlier send, held ones, and named ones), the send first waits for its leftovers' deletes, 5 minutes at most; with still no room, a chunk that needs a fresh name stops with the limit named, as before. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Bundle.Tests.ps1`. [ADR 0003](docs/adr/0003-t2-host-size-security-type-bootstrap.md#2026-11-16-amendment-chunk-names-azure-keeps); R860.
- **Unit tests reached the real Azure CLI with your sign-in** (found 2026-11-16 in the Azure CLI's own logs; tests only). Five test files let 412 calls through to `az`, for a made-up subscription: the control's setup, the safety stop's and the control's schedules, policy assignments, and a resource group's tags, a write. Each file now answers what nothing else mocks with the real CLI's answer for that subscription, without leaving the process. `tests/Invoke-StaticValidation.ps1` runs Pester with a guard (`tests/TestAzGuard.ps1`): a stand-in `az` stops and lists any call (`STATIC.PESTER.AZURECLI`, a **Fail**), and `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` is an empty folder, so no test uses a sign-in; `Repo.TestAzGuard.Tests.ps1` proves a call fails at once and is listed. The Key Vault opt-in's destroy test also stopped waiting 15 minutes, in real time, on a made-up replica's deletion: that file takes 9 seconds instead of 910.
- **A run that stopped before DONE's checks said they had read T4's replica** (live, 2026-11-16: the upgrade test's run ended **NOT DONE** on T2, and `DEPLOY.T4.REPLICA.END` said the replica was deleted "once DONE's checks and the history snapshot had read it"). The line now says the run stopped before those checks and that the next `./scripts/deploy.ps1` creates the replica again for them; the deletion itself is unchanged. A test in `DbHubDemo.T4Replica.Tests.ps1`.
- **A VM restart that Azure never finished held `deploy.ps1` with no end and no NOT DONE** (live, 2026-11-16, on the upgrade test environment; `main` too). The watchdog's `az vm restart` waited without a limit, and Azure kept the T2 host Updating, with its agent Not Ready, for over 40 minutes. Now the restart goes with `--no-wait` and is waited for 15 minutes at most; one that Azure doesn't finish gets one deallocate and start, automatically and bounded (the T2 guest shut down cleanly first when the host can still run commands), which `DEPLOY.WATCHDOG_<VM>`'s **Warning** names. Azure refusing that, or a VM still not Ready, fails the step at once with "Wait 30 minutes, then run ./scripts/deploy.ps1 again". Every other call that waits on a VM is bounded as well: `az vm extension set` (T1's monitoring flag), T1's Run Command for T3's SQL, and the workload actions; starts, deallocations, managed Run Commands, and their deletes already were. Tests in `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1`. [ADR 0003](docs/adr/0003-t2-host-size-security-type-bootstrap.md#2026-11-16-amendment-a-restart-azure-doesnt-finish); R857.
- **"The Run Commands on the VM could not be listed" in nearly every deploy, while the list had worked** (found 2026-11-16; `main` too). With no chunk Run Command on a VM, the usual case, the list's empty result reached its callers as nothing, which they read as a failed list. The same slip made the cleanup at a send's end report every chunk it had asked Azure to delete as "still deleting" once they were all gone, so `DEPLOY.ARTIFACTS.CLEANUP_<VM>` was a **Warning** even when nothing was left; it now shows only when Azure still lists chunks after its 2-minute wait. A list that really fails is tried again, up to 3 times 5 seconds apart, and its **Warning** says az's error, redacted. Tests in `DbHubDemo.Bundle.Tests.ps1`. The risk is R856.
- **The history's lag finding, and the Insights app, named a restarted replica's catch-up as the session's lag** (found live 2026-11-13). Azure Monitor's Read replica lag is the time since the replica last replayed a transaction, so a replica that was stopped reads at least the time since its stop until T4 writes again: 1,380 seconds at 01:00, before the `replica-lag` beat's 219. Now the notebook's lag finding and the app's insight and marked peak leave that catch-up out and say so: after a gap of 10 minutes or more in the replica's series, or after the window's start, the leading points that reach half the time since the gap began. A capture with only catch-up is a **Pass** that says that, and the rows keep every point. Tests with the Nov 13 data: `Fabric.Notebooks.Tests.ps1` and the app's `insights.test.ts`. The risk is R854.
- **A stopped replica's start-first shows on T4's start record** (found 2026-11-13). `start-demo.ps1` and `deploy.ps1` start a stopped read replica before T4, since Azure starts T4 only after its replicas, and that record was only in `START.T4.START`'s evidence.
