T4's record now carries its message, and its status and fix when that's worse, so a start-first **Warning** never hides. A test in `DbHubDemo.T4Replica.Tests.ps1`. The risk is R855.
- **A resumed `deploy.ps1` kept the older notebooks, history model, and Insights app** (found 2026-11-16 in the merge's upgrade test; `main` has it too). A resume skips a Fabric step that an earlier run recorded as finished, so an environment whose last `main` run ended unfinished after its Fabric step (for example, stopped by `main`'s loop on stuck Run Commands) kept `main`'s `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity`, history model, and Insights app after the upgrade. A session's history then lost T4's metrics: Run all's history job passes them, with the session's window, to a notebook that ignores them, and the app's **PostgreSQL** page stays empty. Now:
  - The Fabric step records what it published, a SHA-256 of the notebooks, the dashboard, the history model and report, and the Insights app's source (`DbHubFabricSha256`), once all of them, the app included, are this version's.
  - A resume runs the Fabric step again when this copy publishes other content, or when nothing is recorded (an environment from before this fix, or a run whose app wasn't deployed). The status page's Fabric rows and the console say why. That needs no Fabric call, so it works on a paused capacity too. `-Sequential -Resume` continues from its Fabric stage the same way.
  - With `fabric.importNotebooks` false, `deploy.ps1` still keeps a workspace's notebooks as they are, by design. `STOP.FABRIC.SAVEHISTORY`'s and `FABRIC.T4.HISTORY`'s fix now names `./scripts/configure-fabric.ps1` then, not `deploy.ps1`.
  - Tests: `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.FabricItem.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`, which fail on the code before it. The risk is R853.
- **A first `deploy.ps1` could end NOT DONE on a Failed SQL IaaS Agent extension, with the portal's Repair as the only fix** (live, 2026-11-16, the new-user test environment; `main` has it too). T1's Bootstraps step merges the monitoring flag right after the loader starts T1's bootstrap (ADR 0018). There, that update met the bootstrap's lockout recovery, which restarts SQL Server in single-user mode, and failed ("Login failed for user 'NT Service\MSSQLSERVER'. Reason: Server is in single user mode"), so T1's Monitoring step was a **Fail** on every later run. Now:
  - The Monitoring step repairs it, once per run and only when it's safe: the extension is Failed, `sqlvm01` is running, and T1's bootstrap is Ready with SQL Server in multi-user mode (a read-only Run Command, `DbHubDemo-SqlModeRead`). `-Sequential`'s Monitoring stage and `start-demo.ps1` after it recreates T3 do the same.
  - The repair is the documented API behind the portal's Repair ([Sql Virtual Machines - Redeploy](https://learn.microsoft.com/rest/api/sqlvm/sql-virtual-machines/redeploy), "Uninstalls and reinstalls the SQL IaaS Extension"; a Contributor may call it), with at most 15 minutes for the extension to report Succeeded.
  - "If you repair or reinstall the SQL IaaS Agent extension, your settings aren't preserved, other than licensing changes" ([Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/virtual-machines/windows/sql-agent-extension-troubleshoot-known-issues#repair-extension)), so the SQL virtual machine's settings are sent again as the template sends them (LOCAL connectivity on port 1433, automatic upgrade; the license type is kept) and read back, and the monitoring flag is merged again.
  - A repair is a **Warning**, `DEPLOY.T1.SQLIAAS.REPAIR`, on the console, the Monitoring step's row, and **DONE**, with what was re-applied. A repair that can't run, or that fails, is a **Fail** with the reason and the portal's Repair.
  - An environment whose extension is already Failed gets it at its next `deploy.ps1`.
- **The race behind it is gone for new deployments** (the user, through the coordinator, 2026-11-16: no known bug ships unfixed). T1's Bootstraps step now merges the monitoring flag before the loader, once `sqlvm01`'s extensions have settled. VM extension operations run one at a time, so the extension's update ends before the bootstrap, and its lockout recovery, starts. It costs about 4 minutes on T1's track, which isn't the critical path. In an environment that's been deployed, the flag is already set, so nothing is written there. A test in `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1` fails on the old order.
  - Tests: `DbHubDemo.Lifecycle.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, which fail on the code before it. The risk is R852.
- **`stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` and `configure-fabric.ps1 -RunLiveActivity` sent T4's metrics to a notebook that ignores them, and said nothing** (found 2026-11-16 in the merge's review of mixed versions): a workspace's `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` from before 2026-11-13 has no `POSTGRES_METRICS`, for example after an upgrade whose Fabric step a paused capacity kept from finishing, so a session's metrics, which go with the deleted replica, were lost while the history read **Pass**, and `-PostgresMetricsPath` moved its file to `saved`. Now both read the notebook's definition first (`Test-DemoFabricNotebookParameter`, Items - Get Item Definition): an older copy isn't sent them. `STOP.FABRIC.SAVEHISTORY` is then a **Warning** that says the SQL targets' history was saved and T4's metrics weren't, with `./scripts/deploy.ps1` as the fix, and `stop-demo.ps1` keeps them in `out/history-pending` (`STOP.T4.HISTORY.PENDING`); `configure-fabric.ps1` says so in `FABRIC.T4.HISTORY` and leaves the file where it is. A definition that can't be read gets them, as before. Tests: `DbHubDemo.FabricItem.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.Fabric.Tests.ps1`.
- **`stop-demo.ps1` confirms T4's stop after it deletes the replica** (2026-11-14, R843; the coordinator: a T4 left running silently breaks the "failures surface" rule): Azure can accept a primary's stop and refuse it a minute later while an operation still runs on its read replica (`SourceReplicasStartStopOrder`), and the replica's read answers 404 about 20 seconds before its delete finishes; live in the control's runbook, twice, that left T4 running. When `stop-demo.ps1` asked for the replica's deletion, it now reads T4 until it's Stopping or Stopped and asks again, at most 3 times in 10 minutes (`Invoke-DemoT4StopConfirmed`), as the runbook does; a T4 still running then is `STOP.T4.STOP` **Fail** with `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` and the nightly safety stop as the fix. A stop that Azure takes at once costs one read. Tests: `DbHubDemo.T4Replica.Tests.ps1` and `DbHubDemo.FirstRun.Tests.ps1`.
- **A Run Command name Azure kept in Deleting held `deploy.ps1` for hours** (live, 2026-11-13, the T2 host on both test environments; `main` has it too). On `archost01`, Azure ended most Run Command deletes with `InternalExecutionError` after 6 to 11 minutes (27 of 29 on the new host) and kept the name marked for deletion until a later delete succeeded, so the next Run Command with that name failed with `OperationNotAllowed`. Each of the host's health reads then waited 8 minutes and failed, and `main`'s own run on the upgrade test environment looped on them for 3 hours. Now:
  - `Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand` runs the script under a fresh name, `<name>-yyyyMMddHHmmss`, when Azure still lists its name after the 5-minute leftover wait; there the fresh one ran within a minute. Later in the same run, that name is waited for 30 seconds at most, and a fresh name's own delete isn't waited for: live, a health read still took 11 minutes before that.
  - It asks earlier fresh copies, and chunk Run Commands left from finished transfers, to go without waiting, and makes no fresh one when the VM has 24 or more Run Commands (Azure allows 25). When the T2 host's health reads find no room for 15 minutes, its waits stop there with that reason (`DEPLOY.T2.WAIT`, `DEPLOY.T2.READY`), instead of looping until their deadline.
  - The console says so at once. On the status page, `DEPLOY.RUNCOMMAND.STUCK_<VM>` is a **Warning** on the step's row (on T2's host-build row while it lasts), and **DONE** or **NOT DONE** says so too. Nothing needs a person.
  - The tests in `DbHubDemo.Arc.Tests.ps1`, `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1`, and `DbHubDemo.OneCommand.Tests.ps1` cover the fresh name, the limit, the row, and the note. The risk is R832.
- **A first `deploy.ps1` that met a paused Fabric capacity never created the workspace** (live, 2026-11-13, the upgrade test environment; `main` has it too). The capacity was paused when the first run reached its Fabric step, so the workspace couldn't be created (**Manual action required**). Only a **Fail** kept the step from being recorded as finished, so every later run skipped it, and DONE's check said to run `deploy.ps1` again each time. Now:
  - A Fabric step with a **Manual action required** record, a paused capacity's included, isn't recorded as finished. `DEPLOY.TRACK.FABRIC` is a **Warning**, its rows say why, and the next `deploy.ps1` runs it again.
  - An environment that an earlier version recorded that way: a resume reads the required Fabric items first. When the workspace, the lakehouse, or an item is missing, the step runs again, and its rows say why. When Fabric can't answer (a paused capacity, for example), the run goes on as recorded, and DONE's check names the capacity.
  - `DbHubDemo.Tracks.Tests.ps1` fails on `main` for both. The risk is R833.
- **`estimate-cost.ps1` never read whether a safety stop covers T4's 7-day restart, and said Defender wasn't read when it was** (found 2026-11-13; since 654145e, 2026-11-04). Signed in, the script called two of the module's internal functions (`Test-DemoT4Enabled`, `Get-DemoT4RestartCoverage`) from its own scope, where they aren't visible. The call threw, so every signed-in estimate counted T4's restart for the rest of the month (720 hours, $256.32 at `Standard_D4ds_v5`, a dated example), even with the nightly safety stop in place (96 hours, $34.18), and warned "The Defender for Cloud plans, the T3 free offer, or the golden snapshots weren't read", though all three had been. Preflight and `start-demo.ps1` weren't affected: they run inside the module. Now the script runs the module's internal functions through the module object it imported (`& $module { ... }`), and each read (the sign-in, the Defender plans, the T3 free offer, the golden snapshots, and the safety stop's coverage) stands on its own: one that fails says what it left out, in one line, and the others are still read. `DbHubDemo.EstimateCost.Tests.ps1` fails on the old script. The risk is R823.
- **`start-demo.ps1` stopped with "The term 'DbHubDemo DbHubDemo DbHubDemo' is not recognized" after it had started the environment** (live, 2026-11-10: `./scripts/start-demo.ps1 -WarmUpMinutes 10` in a PowerShell window that had imported the demo's module from three copies of the repository over time). PowerShell keeps each copy's DbHubDemo loaded, and the script looked the module up by name (`Get-Module -Name 'DbHubDemo'`), which returned all three, so the code it runs in the module's scope (`& $module { ... }`, the pending activity switch) failed, and the script stopped before its report. Running the scripts from a second clone in the same window is enough, for example after cloning again. Now:
  - `start-demo.ps1` and `setup-github-repo.ps1` keep the module object they import (`Import-Module ... -PassThru`) and run module code only through it, so each uses its own copy, whatever else the window loaded. `setup-github-repo.ps1` stopped the same way, before it changed anything, when it read `github.repository` from the configuration.
  - The deployment-stack trial (`tests/teardown/Invoke-DeploymentStackTrial.ps1`) imported its own copy only when no DbHubDemo was loaded, so it could run another copy's code; it always imports its own now.
  - `Repo.Conventions` tests: no entry script or tool looks DbHubDemo up by name; module code runs only through the module object the script imported; the jobs that do look it up (the parallel tracks, the builds, the policy inventory, T1's monitoring, and the status page's writer) run in their own process or runspace and import it there first; and in a process that loaded two copies, each entry script's own selection, top-level and nested, gets its copy. They fail on the old scripts with the live error.
  - The risks are R820 to R822.
- **A paused Fabric capacity made NOT DONE say the lakehouse was missing** (live, 2026-11-10, `rg-dbhubfv-demo`: the capacity's owner paused it between the Insights app's check and the validation). List Lakehouses answered HTTP 404 `CapacityNotActive`, and `FABRIC.ITEMS.REQUIRED` was a **Fail**, "The workspace ... is missing: the lakehouse LH_DatabaseHub", the **NOT DONE** reason, with a rerun as the fix, which fails the same way until the capacity is resumed. Now only a list that answered without an item makes it missing:
  - A list that Fabric refuses because the workspace's capacity is paused or inactive makes the required items **Manual action required**, "couldn't all be checked", with the capacity's name and state. **NOT DONE** names the capacity first (`FABRIC.P5.CAPACITY`, `Get-DemoFabricCapacityBlock`), ahead of the Fabric records it causes. The next step is to resume it, or have its administrator resume it, then run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again. `deploy.ps1` never resumes, pauses, or scales a capacity.
  - Throttling (HTTP 429) and service errors (5xx) are a **Warning** with the HTTP status, whose fix is to run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again in a few minutes.
  - `FABRIC.RESOLVE.LAKEHOUSE` gives the resume, not a workspace role, as the fix for `CapacityNotActive`, and every list that didn't answer is marked `listed` false in its evidence.
  - The history report's snapshot (`DEPLOY.FABRIC.HISTORY`) says the capacity isn't running, not "The Fabric track didn't create the workspace or the lakehouse"; another failed list says the items couldn't be read.
  - `status.ps1`'s `STATUS.FABRIC` passes on a paused capacity again: it looks the lakehouse up among the workspace's items, which Fabric still lists. `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` names the capacity, not a lakehouse to create, and a notebook run whose list failed no longer says to import the notebooks.
  - The risks are R810 to R813.
- **A guided-stop cleanup that failed once before T2's loader kept the run NOT DONE after a later one worked** (review of 27396a4): the cleanup before the loader reports what's still open as a **Warning** now (`DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDLEFTOVER`), and the T2 step's own cleanup at its end, and **DONE**, which reads the deployment state, give the final word, so **NOT DONE** no longer names `stop-demo.ps1 -RemoveBastionOnly` when nothing is open.
- **`status.ps1` passed the guest's automatic sign-in while it was still armed for an earlier guided stop** (`STATUS.T2.AUTOSIGNIN`): it's a **Warning** now, since nothing uses it after the retirement, with how `deploy.ps1` clears it.
- **An older run's `None` or `Refused` T4 record stayed until the next `deploy.ps1`:** `start-demo.ps1` clears it, `DbHubT4Refused` with it (`START.T4.RECORD`).
- **Arc onboarding's `agentKeys` evidence was a list inside a list:** it's a flat list now.
- **Tests:** `Vm.WorkloadSteps`' `EnableT3` and `EnableT4` tests failed from 23:49 to 23:59 UTC (CI, 2026-11-09), since they read the real clock and the activity profile isn't set within 10 minutes of the auto-shutdown; they use a fixed clock now, and a new test covers that boundary. `Vm.GoldenGuest`'s Hyper-V stand-ins no longer leak into later test files (R795).
- **A refused T4 size still ended the run NOT DONE, because Azure keeps the server it refused as a shell for a few minutes** (live, 2026-11-09, the first live run of the fallback below): with `t4SkuName` `Standard_B1ms` in West US 3, Azure refused the size with `SkuNotAvailable` and kept the server it had begun, in `Dropping`, for about 3 minutes. The read after the failed attempt took it for a server the attempt had created, logged "T4's server exists after this failed attempt ... the fallback stops here", and the run ended **NOT DONE** at `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE`, with no retry in another zone and no `Standard_B2s`. The default's `Standard_D4ds_v5` had the same flaw. Now a server counts as T4 only when it's usable: after a failed attempt, the T4 deployment's own operations decide first (a `SkuNotAvailable` for the size sent wins over the server's state), and a server in `Dropping` is a shell. The next attempt, which uses the same name, waits for the shell to go, up to 15 minutes, read every 15 seconds, and says so on the console and the status page's Infrastructure and T4 rows; then the fallback goes on with `DEPLOY.T4.SKU` and `DEPLOY.T4.FALLBACK` as designed. If the shell outlasts the wait, the run records the next size and ends **NOT DONE** with `DEPLOY.T4.REMOVING`, and the plain rerun goes on with that size. A server in `Dropping` is also not deployed for the resume's `DEPLOY.T4.MISSING`, `start-demo.ps1`, `stop-demo.ps1`, `status.ps1`, `validate.ps1`, `reset-demo.ps1`, and **DONE**'s note ([ADR 0019](docs/adr/0019-t4-postgresql-flexible-server.md#2026-11-09-live-finding-a-server-azure-refused-is-a-shell-that-it-removes-not-t4), R790 to R793).
- **`deploy.ps1` didn't update itself where `git` isn't on PATH** (live, 2026-11-09: a clone of `main` in a terminal whose only git was an app's bundled one warned "Git isn't installed" and ran the old code). When `git` isn't on `PATH`, the self-update now uses the first git that answers `git --version` from where Git is usually installed: on Windows Git for Windows (`%ProgramFiles%`, `%ProgramFiles(x86)%`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs`), GitHub Desktop's newest `app-*`, then each Visual Studio that `vswhere` lists; on macOS and Linux `/usr/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, and `/opt/homebrew/bin`. Every git call runs it by its full path, without changing `PATH`, and the update line, the restarted run's note, or the **Pass** names it. When no git runs at all in a checkout (a `.git` is there), the **Warning** now says to install Git and run `git pull --ff-only` in this folder, not to clone again; the advice to clone, kept for a folder that isn't a checkout, names the configuration this run used (its `-ConfigPath`) instead of always `config/demo.config.json` ([R800](docs/implementation-report.md#risk-register) to R802).
- **A configuration that set `t4SkuName` to `Standard_B1ms` left T4 out when Azure refused that size** (a tester, 2026-11-09, West US 3): a size the configuration set had no fallback, so the run ended **NOT DONE**. It falls back now (above). The likely source, `PREFLIGHT.COST.MONTHLY`'s fix "set t4SkuName to Standard_B1ms", is gone: no fix text or schema description asks for a size; the ceiling to raise stays the fix.
- **An older configuration's `monthlyCeilingUsd` of 250 stopped an upgrade at the monthly ceiling** (R727): `init.ps1` wrote 250 from 2026-10-27 to 2026-11-04, when T4's default was `Standard_B1ms`, and T4's 4-vCore default since then puts the month over it. When only a T4 size the person didn't choose puts the month over `monthlyCeilingUsd`, and the month would fit at the reference size, `PREFLIGHT.COST.MONTHLY` is a **Warning** that names the amount, in preflight, `start-demo.ps1`, and `estimate-cost.ps1`: exactly 250 without `t4SkuName`, at `Standard_B1ms`, or a size the fallback chose instead of the one `t4SkuName` sets, at that size. The value stays as it is, the budget keeps alerting at it, and `start-demo.ps1` still stops starting the demo once the month's actual cost reaches it; any other ceiling keeps its **Fail**.
- **A PC whose first `dotnet` had no SDK failed `deploy.ps1` 15 minutes in** (live, 2026-11-09, the fresh-deploy test of #41): T1's and T2's Artifacts steps failed with `DEPLOY.ARTIFACTS.BUILD` "dotnet publish failed with exit code -2147450725 ... A compatible .NET SDK was not found", and the generic "Run New-DemoBootstrapBundle and Publish-DemoWorkloadRunner yourself". The `dotnet` first on `PATH` was a runtime-only `C:\Program Files\dotnet`, which Windows apps and Visual Studio install, and the SDK's folder wasn't on `PATH`; earlier runs on that PC had passed only because the runner's zip was reused, until #40's SDK pin changed its inputs. Now preflight checks the .NET SDK before anything is created (`PREFLIGHT.TOOLS.DOTNETSDK`, unless `-SkipRunner`): it runs the same `dotnet` the build runs, with `--version` in `workload-generator`, where `global.json` applies, and `--list-sdks`. A **Fail** says which `dotnet` ran, which SDKs it has, what `global.json` needs (the pin, `rollForward` `latestFeature`), and the exact fix: `winget install Microsoft.DotNet.SDK.10` or Microsoft's installer, or, when `DOTNET_ROOT`, `%ProgramFiles%\dotnet`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\dotnet`, or `~/.dotnet` has an SDK it accepts, the command that puts that folder first on `PATH`. A **Pass** has the SDK's version in its evidence. `DEPLOY.ARTIFACTS.BUILD` gives the same diagnosis and fix when its build failed on the SDK. `preflight.ps1 -SkipRunner` skips the check, and `validate.ps1` doesn't run it. The prerequisites and troubleshooting say so (R740 to R742).
- **`deploy.ps1` installs the .NET SDK itself when it's missing** (the owner's decision on R742, 2026-11-09: "Install it automatically", for one click after `az login`). Before it creates anything, and only when the run builds the workload runner, `deploy.ps1` looks for a `dotnet` whose SDK `global.json` accepts: first on `PATH`, then the one it installed for you earlier. With neither, on Windows (x64 or Arm64), it installs the pinned SDK with no prompt, no administrator rights, and no change to `PATH`: Microsoft's zip (287 MB for x64) from `builds.dotnet.microsoft.com` into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DbHubDemo\dotnet\<version>`, used only after its SHA-512 matches `config/versions.json` (`dotnetSdkInstall`, from Microsoft's release metadata), `dotnet.exe` has Microsoft's Authenticode signature, and `dotnet --version` in `workload-generator` prints the pin. Every build of the runner runs that `dotnet` by its full path (`Publish-DemoWorkloadRunner`, wherever it's called). The console and the status page's new .NET SDK row show the download's size and progress; `DEPLOY.DOTNETSDK` is a **Pass** with the time and size, or a **Fail** that says nothing was created and why. A failed check deletes what it downloaded, an install that didn't finish (a stopped run) is removed and done again, a later run reuses the install after checking it again, and two runs at once install it once. Elsewhere, `DEPLOY.DOTNETSDK` gives #43's manual fix before anything is created. `PREFLIGHT.TOOLS.DOTNETSDK` passes with the installed SDK, and `preflight.ps1` on its own, which installs nothing, makes a missing SDK a **Warning** on Windows. Locally, with a runtime-only `dotnet` first on `PATH`: installed and checked in 14 to 20 s, the runner built with it, and reused in under a second (R742 closed; R743 to R746).
- **The one-click upgrade's review of 849d4c2** (three medium findings, fixed before release): an interrupted guided stop no longer blocks the rebuild of an unfinished `PaygWizard` guest, because the T2 host runs its guided-stop cleanup in any mode while such records are there, and the T2 track clears a recorded guided stop's request before the loader; the old guest's Arc resources are deleted only when the host and Azure both show they're the old guest's (unknown deletes nothing), and the rebuild's record ends `Done` once the new guest is onboarded, so a later run never deletes the new guest's Arc machine; and no fix names a retired value any more (`PaygWizard`, `t4Enabled`): the monthly ceiling's **Fail** names the `monthlyCeilingUsd` to raise first, and an evaluation-only T2 with an older configuration's `PaygWizard` stops at preflight naming `ProductKey` or `Developer` ([ADR 0006](docs/adr/0006-sql-editions-and-licensing.md#amendment-older-demos-upgrade-in-one-click-2026-11-09)).
- **A fresh deployment's T2 step stopped at Arc onboarding** (live, 2026-11-09, on the one-click branch before release): when `archost01` reached `WaitingForArcOnboarding`, the T2 step stopped with "The property 'checkId' cannot be found on this object", because it read the host build's follow-up results, an empty array, as one item. It reads them as they are now, and its tests use the wait's real return shape.
- **A run after `git pull` in the same terminal ran the old code** (live, 2026-11-06: `deploy.ps1 -Resume`, from a clone that had run `deploy.ps1` at an earlier commit in the same PowerShell 7 session, logged no "Resuming: checking that ..." line, recorded no `DEPLOY.ARTIFACTS.CHANGED_*`, and went straight to the validation, though the bundle had changed): `deploy.ps1` and most other scripts in `scripts/` reused a DbHubDemo module that was loaded already, so the run's own code was the earlier run's; only the track processes, which load the module themselves, ran the new code. Now every script in `scripts/` loads its checkout's module again (`Import-Module -Force`) on every run, unless the repository's own code runs it (a DbHubDemo function, another script, or a test), which keeps the module and its state, such as the subscription guard's validated context and the redaction's registered values. A convention test keeps every script that way.
- **A fix pulled during a deployment didn't reach the VMs** (live, 2026-11-06: a fix to the T2 host's bootstrap was pulled while `archost01` built its guest): the plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1` that **NOT DONE** named resumed each track after its last step, so T2's Artifacts and Bootstraps steps were skipped and the host kept running the old bundle; only `-FromStage Artifacts` sent the new one. Now a resumed run (a plain run after a deployment that didn't finish, or `-Resume`) builds the bootstrap bundle and the workload runner as the Artifacts step does and compares them with the state tags; a VM whose bundle or runner differs, or isn't recorded, gets its track run again from the Artifacts step, with the steps after it, and the console, `DEPLOY.ARTIFACTS.CHANGED_<VM>`, and the status page's Artifacts row say why. With nothing changed, a resume is exactly as before ([ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md#amendments-2026-11-06-a-fix-pulled-during-a-deployment-reaches-the-vms)).
- **A changed bundle waited for the old bundle's bootstrap run** (live, 2026-11-06): the loader registered the task again and started it, but the task ignores a start while a run goes on (`IgnoreNew`) and ends one only after 6 hours, and the old run was in the Jumpstart image's 34 GiB download; the owner had to end its `powershell.exe` and disable the task by Run Command. Now the loader stops a run of an earlier bundle, and the processes it started, before it starts the task, says so in `loader.log` and `INFRA.BOOTSTRAP.LOADER_<VM>`, and leaves a run of the same bundle alone, on `sqlvm01` and `archost01` in Windows PowerShell 5.1. The state machine resumes: the lock goes with the process, its records are replaced whole, and downloads resume from their `.partial` files ([ADR 0003](docs/adr/0003-t2-host-size-security-type-bootstrap.md#amendment-a-changed-bundle-takes-over-from-a-run-thats-still-going-2026-11-06)).
- **A guest VHDX build that was stopped left its media attached** (found while checking that a run can be stopped safely, 2026-11-06): in the build modes, the ISO and the half-built VHDX stayed attached until the host restarted, and the next build couldn't delete the disk. The build now detaches both first.
- **A loader that found its chunks damaged failed the same way on every rerun** (found on the way, 2026-11-06): it deletes them, fail closed, but the state still listed the VM as having the bundle, so neither a plain run nor `-FromStage Artifacts` sent it again. Now the run takes the VM out of that record, and the next run sends the bundle before the loader.
- **NOT DONE's command and its next action disagreed, and both dropped `-ConfigPath`** (live, 2026-11-06): the next action said `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts` while the **Run** line gave the plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -ConfigPath "./config/livetest.local.json"`, so a copied command either didn't start a new bootstrap generation or deployed `config/demo.config.json`. Now each `deploy.ps1` that a remediation tells you to run is the run's own command with the remediation's parameters, the **Run** line is that same command, and other `./scripts/` commands it names get the run's `-ConfigPath`, in **NOT DONE**, the console's **Fail** and **Warning** lines, `DEPLOY.SUMMARY`, and the GitHub job summary. A `-Resume` that an older remediation names is left out, because the plain command resumes by itself and, after a run whose stage reached the validation, starts over with the template, which `-Resume` would skip. A host step's next action now says that a plain rerun also starts a new generation when your fix changed the bootstrap bundle, and a run stopped with Ctrl+C says that the rerun sends what a pulled fix changed.
- **The Insights app's sign-in said the app isn't created while the app's step deployed it** (live, 2026-11-06): nobody answered the sign-in's window, and at its 15-minute deadline the read of the CLI's sign-in said "not signed in", so `DEPLOY.FABRIC.APPSIGNIN` was a **Warning** ("... without the sign-in the Insights app isn't created ..."); six minutes later the app's step, released by the same deadline, had found the CLI signed in and deployed the app. The two reads ran at the same moment, and the Rayfin CLI's token cache is a file rewritten in place when a token is renewed (`@azure/msal-node-extensions` 1.5.32 in the pinned CLI 1.36.2), so one read most likely found it empty; stopping `rayfin login` changes no token cache. Now, past the deadline, the app's step waits while the run reads the sign-in, at most 4 minutes more (`Wait-DemoFabricAppSignIn`), so the reads don't overlap. That read has 90 seconds, as the start's does, instead of a minute (a cold read took 64.8 seconds live), and when it finds no sign-in, or can't be read, it reads again 10 seconds later (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInState -Attempts 2`), with an Info line each time. And the app step's own records settle the sign-in: `FABRIC.APP.SIGNIN` or `FABRIC.APP.DEPLOY` **Pass** turns its **Warning** into **Pass**, in the results and on the status page (`Update-DemoFabricAppSignInFromApp`), in `deploy.ps1` and `deploy.ps1 -Sequential`.
- **A browser opened for the Insights app's sign-in though the Rayfin CLI was signed in** (live, 2026-11-06, a rerun with `-FromStage Artifacts`): `deploy.ps1`'s start read the CLI's sign-in (`rayfin login status --json`) for at most 30 seconds, from the app's stage only. A cold read took 44 seconds, so a sign-in nobody needed started, and only a debug line said why. Now the read starts with the run, before the subscription guard, and runs alongside it and the stopped VMs' start (`Start-DemoFabricAppSignInCheck`). The run takes its answer before preflight, at most 90 seconds after the read started, and while it waits the console and the status page say "Checking whether the Rayfin CLI is already signed in". Without a stage (a first run, a fresh clone), the pinned CLI answers through `npx --prefer-offline`. The log says why a browser opens: Info when the CLI isn't signed in to the demo's tenant, and a Warning with the reason (for example "no answer within 90 seconds") when the read failed or ran out of time. The process layer can start a process without waiting (`Start-`, `Receive-`, and `Stop-DemoFabricAppProcess`; `Invoke-DemoFabricAppProcess` is the start and the wait).
- **`configure-fabric.ps1 -RunLiveActivity` analyzed the wrong resource group outside the default environment** (live, 2026-11-06): its step 5, which also runs once after the live-activity notebook is first imported, sent no `RESOURCE_GROUP`, so the notebook's queries kept its default, `rg-dbhub-demo`. A clean deploy in `rg-dbhublt-demo` then saved no telemetry snapshots (`telemetry_snapshots` 0), so the history report had no telemetry. Step 5 now sends `rg-<prefix>-<environment>`, as `stop-demo.ps1 -SaveHistory` and `deploy.ps1`'s new snapshot do, and the help example of `Invoke-DemoFabricNotebookRun` is analyze-only and scoped too. Tested in `tests/unit/DbHubDemo.Fabric.Tests.ps1`.
- **The Insights app's sign-in opened no browser** (live, 2026-11-06): the terminal's `BROWSER` held a command line set for `az login`, `"C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft\Edge\Application\msedge.exe" --inprivate %s` (Python's [`webbrowser`](https://docs.python.org/3/library/webbrowser.html) module, which the Azure CLI uses, takes that form), and the Rayfin CLI 1.36.2 opens its sign-in page with `"<BROWSER>" "<url>"` without checking the result, so nothing opened and `deploy.ps1`'s sign-in waited until its 15-minute deadline. Now both sign-ins, `deploy.ps1`'s and `configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn`'s, remove a `BROWSER` that's neither the path of an existing file nor a program on `PATH` from the CLI's process only (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInEnvironment`) and say so in one line; the CLI then opens the default browser, where `rayfin login` still asks which account to use. The terminal's own `BROWSER` doesn't change, so `az login` keeps it, and a usable one, such as a dev container's helper or a Linux desktop's browser, still reaches the CLI.
- **A false Warning for the Insights app's sign-in** (live, 2026-11-06): after that window was closed (exit code -1), `DEPLOY.FABRIC.APPSIGNIN` and the status page said "without the sign-in the Insights app isn't created", though a separate `rayfin login` had already signed the CLI in to the demo's tenant, so the app's step would create the app. Now a window that's closed, fails, or is stopped at its deadline or the tracks' end settles from the CLI's own `rayfin login status --json`, read once, at most a minute (`Get-DemoRayfinSignInState`: the stage's pinned CLI when it's installed, otherwise the same version through `npx --prefer-offline`, since a first run has no stage yet). Signed in to the demo's tenant is **Pass** ("... its sign-in window was closed, with exit code -1, but a sign-in already exists; the Insights app's step uses it"); the **Warning** says the app isn't created only when the CLI really isn't signed in, and says so when the status couldn't be read. It isn't read after Ctrl+C, so a stop stays quick.
- **T2's Jumpstart image downloaded at 0.2 MB/s** (live, 2026-11-06): the T2 host's download loop gave its 4 MiB buffer to a PowerShell function for every network read, and Windows PowerShell 5.1 checks a mandatory array argument by walking all of its elements, so `GuestImage` kept a core at 99% and would have needed about two days for the 34 GiB. The loops now call the hash algorithms' .NET methods directly, and the partial file is written through a 4 MiB buffer: over reads of 16 KiB in Windows PowerShell 5.1, 201 MiB/s instead of 0.07 MiB/s (an offline benchmark), so the download is bound by the network and the host's disk. Its checks are unchanged: the ETag, the length and Content-MD5 (fail closed), and the SHA-256. A convention test keeps mandatory `[byte[]]` parameters out of the VM-side code.
- **Resuming T2's Jumpstart image download always started over** (live, 2026-11-06): the host asked for the rest of the image with an open-ended range (`bytes=N-`), and Blob storage serves an anonymous request without `x-ms-version` at its earliest service version, which answers that with the whole image, so every resume silently started over from the first byte. The host now asks for a closed range (`bytes=N-M`), which every service version honors (206, checked against the image from Windows PowerShell 5.1), and logs a **Warning** when a server still sends the whole image and the download starts over. It sends no `x-ms-version`, which would change the format of the ETag that the resume and the pin compare.
- **T2's first boot never deleted `C:\Jumpstart`** (live, 2026-11-06): `JumpstartImageCheck` failed 3 times with "The Jumpstart guest isn't ready yet: C:\Jumpstart is still there." The first boot passed its cleanup `[string]$x` arguments without parentheses, which in argument mode are strings, not casts, so the guest got "[string]System.Collections.Hashtable[jumpstartFolder]" as the folder (and similar text as the feature and the instance name for setup's Integration Services uninstall), while the log said "extras removed" regardless. The arguments are now in parentheses; the host logs what the cleanup did (the services, the uninstall's result, and the folder removed or absent); and a convention test fails any `[type]$value` command argument in the repository. An environment that hit it: `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts`, whose image check runs the cleanup again.
- **The showcase didn't start on T1 or T2** (live, 2026-11-04): `ContosoCare`'s `demo.activity_profile` kept the profile-name check it has had since the first release (`off`, `baseline`, `business-day`, and `incident:<scenario>`), so `profile set` refused `showcase` there with error 547. T3 and T4 took it, because their profiles are files on T1, and the control's job still said Dispatched ([#28](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/28)). `010-schema.sql` now creates the check with `showcase`, and `110-demo-control.sql` widens it, once and in one transaction, in a database whose check lacks any allowed name, such as one created before the showcase ([#31](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/31): its guard looks for every name, not only `showcase`). So `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` (or Stop now, once the VMs are deallocated), then `./scripts/deploy.ps1 -FromStage Artifacts` or the fuller update, then Run all, fixes an existing environment ([#30](https://github.com/MarcBushong/fabric-database-hub-demo/issues/30): the update comes after the stop, because `deploy.ps1` doesn't cancel a run's pending auto-stop). A test keeps the check's names, the migration guard's, and the procedure's the same.
- **An `off` started the VM or T4 it was meant to turn off** (the review of PR 20, 2026-11-04): a request such as T1 and T3 `showcase` with T2 and T4 `off` booted `archost01` and started the 4-vCore T4, waited up to 15 and 20 minutes, then sent `off`, and both billed until the auto-stop. Now an `off` never starts anything: a VM whose only activities are `off` gets them only when it already runs (otherwise they're skipped), T4's `off` goes with `sqlvm01`'s activities without reading, starting, or waiting for T4 (its profile is a file on T1), and a run whose only dispatched activities are `off` stops what it started.
- **The update command moved T4 without a cost check** (the review of PR 20, 2026-11-04): `deploy.ps1 -FromStage Infrastructure` skips preflight, so it scaled an existing T4 to `Standard_D4ds_v5` unpriced (with `monthlyCeilingUsd` at 250 and no safety stop, the month came to 312.21 USD). Now the template step prices a move to a larger size, the default or a `t4SkuName` changed on an existing server, with the 2-vCore fallback's estimate and the safety stop's restart rule; over either ceiling, or without a price, T4 keeps its size, and `DEPLOY.T4.SIZE` names the ceiling to raise.
- **T1 missing right after a cold start from Fabric** (live, 2026-11-04): a minute after the run started `sqlvm01`, the SQL IaaS Agent extension's instance view still held the plugin status from before the stop, Running and OK, so no restart went out, and T1 sent no telemetry for 12 minutes. The control's runbooks (`DbHub-Control`, `DbHub-Monitor`), `start-demo.ps1`, and `deploy.ps1` now count a status only when its `MainServiceLastReportedTime` is later than the VM's start (the run's accepted start, or the VM's latest start in its instance view), wait up to 8 minutes for one (5 before), and restart the agent once when none comes or it isn't Running; after the restart, only a status reported since then counts. The re-checks run only while at least 5 minutes of the run remain: none in a run under about 35 minutes, two in the 90-minute showcase. Not proven live.
- Phase 28, from an independent review of pull request 9 (each with a Pester test that reproduced it first):
  - A golden redeploy in `PaygWizard` mode configures SQL Server enabled by Azure Arc again: the guard that waits for the host's setup check honors `-Force` and lets a guest that already runs Standard through, where it had stopped a golden copy settled at `ArcOnboardingBeforeWizard` after its Release (`T2.ARC.CONFIGURATION`).
  - With `-NoStatusPage`, or a status page that couldn't be set up, the guided stop still prompts: without a progress model the T2 track's process prompts itself, and the `HUMAN ACTION REQUIRED` line points to the console instead of a page that doesn't exist.
  - A clipboard counts only where the person can paste from it: on Windows when `Set-Clipboard` takes text, on macOS with `pbcopy`, and on Linux with an X display and `xclip`; never in GitHub Codespaces, an SSH session, or Azure Cloud Shell, where PowerShell's `Set-Clipboard` doesn't fail but keeps the text in its own process. Those sessions get the portal's **Reset password** path, in `deploy.ps1` and `start-demo.ps1 -Interactive -ResetAdminPassword`.
  - `deploy.ps1 -Sequential` can end **DONE**: its Fabric stage imports the Real-Time Dashboard and the history model and report, as the parallel Fabric track does, so a new workspace has what DONE looks for; after its validation it checks what DONE needs, read-only; and it's judged by its recorded stage, since it records no tracks.
  - A `t4SkuName` the configuration sets wins over a recorded "no T4" from the Burstable fallback, so DONE needs T4's estate and metrics again, and a deployment with T4 in it clears that record.
  - The watchdog never restarts `archost01` while the golden Release runs on it or while its guest build may be running, and the artifacts sent during the Release go without the extension wait or the watchdog. It judges the build from the deployment state alone (`DbHubT2BuildUtc`, set when the host's loader starts a bootstrap generation and cleared when a new golden copy is released and after the T2 step), never through the host's agent, which is what's stuck when it asks; so a golden redeploy's stuck copy is restarted instead of failing after a health read that timed out.
  - With `networkMode` `public`, the policy inventory stays in preflight, so a Modify policy that turns SQL public access off still fails `PREFLIGHT.POLICY.SQLPUBLIC` before anything is created; only `private` defers it.
  - Relative paths given to the scripts (`-ConfigPath`, `-OutputPath`, and the rest) resolve against PowerShell's current location (`Resolve-DemoUserPath`), not the process's working directory, which `Set-Location` and `Push-Location` don't change: `init.ps1 -ConfigPath ./config/proof.local.json` after a `cd` had written the file elsewhere. The module does the same wherever it makes such a path absolute.
- Phase 28, from the live proof from scratch (each with a Pester test that reproduced it first):
  - Ctrl+C stops `deploy.ps1` within seconds, its track processes too. At the guided stop it had seemed to do nothing: the stop's `finally` block called `Stop-Job`, which waited for the T2 track's own cleanup (minutes of Azure calls), and a second Ctrl+C can't interrupt a `finally` block. Now the run records the Ctrl+C (`Console.CancelKeyPress`) and leaves a cancel marker for the track processes, so the slow Azure cleanups (a Run Command's delete, the chunks' cleanup, the guided stop's Bastion removal and password rotation) are left to the next run or `stop-demo.ps1`; each job reports its process ID first, and one that doesn't end within about 3 seconds is ended with everything it started. The tracks' private files live under the run's handoff directory, which goes. A time-limited Azure CLI call waits in half-second steps, so a stop gets through, and ends the CLI with it. The PC may sleep again, and the console and the status page end **Stopped, not done** with the command that continues; the steps that were running go back to **Pending**.
  - The optional Insights app's step gets the workspace that the Fabric step has just resolved or created (`configure-fabric-app.ps1 -WorkspaceId`, now an override), instead of looking it up by name again, which failed right after the creation (`FABRIC.RESOLVE.WORKSPACE`). In `deploy.ps1` the app's records are never a **Fail**: a failure there is **Manual action required**, marked optional, so it never blocks **DONE** (its `FABRIC.RESOLVE.WORKSPACE` would have); the status page shows it **Skipped**, and **DONE** lists it, with the actual reason and that record's own fix, instead of a generic word about tenant settings.
  - `stop-demo.ps1` before T2's setup reports `STOP.PROFILE_T2` **Not selected**, "T2 isn't set up yet", not a **Warning** with the guest's `Import-Module` error: the guest gets its demo module after SQL Server setup. `status.ps1 -Detail` and `start-demo.ps1` say the same, and the guest now says so itself before it imports anything.
- Phase 28, from the review of the live-proof fixes (each code fix with a Pester test that reproduced it first):
  - A run after a Ctrl+C at the guided stop could end **DONE** with on-demand Bastion still up and the handed-over host password still valid (with the Key Vault opt-in, its secret still enabled): the stopped guided stop had skipped its cleanup, setup finished in the guest anyway, and the next run's guided stop, no longer needed, returned before any cleanup. Now the guided stop that isn't needed, the T2 step at its end (also with `-NoWaitForT2` and on a golden redeploy), and `-Sequential`'s T2 stage clean up whatever the deployment state records (`DEPLOY.T2.GUIDEDLEFTOVER`): the request, Bastion, the password's rotation, and the Key Vault copy. **DONE** never comes while a handed-over password is unrotated or the guided stop's Bastion may be up: that's **NOT DONE**, naming `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1 -RemoveBastionOnly`. The guided stop's record (`DbHubGuidedStopUtc`) now stays while its Bastion couldn't be removed, and `stop-demo.ps1` clears it once Bastion is gone.
  - The docs no longer promise exit code 2 after Ctrl+C: PowerShell usually ends the script itself, with its own exit code, while the console and the status page still say **NOT DONE** with the command that continues.
  - A stop ends a track's process only if its start time is the one the track reported with its process ID, so a process that later gets the same ID is never ended.
- Phase 28, from the live proof's second day (2026-10-30; each with Pester tests that reproduced it first): running `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again didn't start VMs that were stopped, so "it continues where it stopped" failed whenever the nightly auto-shutdown, `stop-demo.ps1`, or a stop had deallocated them: its first Run Command failed with "the VM isn't running". Now, on an existing environment, `deploy.ps1` asks Azure to start a deallocated or stopped `sqlvm01` or `archost01` (and a deployed T4 that's stopped) before preflight, so they boot while it runs, and has them running before the template or any track needs them (`DEPLOY.VMSTART`; one that doesn't start ends **NOT DONE** with the next action). The T2 guest starts with its host, and a golden snapshot's source host gets its guest back, as `start-demo.ps1` does. Running VMs are only read; the status page shows **Starting sqlvm01 and archost01, which were stopped (deallocated); no action needed from you**. Also in `-Sequential`.
- Phase 28, from the live proof's guided stop (2026-10-30; each with Pester tests that reproduced it first): setup's **Use Azure Login** opened a white window, then Internet Explorer's "Content within this application coming from the website listed below is being blocked by Internet Explorer Enhanced Security Configuration" for `https://login.microsoftonline.com`, though the `GuestWebView2` step had succeeded; turning IE ESC off while setup was open didn't help. Setup's sign-in is an IE-based control (MSAL.NET documents its legacy web view for Microsoft Entra ID authorities, whatever WebView2 runtime is installed). Now, before anyone is asked, before the guest's automatic sign-in, and before setup opens, the host checks the WebView2 runtime (installs it when it's missing), turns IE Enhanced Security Configuration off for administrators in the guest, and adds the Azure portal's authentication hosts to Trusted sites for the guest's Administrator; `deploy.ps1` asks only once the host's health says `guidedSignIn` ready, and the status page shows the preparation as Azure-side, no action needed. The guided stop's cleanup, after success, failure, or a time-out, turns IE ESC back on and puts the Trusted sites entries back from a snapshot, the dialog's **Add...** entries included (fail closed). The host's instructions no longer blame a missing WebView2 for a blank sign-in window. Everywhere the person sees the guided stop's steps (the README, its AI assistant section, the console's **WAITING FOR YOU** banner, the status page's **Waiting for you**, the host's own instructions in `health.json`, and troubleshooting), the owner's instruction is the fallback beside the automatic fix: if the Azure Login window stays blank, or Internet Explorer says content from `https://login.microsoftonline.com` is blocked, follow the dialog's directions, **Add...**, then **Add**, to put the Microsoft sign-in page in Trusted sites, and **Close**; then **Use Azure Login** again, and the same for any other sign-in site it names (confirmed live on 2026-10-30; turning the setting off while setup is open doesn't help).
- Phase 28, from the live proof's resumed run (2026-10-30; each with Pester tests that reproduced it first): T1's monitoring plugin, running when `start-demo.ps1` checked it at 07:47, was `{"State":"NotRunning"}` by 09:12, and the resumed run, whose T1 track had finished earlier, ended **NOT DONE** on `TELEMETRY.T1`. Now every run that ends with the validation and has no T1 Monitoring step checks the plugin once the VMs run, whether or not it started `sqlvm01` (`DEPLOY.T1.MONITORING`, in its own process beside the tracks, so the guided stop's prompt never waits; before the first stage that needs the VMs in `-Sequential`), and restarts the SQL IaaS Agent service when it isn't running. Before a run is judged on missing telemetry, a target whose data didn't come gets its monitoring checked once (T1's plugin, restarted if it stopped; T2's Arc connection and monitoring; T3 woken, with its extended property) and its telemetry once more, bounded by `-TelemetryWaitMinutes`, in the tracks' telemetry steps and last in DONE's checks; the status page says "Restarting T1's monitoring plugin, which stopped after a restart; no action needed." The NOT DONE reason no longer ends in `System.String[]` (the outcome's notes, an array, were joined as one object), and the banner prints each note on its own line.
- Phase 28, from the live proof's final run (2026-10-30; each with Pester tests that reproduced it first): every DONE requirement passed, and the run ended **NOT DONE**, exit 2, telling the person to run `deploy.ps1` again, which couldn't change it: `DEPLOY.SUMMARY` held `PREFLIGHT.P4.HUBSETTING` and `FABRIC.P4.HUBSETTING`, the same tenant setting twice, and `FABRIC.APP.BROWSER`. The status page read as if the setting "Users can access the Database hub (preview)" were off; it was on. The check never read it: `deploy.ps1` never passed `-FabricAdmin`, so it always said "Confirm that ... is enabled", and only the preflight copy was exempt from DONE. Now preflight and the Database Hub checklist read the setting through the Fabric admin API (Tenants - List Tenant Settings) on every run, and log the call and its outcome, never a token: **Pass** when on; a **Warning** when on only for security groups; "is disabled (read through the Fabric admin API)" when off, the one P4 result that keeps the run from **DONE**; and otherwise "Couldn't check the Fabric tenant setting ... (needs a Fabric administrator; HTTP 403): please confirm it's enabled". `-FabricAdmin` is accepted and changes nothing. What only a person can confirm, which a rerun can't resolve (that setting when it wasn't read, decided from its evidence, an unrecognized capacity SKU, presenters' roles, the app's browser check), never makes a run **NOT DONE**: **DONE** lists each once under **Please confirm**, on the console and the status page, and `DEPLOY.SUMMARY` keeps them apart from what needs you, without `System.String[]`. **NOT DONE** names only what blocks it, with that item's own fix. On a new workspace, the history model's blank measures (about 40 `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURE.*` **Warnings**) are one **Warning** in `deploy.ps1`, `FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.MEASURES`: the history report is empty until `NB_DatabaseHub_LiveActivity` saves its first snapshots.
- Phase 28, from the run on the fixes (2026-10-30, **DONE** in 20 minutes with no person needed) and the reviewer's delta review: the console's results no longer repeat one remediation for each of many **Warnings**: 3 or more that share it are one line with their count and two examples, every **Fail** keeps its own line, and the table, `out/deploy.jsonl`, and the job summary keep every result; after a **DONE** run, the heading of the **Manual action required** items says that none of them keeps it from DONE. The status page says "Starting sqlvm01 and archost01, which were stopped (deallocated); no action needed from you", and Preflight's step says "T3 free offer: not used" and where T2 comes from in words. The Hub tenant setting is judged by the run's latest read, so the Fabric track's read supersedes preflight's when an administrator turns it on during the run, and a latest read that says off is read once more before the run is judged. The guided stop's snapshot of the guest's Trusted sites never holds the sign-in hosts' own entries, so the cleanup removes them even after a pass whose PowerShell Direct call failed. The host's preparation of setup's sign-in fails open: not ready about 15 minutes or 3 passes after it began, the host logs a **Warning** and opens setup anyway, `deploy.ps1` asks for the person with **Add...** first and says the automatic preparation didn't finish, and the cleanup still restores IE ESC and the Trusted sites. A **NOT DONE** or stopped run that started or created VMs names, in one line, what now runs and bills, and how it stops: `./scripts/stop-demo.ps1` now, or the nightly auto-shutdown at its configured time.
- Phase 28: a test of Ctrl+C at the guided stop could fail on the Linux CI runner: its run takes about 250 ms, so the mocked T2 thread job could be stopped before its script started, which ends a thread job **Failed** rather than **Stopped**. The mock now waits until the job runs. Test-only.
- Phase 28, from the rerun on b18a028 from deallocated VMs (2026-10-30, **DONE**, exit 0, in 45 minutes instead of 20) and the reviewer's delta review (each code fix with Pester tests that reproduced it first): T1's Monitoring step waited 25 minutes on a plugin that reported `{"State":"NotRunning"}`, silently, before the SQL IaaS Agent's restart brought it back in 17 seconds; the console then still printed "T1.SQLVM.MONITORING: Run configure-monitoring.ps1 -WaitMinutes 15". Now T1's Monitoring step (and `-Sequential`'s) gives a `NotRunning` plugin a short grace before the restart: 3 minutes when the monitoring flag was set before the run, 5 when the run merged it. `start-demo.ps1`, the check beside the tracks, and the second look before a **NOT DONE** for T1's telemetry still restart it at once, as before; `start-demo.ps1`'s wait after it recreates T3, which waited all of `-MonitoringWaitMinutes` and never restarted the agent, now restarts it at once too. The step's wait counts elapsed time, its reads included, never sleeps past its end, and says every few minutes, on the console and the status page, what it waits for ("T1's monitoring plugin reports NotRunning (3 min so far); the deployment restarts it if it doesn't start by itself within 1 minute; no action needed"), then "Restarting T1's monitoring plugin, which stopped after a restart; no action needed." After a restart that worked, `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` is read again and reported as it is then, with the restart in its evidence; a read that's still a wait leaves its **Warning**, marked superseded, without a remediation, on every run whose T1 track runs that step. The console logs a check's remediation once when two sources report the same check and remediation (`PREFLIGHT.FABRIC.VIEWERLICENSE` came twice); the table, `out/deploy.jsonl`, and the job summary keep every result. The billing line names only what exists: a first deployment records its VMs once the template succeeded, and T4 only when it was deployed (the Burstable fallback can leave it out), nothing after a failed template; and a rerun records only the VMs it issued a start for, not one that was already starting.
- Phase 28, from the rerun on 3a26bdb (2026-10-30: **NOT DONE**, exit 1, for one transient TLS error), the run after it, and the reviewer's delta review of 3a26bdb (each code fix with Pester tests that reproduced it first):
  - A transient network error no longer ends a run. `configure-powerbi.ps1` stopped on "The SSL connection could not be established, see inner exception." during the history model or report import, because the REST helpers retried only HTTP 429. Now the Fabric and Power BI helpers and `Invoke-DemoRestRequest`, and through them the item imports, their long-running operations, the Fabric admin API's tenant-settings read, and the Hub's Fabric reads, share one policy (`Invoke-DemoTransientRetry`). A network error (a failed TLS handshake, a reset connection, a DNS failure, a timeout) and HTTP 408, 429, 502, 503, and 504 get up to 4 attempts, 5, 15, and 30 seconds apart, or after what Retry-After says, with one line per retry, such as "A network error reached api.powerbi.com; retrying in 5 s (attempt 2 of 4); no action needed.".
  - A request that starts or adds something goes again only when it never reached the service, or on HTTP 429: a notebook run, a refresh, Microsoft Graph's group writes, and the activity control's creates. A create sent again that finds its name taken resolves the item, the lakehouse, or the workspace by name. The token handling and the allowed hosts are unchanged.
  - Errors name their innermost cause, redacted, instead of "see inner exception".
  - A track step that fails says so on the console at once: `The history report step failed: <why>. The other tracks continue; the run ends NOT DONE, and ./scripts/deploy.ps1 resumes it.`
  - When a network error stopped a step, **NOT DONE**'s next step is to run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again, which resumes, not to run the step's script yourself.
  - A resume never skips a step that failed. A fresh run's failed Fabric step had kept the record of the **DONE** run before it (`DbHubTrackFabric`), so the next plain `deploy.ps1` resumed past it and ended **DONE** without running it. Now a run that starts over clears every track's record once preflight passes, and any other run lowers the records of the tracks it runs again, `-Sequential` included. The status page shows the step a track resumes at as "Didn't finish in the previous run; running it again." `-Resume` and `-FromStage` plan as before.
  - After the agent's restart, a read of `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` that comes back **Fail** (uploads that fail with HTTP 403, for example) is returned as it is, with the restart in its evidence, so the T1 track stops on its own cause. "Superseded" is only for a read that's still a wait, and never claims uploads OK. `Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin` passes a running plugin only when its uploads are OK, and returns a running plugin whose uploads fail as a **Warning**, with the identity and outbound HTTPS fix and no restart.
- Phase 28, from a plain `./scripts/deploy.ps1` on an existing environment in a later month than its first deployment (2026-11-02: **NOT DONE** at the template, about 4 minutes in) and the independent reviews of c7c86ec and 76d10b2 (each with Pester tests that reproduced it first):
  - A redeploy keeps the budget's start date. Azure refused the template with "Start date of budgets cannot be updated. Please delete and create a new budget.": the budget's start date was read through PowerShell 7's `ConvertFrom-Json`, which turns ISO 8601 text into a date, so the `yyyy-MM-dd` match never held and the template sent the first day of the current month; in the budget's own month that's the same date, which hid it. Now `az rest` returns the start date as text (`--query`, `--output tsv`), read as UTC whatever the time zone or culture, for the deployment, `-Sequential`, GitHub Actions, and preflight's what-if alike. No budget yet stays quiet; a budget that can't be read gets one **Warning** that a later month's redeploy may fail at the budget.
  - A failed template names its cause: `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE` reads the deployment's failed operations (`az deployment operation sub list`, then `group list` for nested deployments, at most 4 levels and 5 errors) and gives the innermost errors, redacted, such as "The template create failed: Microsoft.Consumption/budgets budget-dbhub-demo: 400: Start date of budgets cannot be updated. ...", instead of "At least one resource deployment operation failed". For that error, its fix is to delete the budget at the resource group's scope and run `./scripts/deploy.ps1` again, which creates it anew. T3's free-offer retry and T4's Burstable fallback still read the error as before.
  - A lasting outage costs a bounded number of requests: the policy inventory stops reading definitions after the first one a network error stopped and counts the rest as unreadable, with one **Warning**; the wait for a new workspace's capacity counts elapsed time, its reads included, and reads on through a short outage, and only a window of 5 minutes with no read that came back is a `FABRIC.RESOLVE.WORKSPACE` (or `.CAPACITY`) **Fail**, to run again; the notebook run's wait counts its reads' time too.
  - `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` tells a read that failed from one that found nothing: "Azure couldn't be read for sqlvm01's monitoring: ...", with the redacted error and "Run ./scripts/deploy.ps1 again: it resumes at T1's Monitoring step", instead of "sqlvm01 has no SqlIaasExtension" or no identity. A Microsoft.Sql registration that can't be read stays the **Warning** an unregistered one gets.
  - The network error's advice is true for a request sent only once too: "A network error stopped it (requests that are safe to repeat were retried)."
- Phase 28: the template's subscription-scope deployment record is `<prefix>-<environment>-main`, which is `dbhub-demo-main` with the defaults, as before. It was `dbhub-<environment>-main` whatever the prefix, so an environment with its own prefix and the same environment name, such as a throwaway proof, would have replaced and read another environment's record, and deleted it at its destroy; the residual check now looks for `<prefix>-<environment>-*`.
- Phase 28 follow-up, from destroying a proof environment with another prefix next to `rg-dbhub-demo` (2026-11-02; the destroy deleted only its own resources, but `DESTROY.RESIDUAL` failed) and the coordinator's review (each with Pester tests that reproduced it first):
  - The residual check counts only this environment: its resource group by the exact name `rg-<prefix>-<environment>` and the manifest's `deploymentId` tag, never the `solution` and `environment` tags alone, and never a resource's name alone. It had listed 24 resources and groups of the other environment with the same environment name (its group, VMs, PostgreSQL server, shutdown schedules, and Arc machine `arcsql01`) as residue. Without a manifest, `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.TAGGED` looks for the group by name only and is a **Warning**. `validate.ps1 -Area Teardown` matches the `deploymentId` only, too, and is a **Warning** after a destroy without `-DeploymentId`.
  - What Resource Graph still lists once the group is gone is confirmed with Azure Resource Manager, at most 25 reads for the whole check (Resources - Get By Id, with the pinned API version or the provider's newest stable one). A row that Resource Manager answers with 404 `ResourceNotFound`, `ResourceGroupNotFound`, or `ParentResourceNotFound` is a stale index record, reported with the other records (`TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.RECORDS`), not residue: ten `sqlserverinstances/databases` rows of the deleted Arc SQL Server instances had stayed listed for more than 30 minutes and failed `GROUPRESOURCES` and `ARC`. A row it couldn't confirm within the bound is a **Warning**, never a **Fail**.
  - A role assignment for a deleted identity of the environment outside the demo's resource groups, which something else made (here a tenant policy with a `DeployIfNotExists` effect, on a storage account in the tenant's governance resource group), is a **Warning** that names its scope and says that an Owner or User Access Administrator can remove the orphaned assignment ("Identity not found"). The demo gives its managed identities role assignments only at or under its group, with `assignRoles`; those, and any for its identities in `rg-<prefix>-control` or `rg-<prefix>-golden`, are still a **Fail**. Nothing deletes them.
  - The on-demand control's runbooks delete the finished `DbHubControl-*` Run Commands on a running demo VM that a run or reset doesn't use, not only the earlier ones on the VMs it uses, so a VM no later run used no longer keeps them: one from 2026-10-27 was still on `sqlvm01`. The stop path still never uses Run Command ([ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md)); the custom role already had `Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/runCommands/delete`. Run `./scripts/setup-activity-control.ps1 -SkipFabric` to publish the runbooks.
  - The resource group's tag values round-trip unchanged: `Get-DemoResourceGroupTag` reads them as Azure holds them (`System.Text.Json`), where PowerShell 7's `ConvertFrom-Json` had turned ISO 8601 text into dates that the template then wrote back as `MM/dd/yyyy HH:mm:ss`, a person's own tags included. Every reader still accepts both forms. Tested in `de-DE` and a non-UTC time zone.
  - `ConvertTo-DemoDeploymentOutput` returns no outputs for an empty `outputs` object instead of throwing under strict mode.
- Phase 28 follow-up, from the independent review of 7fb941e (each code fix with Pester tests that reproduced it first):
  - The manifest guard checks the subscription too. The default manifest path, `out/destroy-manifest-<environment>.json`, is shared by every prefix and subscription with that environment name, so another subscription's destroy of `rg-dbhub-demo` could leave its manifest there, and `destroy.ps1 -ResidueOnly` passed `DESTROY.MANIFEST` and checked that environment's `deploymentId` and identities. Now a manifest whose `SubscriptionId` isn't the configured one (compared without regard to case) is never used: `-ResidueOnly` reports `DESTROY.MANIFEST` **Fail**, naming both subscriptions, and checks nothing, and a destroy that finds one replaces it with a **Warning** in its log. A manifest without `SubscriptionId` is still judged by its resource group.
  - `start-demo.ps1` deletes a `DbHubControl-*` Run Command only when its execution state is terminal, or empty or Unknown more than an hour after the time in its name, so a control run that overlaps the start keeps the Run Command it has just created; `status.ps1` doesn't count such a one on a running VM.
  - The README says that the default path (`assignRoles` false) creates no role assignments, rather than that the demo creates none.
- Phase 28 follow-up, from the independent review of pull request 11 (each with a Pester test that fails on its first commit):
  - Preflight keeps its own cost checks, a monthly-ceiling **Fail** included, when the General Purpose fallback's extra estimate fails (for example on HTTP 429 after the retries): `PREFLIGHT.T4.FALLBACK` is then a **Warning** with `skipReason` `Price`, and `deploy.ps1` still stops at the hard ceiling. Before, the failure replaced them with one `PREFLIGHT.COST.SESSION` **Warning**, and the run went past the ceiling.
  - **DONE** and **NOT DONE** say "T4 created as General Purpose" only with evidence that the server exists: this run's template, T4's own checks, the T4 track's record, or a Servers - Get. When the fallback chose `Standard_D2ds_v5` and its creation then failed for another reason (a quota, a policy denial, a transient error), the banner says T4 isn't created yet, and why; `status.ps1` says `deploy.ps1` creates it. And a record that doesn't match an existing server is cleared rather than set to the server's size, so a `Standard_D2ds_v5` server created through `t4SkuName`, later removed, isn't said to come from a Burstable refusal.
  - After Azure rejects the T3 free offer, the General Purpose fallback's cost check runs again without the free offer's allowance before T4 is created.
  - `stop-demo.ps1` and `status.ps1` say the daily safety stop stops a restarted T4 only when its schedule is in place (read as `CONTROL.SAFETYSTOP` reads it), not whenever `control.enabled` is true: setup can end with "couldn't be scheduled". Their evidence has `safetyStop` (`Scheduled`, `NotScheduled`, `Unknown`, or `Off`).
- Phase 28 follow-up, from the independent review of 64c28b2 after pull request 11 merged: T4's server depends only on the network, so a template attempt that fails because of another resource can still have created it (each with Pester tests that fail on 64c28b2):
  - **NOT DONE** no longer says T4 "isn't created yet" when it was. A `DEPLOY.TEMPLATE` **Fail** while the fallback was creating `Standard_D2ds_v5`, or a `T4.SERVER` **Fail** (which a read that failed gives too), no longer counts as proof that the server is missing: the ending's Servers - Get decides, so a General Purpose server that runs and bills is "T4 created as General Purpose Standard_D2ds_v5", with its hourly price.
  - A server that a failed attempt created counts as existing: `deploy.ps1` reads T4 again after each failed attempt while its fallback is choosing, as it does the VMs, and a server it finds keeps its own size and record, isn't priced again, and is never recorded `None` or `Refused`. After Azure rejected the T3 free offer in the attempt that created it, pull request 11's repeated cost check could turn T4 off and record `None` for a server that billed; `DEPLOY.T4.SKU` now says its check counted the free offer's allowance. Nor does the fallback move such a server on to another size, a resize, when Azure answers its update with `SkuNotAvailable`.
  - `status.ps1` (`STATUS.T4`) and `validate.ps1` (`T4.SERVER`) report a T4 server that exists although the record says T4 is off (`None` or `Refused`) as a **Warning** that it bills, with what keeps or removes it, instead of hiding it.
- Phase 28 follow-up, from a tester's report (2026-11-03): the README's guided stop covered a blank **Use Azure Login** window only when Internet Explorer's dialog came. It now also covers a window that stays blank with no dialog (IE Enhanced Security Configuration was on when setup started): cancel setup, set it to **Off** for **Administrators** in **Server Manager**, start setup again, and redo the **Edition** and **Azure Extension for SQL Server** pages. It also says the dialog can open behind setup's window, and that "Requires all input values for AZURESERVICEPRINCIPAL" only means the sign-in hasn't happened. `docs/troubleshooting.md` already had this.
- Phase 28 follow-up, from the owner's environment (live, 2026-11-03): every start from the Fabric control brings every target back ([ADR 0020](docs/adr/0020-on-demand-activity-control.md#amendments-2026-11-03-every-start-brings-every-target-back), [ADR 0018](docs/adr/0018-fast-redeploy-parallel-tracks-golden-snapshot.md#amendments-2026-11-03-the-capture-returns-its-source-to-service)):
  - **T2 was missing for six days after a golden capture:** the capture left its source air-gapped until the next `start-demo.ps1` or `deploy.ps1`, and the control never released it. `capture-t2-golden.ps1` now returns its source to service once Prepare has run, whatever happens next (`GOLDEN.CAPTURE.SOURCE`: the host started, the guest released as a source only, `arcsql01` Connected after the air gap, the host back in its power state), and the control's T2 relay releases a source that's still air-gapped with the golden script's own Release, starts an off guest, and never starts a copy that isn't reset.
  - **T1's `profile set` failed with SQL error 2 two minutes before SQL Server accepted connections:** before a command that starts activity, the VM-side control waits until its target accepts connections (10 minutes; 20 for T2), retrying the documented transient errors of SQL Server, Azure SQL Database, and PostgreSQL with backoff; each request carries `readySeconds`, and each Run Command's timeout covers the wait.
  - **A slow T4 held back every target for up to 15 minutes:** each VM's activities go out as soon as it runs, and T4's on its own Run Command once T4 is Ready (at most 20 minutes).
  - **T1 sent no telemetry:** the runbook read the monitoring plugin before the agent reported it and sent no restart. Once `sqlvm01` runs, the job waits for the plugin's first status (5 minutes), leaves a running one alone, and restarts the SQL IaaS Agent service otherwise, then checks it again; the new runbook `DbHub-Monitor` does the same every 30 minutes of a run, through one-time schedules that go with the auto-stop.
  - **Nothing said so:** `status.ps1` and `validate.ps1 -Area T2` report `T2.GUEST.STATE` (the guest air-gapped, off, cut off from its switch, or not starting with the host, each with the exact command), `T2.ARC.CONNECTED` fails after 10 minutes Disconnected while the host runs (from the later of the last status change and the host's start; it was 30), and `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` gives the exact restart for a stopped plugin. `GOLDEN.TEST.SOURCE` counts the source's status only while its host stays deallocated for the whole test.
  - **Review fixes (2026-11-04):** a T2 start between a capture's Prepare and its snapshot released the source, so the snapshot could keep a guest that wasn't air-gapped. Prepare now marks the capture as in progress (for 6 hours at most), and only the capture's own Release ends it; the relay leaves the guest alone meanwhile, changes the guest only under the bootstrap's lock on what it reads again, and takes a released record at its word only on the source or a reset copy; `capture-t2-golden.ps1` doesn't start during an on-demand run (`GOLDEN.CAPTURE.CONTROL`), and `T2.GUEST.STATE` says a capture is in progress. `start-demo.ps1`'s three attempts for T1 and T2 share one readiness budget (4 minutes) instead of waiting 10 or 20 minutes each. A guest without SQL Server is "T2 isn't set up yet" (Not selected). T4's readiness wait retries only the transient PostgreSQL codes, not 08P01.
  - **Re-review fixes (2026-11-04):** the golden Release itself now refuses while a capture marker names its host, unless it ends the capture or `start-demo.ps1` and `deploy.ps1` found the snapshot the marker names (then it ends the marker), so an older untagged snapshot can't let them reconnect the guest mid-capture, and a restore that failed early can't leave a stale marker behind a successful release; `T2.GOLDEN.SOURCE` reports a capture in progress as a **Warning**. The marker's time is written in the invariant culture (Windows PowerShell on fi-FI wrote `09.44.28`), and a marker that can't be read holds every release but the capture's own back (fail closed).
  - **With `business-day` by default (PR 16, 2026-11-04):** its profile Run Commands (the interim `baseline` and the switch) ask the VM side for no readiness wait of their own (`readySeconds` 0), so their 15-second retries are the only wait; `start-demo.ps1` gives a T1 or T2 that stayed down through that 10-minute wait one profile attempt without another wait, and doesn't try T2 again while the relay holds its guest for a golden capture. `start-demo.ps1` and `deploy.ps1` release a golden source as soon as `archost01` runs, before its interim, and report `START.`/`DEPLOY.INTERIM_T2` as **Not selected**, sending and waiting for nothing, when the release leaves the guest off; the VM stops trying T2 at once when its relay holds the guest or finds it not running, which is **Not selected** with the reason, also for the switch and `stop-demo.ps1`'s off. When T1 or T2 never answers, a start that booted the VMs waits about 15 minutes in all for it (R634).
  - **Rollout:** `./scripts/deploy.ps1` (it sends the changed bundle to both VMs), then `./scripts/setup-activity-control.ps1 -SkipFabric` (it publishes the three runbooks; `CONTROL.SETUP` says `DbHub-Monitor` is missing until then). Run `deploy.ps1` before the next capture: the capture's marker needs the updated relay on `archost01`.
- Phase 26d (live, 2026-10-28): the golden recapture's proof failed `GOLDEN.TEST.SOURCE` on two Resource Health events about the source's Arc machine (its availability while its host was deallocated), although the machine was untouched. The check now counts only Administrative Activity Log entries as operations, keeps every entry with its category in the evidence (`operations`, `otherEvents`), and fails when a source that was Disconnected before the test has a different last status change after it, so a short Connected period still shows.
- The golden recapture's host compaction (`Invoke-DemoHostCompact`) reported a guest disk's file name with `[System.IO.Path]::GetFileName`, which doesn't split a Windows path on Linux, so its unit test failed on the Linux CI runner. It now splits on both separators.
- Phase 6: the bootstrap loader extracted `bundle-<sha12>.zip.partial` with `Expand-Archive`, which Windows PowerShell 5.1 rejects for any extension but `.zip`, so no VM would have bootstrapped. It now extracts a renamed `.zip` with `ZipFile.ExtractToDirectory`, and a test runs it in Windows PowerShell 5.1.
- Phase 6: generated VM passwords no longer use `%`, `!`, or `^`, which broke or could break `az.cmd` command lines on Windows.
- Phase 7: Phase 5's private `Get-DemoArmResource` silently replaced Phase 7's function of the same name; Phase 7's is now `Get-DemoArcArmResource`, and a test fails on duplicate function names.
- Phase 7: `configure-arc.ps1` skipped configuration when the T2 host was busy after the setup wizard; an Arc machine that's already Connected now proceeds.
- Phase 7: the setup-wizard instructions said not to enter credentials, but pay-as-you-go needs an Azure Login on the Azure Extension for SQL Server page; they now say to use Azure Login and never a service principal secret.
- Phase 7: a managed Run Command created again soon after it was deleted could return the previous run's result without running; `Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand` now detects it by start time, retries once, and otherwise fails closed.
- Phase 7: `Invoke-DemoVmRunCommand` refuses a value that is a .NET type name, the result of converting a SecureString or credential with `[string]`.
- Phase 13: the secret check before a chunk is sent no longer applies the text redaction patterns to binary content, such as compiled assemblies, which can legitimately contain them. It still refuses any registered secret in UTF-8 or UTF-16.
