- Phase 13: the T2 host computes the guest's paths as strings, so `Join-Path` no longer resolves the guest's `C:` drive on the host or on Linux CI ("Cannot find drive"), and stopping the runner matches its processes with either path separator.
- Phase 13: the one-time host password is refused before any Run Command when it's shorter than 12 characters or reads like a type name: a `SecureString` bound to a `[string]` parameter becomes the literal `System.Security.SecureString`. Tests check that the value sent matches Run Command's parameter pattern and is never that literal.
- Phase 12: `Import-DemoFabricNotebook` reports a paused workspace capacity (HTTP 404 `CapacityNotActive`) as **Manual action required** with the resume steps, instead of **Fail** with a hint to check `fabric.workspaceId`.
- Phase 13 guard update: with another tenant's default subscription in the shared Azure CLI profile, the Resource Graph checks (Estate, T3 readiness) were refused with `AccessDenied`, P6 and T1's group membership went to the other tenant's Microsoft Graph, and the Key Vault opt-in would have granted access to the other tenant's object ID. All of them now use the configured subscription's sign-in.
- Phase 14: `T1.SQLVM.MONITORING` reported a Fail on extension version `''` while `sqlvm01` was deallocated, because a deallocated VM's instance view is empty. It now checks what it can read and reports a Warning to start the VM.
- Phase 14: a caller's `-WhatIf` no longer reaches the Azure CLI wrapper, where it skipped the stderr redirection and the deletion of a temporary file.
- Phase 14: the secret scan skipped UTF-16 files as binary, so a token in a Windows PowerShell 5.1 log or another UTF-16 log went unseen; it now decodes UTF-16 and UTF-32 by byte order mark, and UTF-16 without one.
- Phase 14: validation blamed a policy-installed extension update when a Run Command returned a stale result (Phase 7's `Stale`); `INFRA.HEALTH.<vm>` and `T2.GUEST.ISOLATION` now name the stale result.
- Phase 16: `destroy.ps1` left the subscription deployment record `dbhub-<environment>-main` behind, with the template's parameters and outputs, and never ran the residual check that ADR 0002 requires; it now deletes the record and checks.
- Phase 16: a resource group that disappeared during its own delete (`ResourceGroupNotFound`), a deployment record that was already gone (`DeploymentNotFound`), and a Microsoft Graph object that was already gone (`Request_ResourceNotFound`) weren't recognized as gone, so a finished delete could be reported as a failure. `Test-DemoAzNotFound` now recognizes every `...NotFound` code and "could not be found", except a subscription that can't be found (`SubscriptionNotFound`), which means a wrong context or no access, never a deleted resource.
- Phase 16: a `destroy.ps1` run after an interruption that found the resource group gone stopped at "nothing to delete", so the deployment record and the residual check were skipped; it now finishes both, with the manifest recorded while the group existed.
- Phase 16 (live, 2026-10-21): T2's guest couldn't resolve any name, so Arc onboarding failed with `lookup management.azure.com: no such host` (`azcmagent connect` exit code 26). The host's `NatNetwork` step added the gateway address `192.168.200.1`, and two seconds later the `DnsForwarder` step restarted the DNS Server while the new address was still tentative. The DNS Server couldn't bind it (DNS Server events 404, 407, and 408) and answered nothing on the NAT network, while the step's check, which read only the configured listening address, reported it converged. Now `NatNetwork` waits until the address is usable (`AddressState` `Preferred`), `DnsForwarder` restarts the DNS Server only then and confirms that it listens on UDP and TCP port 53 (up to three restarts, then the step fails), and the check requires the bound sockets, so a later run repairs a host left without them.
- Phase 16 (live, 2026-10-22): the subscription guard reported a **Warning** because Microsoft Graph refused the Azure CLI's cached token with a Continuous Access Evaluation claims challenge (HTTP 401, `insufficient_claims`, `TokenCreatedWithOutdatedPolicies`), and the CLI kept handing back the revoked token. `Invoke-DemoRestRequest` now drops a token refused with a claims challenge, gets one fresh token (`Get-DemoAccessToken -Refresh` asks for Microsoft Graph's other audience spelling, which the CLI caches separately, with the same tenant and caller checks), and sends the request once more. Only then is the result a Warning, which names the request, the status, and the `WWW-Authenticate` header with its claims value redacted, and says when the CLI returned the same token (compared by SHA-256 hash). The guard's evidence records it as `graphChallenge`.
- Phase 16 (live, 2026-10-22): the budget at the resource group's scope outlived the group, so every destroy would end with `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.BUDGET` **Fail**; `destroy.ps1` now deletes it by its URL, with or without the group (`DESTROY.BUDGET`). The deployment-stack trial also deletes its subscription stack's deployment records, which outlive the stack, and counts them in `TEARDOWN.STACK.CLEANUP`.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): `destroy.ps1`'s explicit Arc deletes never ran ("Deleted 0 of 1"): the collection came back as one nested array, so the loop found no resource ID, and the resource group's delete removed the Arc resources instead. The deletes now see every item, and a test uses the collection as the real function returns it.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): the destroy of `rg-dbhub-demo` left the same budget behind (R273, `TEARDOWN.RESIDUAL.BUDGET` **Fail**), as Phase 16 found in `rg-dbhub-test`; Phase 16's `DESTROY.BUDGET` fixes both. The residual check now skips `rg-<prefix>-golden`, whose snapshots name their source group by design.
- Phase 20: `infra/modules/demo.bicep` passed the Arc onboarding identity's name as `id---arc-onboard` (empty prefix and environment), and a template with an empty name failed validation, because Azure Resource Manager evaluates both branches of `if()`; the name is now `id-<prefix>-<environment>-arc-onboard`.
- Phase 20: `t2Source` wasn't in `config/environment.schema.json`, which forbids unknown properties, so a configuration that set it (`golden` or `build`) failed validation; only the default `auto` worked.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): `deploy.ps1`'s Fabric track stopped at once. A script that module code runs with the call operator runs in the module's session state, so `configure-fabric.ps1`'s `Get-DemoConfig` couldn't read `$script:RepoRoot`. The Fabric scripts now run in a separate session state, as an operator runs them (`Invoke-DemoRepositoryScript`).
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): the golden Release read the restored guest's Arc agent right after its cold boot, when `azcmagent show -j` prints no JSON yet, and took that for "no identity", so it skipped the local reset. It now waits up to 5 minutes for the agent's state and stops with the guest still air-gapped when it can't tell; `DEPLOY.T2.RESTORE` records the agent's state and says what the reset did.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): `deploy.ps1`'s T3 readiness gate left the extended-property check as a manual step, although T1's track had read the property; it now reuses `T3.PROPERTY`, or reads it from T1.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): after the parallel deploy, T1's unified inventory still listed `sqlvm01_MSAS16_MSSQLSERVER` and `sqlvm01_SSIS_2022` for 46 to 72 minutes after the `AssociatedServices` step had uninstalled them, because nothing restarted the SQL IaaS Agent (the sequential path's second template deployment did); they went on their own about an hour after the agent had started, where a restart took about 6 minutes on 2026-10-21. The step now restarts it once the features are gone, and `deploy.ps1`'s validation fails (`T1.SQLVM.ASSOCIATEDSERVICES`) when they're still listed after up to 10 minutes.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): `validate.ps1` failed the bootstrap loader check after a parallel deploy, which removes the loader's Run Command; it now passes when `health.json` names the recorded bundle.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): two checkouts of the same workload-runner source built two different runners, so a deploy from the other checkout reinstalled the runner on both VMs, and the assembly carried the build's path, with the operator's user folder. Three things varied: the informational version carried the Git commit (`0.1.0+<sha>`), the regular-expression generator's file-local type names derive from the checkout's path, and the assembly records the program database's checksum, whose Source Link data holds the path and the commit. `workload-generator/Directory.Build.props` now leaves the commit out (`IncludeSourceRevisionInInformationalVersion` false) and maps the checkout's path to `/_/` (`PathMap`), and `Publish-DemoWorkloadRunner` builds no program database (`-p:DebugType=none`, now part of the zip's input hash). Two checkouts now build the same runner, byte for byte.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): on a second deploy, `DEPLOY.T2.RESTORE` said the copy's Arc identity was reset, with an empty SQL Server edition, and that the guest's password was rotated, although an earlier run had released the copy and the Release only reconnected it. It now says the copy was released earlier.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): the repeat start reported `sqlvm01` missing (`START.VM_SQLVM01` **Fail**, "wasn't found") although it was starting: right after both VMs' start requests, its instance view carried no power state, and an empty power state counted as a missing VM. `Invoke-DemoVmPowerAction` now waits while the power state is empty and reports a VM missing only when Azure says it doesn't exist.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): with both VMs booting at once, T1's activity profile wasn't set on a repeat start (`START.PROFILE_T1` **Warning**): T1's SQL Server didn't accept connections yet, and only T2 was retried. T1 now retries too, every `-T2ProfileRetrySeconds`, up to three attempts.
- Phase 20 (live, 2026-10-22): after a deallocation and start, T1's monitoring plugin stayed NotRunning (the SQL IaaS Agent extension reported `DatabaseMonitorArcPlugin {"State":"NotRunning"}` for more than 90 minutes), so T1 sent no Database Hub telemetry and the rich incident program's T1 windows never reached the history. Restarting the agent's service (`SQLIaaSExtension`) brought it to Running with uploads OK within a minute. `start-demo.ps1` now checks the plugin once `sqlvm01` runs and restarts the service only when it isn't running (`START.T1.MONITORING`, `Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin`).
- Phase 22 (live, 2026-10-23): the golden Release took an air-gapped agent's answer without a resource (`status: failed`) for "no identity", skipped the reset, and reconnected the guest; the onboarding then found the captured identity and reset it, which was safe only because the destroy had deleted the captured machine. The Release now resets a copy unless its agent names another identity (`Get-GoldenResetDecision`), and `DEPLOY.T2.RESTORE` says when it reset an unknown identity; proven in the second timed run (the onboarding found no local identity).
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): a redeploy failed with `OperationNotAllowed` ("Managed disk storage account type change through Virtual Machine 'sqlvm01' is not allowed"): the subscription's automation had switched both VMs' OS disks to Standard HDD while they were deallocated (2026-10-24), and the template asked for the profile's Standard SSD. For an existing VM, `deploy.ps1` now passes each drifted disk's current type to the template (`diskSkuOverrides`, OS and data disks on both VMs) and carries on with the `DEPLOY.DISKS.DRIFT` **Warning**; it never changes a disk back (R440).
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): `stop-demo.ps1` set T4's profile even when T4 had never been created, and a runner from before Phase 24 refused it (`STOP.PROFILE_T4` **Warning**). A T4 that Azure says doesn't exist is now left alone (**Not selected**); one whose state couldn't be read is still stopped.
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): `demo-control.ps1` refused `-Target T4`, which `demo-control.yml` offers and `Invoke-DemoControl` accepts; a test checks that the script and the module take every target the workflow offers.
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): the first deploy with T4 failed its T4 track at `Telemetry`: `METRICS.T4` built the Metrics - List query string with encoded characters, and Invoke-DemoAz refuses an argument with '%', which cmd.exe can't pass to az.cmd. The query parameters now go in a file (`az rest --url-parameters @<path>`), and the T4 tests check every Azure CLI argument the way Invoke-DemoAz does.
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): `status.ps1` couldn't say when a stopped T4 starts on its own: with `--start-time` alone, `az monitor activity-log list` ends the range 6 hours later (`--offset`), so the eight-day look-back for T4's stop found nothing. `Get-DemoT4StoppedUtc` now passes `--end-time`.
- Phase 24 (live, 2026-10-26): `STATUS.DISKS` passed while both VMs were deallocated, because a deallocated VM's model has no storage type for its disks; `Get-DemoVmDiskDrift` now reads the disk itself then.
- Phase 25b (Phase 25a's finding F4): a profile host lane could end without a trace while the host kept running: an exception outside its profile read's catch filter ended the target's loop, which `Task.WhenAll` observed only at shutdown, and some awaits had no bound. On 2026-10-23 `sqlvm01` stopped serving T1 after 20:06:08Z while T3's lane went on. Each lane now survives any failure of one poll, bounds every await (the profile read at 150 seconds, the reset before a profile at 780), starts and cancels runs off its poll, and a supervisor replaces a lane that ended or hasn't finished a poll for 5 minutes, with a backoff; logging can no longer end a lane or a run ([ADR 0010](docs/adr/0010-workload-generator-technology.md#phase-25b-the-profile-hosts-lanes-and-supervisor-and-the-bounds-phase-25a-met-2026-10-26)).
- Phase 25b (finding F1): after a profile was set again, its new baseline started while the old one was still ending, was refused (`demo.usp_run_begin` error 50108), and never ran; an incident lost its baseline the same way. The host now waits for a run of the same scenario that's still ending, and tries an activity refused with 50108, 50109, or a failed connection again every 30 seconds, at most 40 times within its window; the run's result carries the refusal's reason and SQL error number. Five of the host log's seven 50108 refusals on 2026-10-23 came right after a task restart, from runs the killed host had left Running: before a lane's first start and after each 50108, the host now ends this machine's profile runs that started before it and have no live session.
- Phase 25b: runs that the profile host stopped, or that reached their deadline, recorded "No sessions ran." when their sessions took longer to end than the runner waits; their outcome now counts every session that ran (`(16 still ending)`) and says when its caller stopped it.
- Phase 25b (finding 3): the live-activity notebook and the Real-Time Dashboard read `AvgCPUPercent`, which is 0 for SQL Server in this preview, so T1's and T2's CPU never showed and no CPU window was found on them (Phase 25a: `SQLProcessCPUPercent` reached 99 and 97). T1 and T2 now read `SQLProcessCPUPercent`; T3 keeps `AvgCPUPercent`. The CPU prompts and the `capacity-max` signals say so.
- Phase 25b: the Insights app's deploy-time check replayed T3 from 21:00 the evening before the newest telemetry day to its midnight, so `FABRIC.APP.QUERIES` warned that `replay-T3` and `spotlight-T3` had no rows (Phase 25a, 2026-10-26). `history-meta` now also returns T3's newest incident window and newest bin, and the check replays that window (15 minutes on each side, 3 hours at most), else the 3 hours up to that bin, else the whole newest day; the query IDs are unchanged.
- Phase 25b (Phase 24's live run, 2026-10-26, R442): T1's monitoring plugin didn't come back after a start. `start-demo.ps1` restarted the SQL IaaS Agent service about a minute after boot, while the agent reported no plugin status yet, and the plugin then stayed NotRunning, so `TELEMETRY.T1` failed; the same restart later brought it to Running and OK in 144 seconds. `START.T1.MONITORING` (`Restore-DemoSqlVmMonitoringPlugin -SettleMinutes`) now waits up to 5 minutes for the plugin's status before it decides, restarts the service only when the plugin isn't Running or still has no status, and checks for Running and OK with a bounded number of polls; a restart that doesn't help stays a **Warning**.
- Phase 25b (pre-existing): `STATUS.PROFILE_<target>` said the activity profile couldn't be read, because `Invoke-DemoWorkloadCommand` read `--json` records only from a JSON array and `profile get --json` writes one object. `ConvertFrom-DemoRunnerRecord` now takes one object as one record and an array's items as records, and a bracket in text around the document can't hide it; `status.ps1` reads the profile from that record.
- Phase 25d (live, 2026-10-27): the runner's `setup` failed on every target ("3 scenario procedure(s) are missing"), so `deploy.ps1` from `main` failed T1's and T2's WorkloadRunner step. It looked for `demo.usp_scenario_<id>` for each row of `demo.fn_scenario_catalog()`, and `blocking-chain`, `memory-grant-pressure`, and `log-flush-waits` name other entry procedures. `setup` now checks the procedure each scenario that runs on the target names in its `scenario.json`, and names any that's missing.
- Phase 25d (live): the T2 track of `deploy.ps1` passed while `archost01`'s health said Failed (its last step, the T2 runner's install, had failed). Like T1, the T2 step now waits for the host's Ready or Failed before it sets T2's profile (`DEPLOY.T2.READY`; `T2ReadyMinutes`, 30 by default), and a failed host stops the track.
- Phase 25d (live): after `deploy.ps1`'s template run, T1's monitoring plugin stayed NotRunning and the Monitoring step only waited, so T1 sent no telemetry. When the wait ends with the extension Succeeded, the flag set, and the plugin not Running, both deployment paths now restart the SQL IaaS Agent service, as `start-demo.ps1` does, and report `DEPLOY.T1.MONITORING`.
- Phase 25d (live): `start-demo.ps1` said the VMs and T4 were "already running" when it had started them itself; the results now say the start requested them.
- Phase 25d (live): the on-demand activity control's role and assignment lookups, `-Remove`'s assignment list, and `start-demo.ps1`'s stop-job read put a percent-encoded `$filter` on the `az rest` command line, which cmd.exe can't pass to az.cmd: setup reported `CONTROL.ROLE.A1`-`A3` as not checked, and `-GrantRole` would have failed. The query parameters now go in a file (`--url-parameters @<path>`), and the unit tests' fake Azure refuses such arguments as the real call does.
- Phase 25d: the control notebook couldn't be run through the Fabric job API or a pipeline: `ACTIVITIES` and `RESET_TARGETS` were lists only, and a job parameter is a string, a number, or a bool; and with `STOP_NOW` and `CONFIRM`, **Run all** sent a run request before the stop. Both lists also take JSON text, and with Stop now or Reset set, the run cell sends nothing.
- Phase 25d (live): `capacity-max` on T2 ended **Failed** because 2 of its 12 sessions couldn't open their connection: SqlClient runs only as many non-pooled opens at once as there are processors (2 in T2's guest), and under the scenario's load the queued opens waited past their 30-second timeout ("too many simultaneous non-pooled connection attempts"). The runner now retries that timeout like a transient error, within the same retry window, since nothing was opened.
- Phase 25d (live): T4's profile lane didn't try a refused baseline again: after the profile host restarted (`deploy.ps1`), T4's baseline was refused because the earlier host's run was still Running (SQLSTATE CC002), and later, while T1's `capacity-max` saturated `sqlvm01`, because T4 couldn't be reached in time; each time T4 had no baseline until its next 30-minute slot. The PostgreSQL runner now gives its refusals the T-SQL runner's reasons (`running-run` and `run-limit` for CC002, `connect`, `request`, `sql`), so the host retries them every 30 seconds within the activity's window, as it does on T1 to T3. A T-SQL control connection that timed out behind other opens is now a `connect` refusal too. T4 still has no recovery of runs an earlier host left Running (follow-up), so its retries wait for that run's deadline.
- Phase 25d (live): the control's runbooks couldn't start or stop T4. Without a body, Windows PowerShell 5.1 sends a POST as `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, which the PostgreSQL resource provider refuses (HTTP 415 `UnsupportedMediaType`; the VMs' provider accepts it), so T4's start failed, and Stop now, the auto-stop, and the daily T4 safety stop would have left a running T4 running. A POST without a body now sends an empty JSON object as `application/json`. And when T4's start fails, or T4 is still stopping, the run skips T4 at once instead of waiting 15 minutes for it, which had delayed the other targets' activity until just before the run's auto-stop.
- Phase 25d (live): running `setup-activity-control.ps1` again unlinked the daily T4 safety stop. Azure Automation reports the schedule's zone in its IANA form (`America/New_York` for `Eastern Standard Time`), so setup didn't recognize its own schedule, replaced it, and then couldn't link it to `DbHub-Stop` again: the link's fixed ID was refused as already existing (`CONTROL.SAFETYSTOP` **Warning**). Setup now compares the zones' rules, and gives a new link a new ID.
- Phase 26g: a first deploy into a new Fabric workspace failed the history model's refresh (`FABRIC.SEMANTICMODEL.REFRESH`, 0xC14700DF): Direct Lake didn't see the tables the live-activity notebook had just created for about 20 minutes. `configure-powerbi.ps1` now refreshes again every 2 minutes for up to 30 minutes while that error lasts (`-RefreshRetryMinutes`; `Invoke-DemoSemanticModelRefresh -TableRetryMinutes`), and any other failure is reported at once. `FABRIC.APP.CONNECTOR` reports an empty table's count as 0 instead of a blank.
- The LSA secret Azure's provisioning leaves (live, 2026-11-06; user-directed): every deploy reported `T2.ARC.ONBOARDING` as a **Warning** that "Windows automatic sign-in was left armed on archost01". It wasn't: Azure's Windows provisioning leaves an LSA secret named `DefaultPassword` on every VM the demo creates, with automatic sign-in off, and the sign-in data sweep counted that secret alone as armed. The sweep now counts it apart, its cleanup deletes only that secret, and the result keeps its **Pass** with a note. Automatic sign-in that's really armed is reported as before (a **Warning** after the cleanup, a **Fail** before it), and a scan without `-Clean` that finds the lone secret is still a **Fail**. `sqlvm01` kept the secret, because T1 had no sweep: T1's bootstrap has a new last step, `AutoLogonSecret`, that deletes it with the sweep's automatic sign-in check only (`-AutoLogonOnly`), and `DEPLOY.T1.READY` adds its note ([ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-secrets-handling.md#amendment-the-lsa-secret-azures-provisioning-leaves-2026-11-06)).
- **The nightly safety stop's status and setup misread its links** (live, 2026-11-09; user-directed): Job Schedule - List By Automation Account returns `"parameters": null` for every link, as Learn's own sample answer does, and the module read each link's scope from it. So `status.ps1` said the safety stop wasn't in place (`STATUS.SAFETYSTOP`: "isn't linked to DbHub-Stop -Scope Nightly"; `STATUS.T4`: nothing stops a restarted T4) while all six of its jobs of 2026-11-06 to 08 completed; the cost estimate counted T4's restart for the rest of the month; and every `deploy.ps1` deleted and created both schedules and their links again, which the activity log showed at each of the four runs on 2026-11-06. Each link to `DbHub-Stop` is now read once more by its ID with Job Schedule - Get, which returns its scope (`Get-DemoSafetyStopLink`), so the setup keeps the schedules that are right and everything that reads the safety stop sees it in place; a link that can't be read is a **Warning** in the setup and changes nothing. Nothing else read a link's parameters: the on-demand control's schedules and the runbooks' auto-stop cleanup match links by name ([ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md#amendment-link-scopes-read-one-by-one-and-t4-at-the-t3-check-2026-11-09); [troubleshooting](docs/troubleshooting.md#set-the-safety-stop-up-alone)).
- **`STATUS.SAFETYSTOP` said "It hasn't run yet." after the safety stop's scheduled runs** (live, 2026-11-09, the check of #39): `Get-DemoSafetyStopLastRun` read a job's output only when the job's name was a GUID, and a job that a schedule starts is named `SCH_<guid>_<guid>_<ticks>`, so it read only a job started by hand, never a scheduled run; the cost check's "its last run didn't fail" passed for the same reason without one. It now reads each job's output by its ID (`properties.jobId`), which Job - Get Output answers for as for its name (live), and the name only for a job without an ID, when it's a GUID or that schedule form. A job that ended without its result line counts as the stop or the T3 check by its own `Scope` parameter (Job - Get), and `STATUS.SAFETYSTOP`'s evidence names the jobs it read.

### Security

- The nightly safety stop's role (2026-11-05): `deploy.ps1` creates one custom role, "DbHub Demo Safety Stop" (7 read and stop actions: no start, no Run Command, no data access), and assigns it to the safety stop's own identity at the demo resource group only, when the deployer may: an owner-approved exception to ADR 0008's default of no role assignments. `SECURITY.RBAC.ROLEASSIGNMENTS` accepts exactly that assignment and warns about any other scope; `destroy.ps1` leaves the unassigned definition and names the command that deletes it ([ADR 0024](docs/adr/0024-nightly-safety-stop.md)).
- T2's `JumpstartImage` mode (2026-11-05): the image's published default credential is read during the deploy from Arc Jumpstart's own `ArcServersLogonScript.ps1` at a pinned commit, checked by its SHA-256 (fail closed), and kept only in memory; no password text is in the repository, the configuration, a log, or a test (tests build fakes at run time). The host uses it once, over PowerShell Direct while the guest has no network, to set a random password that it keeps with DPAPI like the other guest passwords, and disables `sa` (with a random password nobody keeps) and every other SQL login. Downloads fail closed on a Content-MD5 or length mismatch ([ADR 0009](docs/adr/0009-secrets-handling.md)).
- **The Insights app's `@azure/msal-node` alert** (2026-11-09; [GHSA-xcx6-jx8p-x3h5](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-xcx6-jx8p-x3h5), high, fixed in 7.1.0): an npm override moves it to 7.2.1 in `fabric/app`, from 5.6.0 (the Rayfin CLI 1.36.2's `^5.5.0`) and 6.0.1 (through `tedious` and `@azure/identity`, now 4.14.1, which declares `^7.1.0` itself). No Rayfin release accepts MSAL Node 7.1, not even the newest prerelease, so `config/versions.json` records the override and why, as it does zod's. MSAL Node 7 removed `responseMode` from `acquireTokenInteractive` but still accepts the `form_post` the CLI asks for, and 7.2 binds the browser's answer to its request's state. `src/test/overrides.test.ts` runs the CLI's own `rayfin login` and `rayfin login status` with it, offline, against a stand-in for Entra ID and the browser (`src/test/rayfin-sign-in-harness.mjs`): the code redeemed alone with PKCE, a forged answer with another request's state refused (5.6.0 took it), and the cached sign-in renewed. `npm audit` reports no high advisory now. A CLI that `npx` installs doesn't get the override, so `deploy.ps1`'s browser sign-in still runs 5.6.0 until a follow-up stops running it through npx ([R540](docs/implementation-report.md#risk-register), R720, R721).
- **No Rayfin CLI through `npx`** (2026-11-09): every Rayfin command, `deploy.ps1`'s browser sign-in and its status read included, now runs the app's stage's CLI, which `npm ci` installs from the lock file, so the `@azure/msal-node` override reaches it, and remediations name `./scripts/configure-fabric-app.ps1 -SignIn` or `./out/fabric-app/node_modules/.bin/rayfin`. With the Azure CLI's tokens, a deploy runs no MSAL sign-in flow at all (R721 closed).
- **.NET 10.0.14, a security release** (2026-11-09; Dependabot #14): the SDK pin moves to 10.0.403 in `config/versions.json`, `workload-generator/global.json`, and the dev container together, because `global.json` rolls forward to a later SDK, never back to an earlier one, and the VMs' runtime pin to 10.0.14, its runtime (`dotnet-runtime-10.0.14-win-x64.zip`, with the SHA-512 and size from Microsoft's release metadata, confirmed by downloading it). A Pester test now checks that `global.json` matches `tools.dotnetSdk`. Install SDK 10.0.403 to build the workload runner; the next deployment rebuilds it and gives the VMs runtime 10.0.14 ([R722](docs/implementation-report.md#risk-register)).
- **The Insights app's glob and minimatch advisories** (2026-11-09; [GHSA-3g3m-2fjr-6pw4](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-3g3m-2fjr-6pw4) in glob, fixed in 14.0.2, and [GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249) in minimatch, fixed in 11.0.1, both moderate): `npm audit` in `fabric/app` reported 14, all in development dependencies, through ESLint, typescript-eslint, and the Rayfin CLI and its `archiver`. No release of those parents declares a fixed version, not even ESLint 10.14.0 or typescript-eslint 8.72.1, so two npm overrides, scoped to each advisory's vulnerable range, move glob 10.5.0 and 13.0.6 to 14.0.3, and minimatch 9.0.9 and 10.2.6 to 11.0.4; `readdir-glob`'s minimatch 5.1.9 is outside the range and stays. `config/versions.json` records them, and the Pester test now accepts a key with a range. `src/test/overrides.test.ts` runs the CLI's ZIP packaging for `rayfin up`, its own glob call, and ESLint's matching with them, offline; the ZIP of the app's built `dist` is identical, entry for entry, to the one before. `npm audit` now reports no vulnerabilities, and `npm audit --omit=dev` none, as before ([R723](docs/implementation-report.md#risk-register), R750).
- Phase 27: the Insights app's Dependabot alerts. Vite 8.3.5, from 8.0.16 (GHSA-9jrq-w75r-8gcw and GHSA-rq7h-c2jc-7f22, and `launch-editor`'s GHSA-r825-w8mm-5pxw, GHSA-587h-7wmj-h5pm, and GHSA-698v-4r3x-3vcv); Vitest 4.1.12, from 4.1.11 (`@vitest/mocker`'s GHSA-qxg6-89mg-rqv8 and GHSA-xp26-3454-mjgp); and an npm override of zod to 4.6.7 (GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5), which `config/versions.json` records in `fabricApp.overrides` and `src/test/overrides.test.ts` tests with Rayfin's own zod code. `@azure/msal-node` (GHSA-xcx6-jx8p-x3h5), a dev dependency of the Rayfin CLI, isn't overridden ([R540](docs/implementation-report.md#risk-register)).
